  <div class="pyq-question">
    At which of the following Harappan civilization sites in Rajasthan was the earliest evidence of a <strong>ploughed agricultural field showing simultaneous cultivation of two crops (mustard and gram)</strong> discovered?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Ahar / Dhoolkot (आहड़ - Udaipur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर - Sikar/Neem Ka Thana)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Kalibangan (कालीबंगा - Hanumangarh)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Bagor (बागोर - Bhilwara)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Kalibangan (कालीबंगा - Hanumangarh)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Excavations at Kalibangan (on the dried bed of ancient Saraswati / Ghaggar in Hanumangarh by B.B. Lal and B.K. Thapar) revealed the world's earliest criss-cross ploughed agricultural field dating to the pre-Harappan level, where mustard and gram were grown simultaneously in perpendicular furrows.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Ahar?</strong> Ahar (Udaipur) is a Chalcolithic site on the Berach river known as <em>Tamravati Nagari</em>, famous for copper smelting furnaces and Banasian bull figurines.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Ganeshwar?</strong> Ganeshwar on the Kantali river is known as the <em>Mother of Copper Age Civilizations</em> (2800 BCE), famous for 99% pure copper fishhooks and arrowheads.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Bagor?</strong> Bagor (Bhilwara) on the Kothari river is a Mesolithic site that yielded India's earliest evidence of <strong>animal husbandry / domestication (5000 BCE)</strong>.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Dual ploughed field + 7 fire altars = Kalibangan; Animal domestication = Bagor; Copper smelting = Ahar; 99% pure copper fishhooks = Ganeshwar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Archaeological Sites — Bairath & Ashoka Edicts — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 2nd Grade & SI</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In 1837 AD, British officer <strong>Captain Burt</strong> discovered the famous <strong>Bhabru Rock Edict (भाब्रू शिलालेख)</strong> of Emperor Ashoka at which hill in Bairath (Jaipur)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Bijak ki Pahari (बीजक की पहाड़ी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Moti Doongri (मोती डूंगरी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Chidiyatook Pahari (चिड़ियाटूंक पहाड़ी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Mesha Plateau (मेषा का पठार)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Bijak ki Pahari (बीजक की पहाड़ी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Captain Burt discovered the Bhabru Edict atop <strong>Bijak ki Pahari (Bairath)</strong> in 1837, which confirmed Ashoka's devotion to Buddhism (Buddha, Dhamma, Sangha). It was later shifted to the Asiatic Society Museum in Kolkata.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Moti Doongri?</strong> Moti Doongri is a prominent hillock in central Jaipur famous for the Ganesh Temple and royal palace.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Chidiyatook Pahari?</strong> Chidiyatook is the high hill in Jodhpur upon which Rao Jodha constructed the **Mehrangarh Fort (1459 AD)**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Mesha Plateau?</strong> The Mesha Plateau is the 180-meter high tableland in Chittorgarh upon which the historic **Chittorgarh Fort** was built by Chitrangada Mori.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Bairath Buddhist ruins & Ashoka Edict = Bijak ki Pahari & Bhimji ki Doongri; Mehrangarh = Chidiyatook; Chittor = Mesha Plateau.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Mewar Dynasty — Rana Sanga & Battle of Khanwa — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC Assistant Professor & RAS</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    During the historic <strong>Battle of Khanwa (17 March 1527)</strong> between Rana Sanga and Mughal invader Babur, which traditional Rajput war practice of inviting all regional rulers under a single confederacy was revived by Sanga?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Awal-Bawal Treaty (आंवल-बांवल की संधि)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Pati Perawan (पाती परवण परंपरा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Saka and Jauhar (साका एवं जौहर प्रथा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Turra-Kalangi Dangal (तुर्रा-कलंगी दंगल)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Pati Perawan (पाती परवण परंपरा)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Maharana Sanga revived the ancient chivalric tradition of <strong>Pati Perawan (पाती परवण)</strong>, sending written invitations to all Rajput sovereigns, Afghan chiefs (Hasan Khan Mewati, Mahmud Lodi), and Marwar's Rao Ganga to unite under the saffron flag of Mewar against Babur.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Awal-Bawal Treaty?</strong> The Awal-Bawal treaty was concluded in 1453 AD between Maharana Kumbha and Rao Jodha at Sojat to demarcate the political boundary between Mewar and Marwar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Saka and Jauhar?</strong> Saka is the combined ritual of Rajput warriors fighting to the death (*Kesariya*) while women commit self-immolation (*Jauhar*) inside besieged forts.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Turra-Kalangi Dangal?</strong> Turra-Kalangi is a folk poetic theater tradition founded by Tukangir and Shah Ali in Mewar, not a military mobilization custom.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Pati Perawan = Sanga's united anti-Babur confederacy (1527); Awal-Bawal = Kumbha-Jodha boundary demarcation (1453).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Mewar Dynasty — Maharana Pratap & Battle of Dewair — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Why did British historian <strong>Col. James Tod</strong> describe the <strong>Battle of Dewair (October 1582)</strong> as the <em>'Marathon of Mewar' (मेवाड़ का मैराथन)</em>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Because it was the first battle where Pratap used cannon artillery</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Because Pratap and Prince Amar Singh decisively annihilated the Mughal imperial garrison under Sultan Khan and launched the liberation of Mewar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Because Pratap concluded a formal friendship alliance with Emperor Akbar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Because Maharana Pratap shifted his capital permanently to Gogunda</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Because Pratap and Prince Amar Singh decisively annihilated the Mughal imperial garrison under Sultan Khan and launched the liberation of Mewar</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> In October 1582 (Vijyadashami), Maharana Pratap and Prince Amar Singh attacked the crucial Mughal outpost at Dewair. Prince Amar Singh killed Mughal commander Sultan Khan with a legendary spear thrust piercing through horse and rider. Tod called Haldighati the <em>'Thermopylae of Mewar'</em> and Dewair the <em>'Marathon of Mewar'</em>.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Rajputs traditionally relied on cavalry and guerrilla warfare; artillery was a Mughal trademark introduced by Babur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Maharana Pratap never compromised or signed an alliance with Akbar; he fought relentlessly until his demise in 1597.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Pratap established his emergency wartime capital at <strong>Chavand (चावंड)</strong> in 1585 AD, not Gogunda (which was his 1572 coronation site).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Haldighati (1576) = Thermopylae of Mewar; Dewair (1582) = Marathon of Mewar; Chavand (1585) = Pratap's new capital.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Chauhans of Ranthambore — Hammir Dev & 1301 Saka — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    During the 1301 AD Siege of Ranthambore, who was the rebellious Mongol commander given asylum by <strong>Hammir Dev Chauhan</strong>, embodying the legendary vow of <em>'Hammir Hath' (हम्मीर हठ)</em> against Sultan Alauddin Khilji?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Mirza Hakim (मिर्ज़ा हकीम)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Nusrat Khan (नुसरत खां)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Muhammad Shah / Mahimashah (मीर मुहम्मद शाह)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Ulugh Khan (उलुग खां)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Muhammad Shah / Mahimashah (मीर मुहम्मद शाह)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Hammir Dev Chauhan gave shelter to rebel Mongol general Muhammad Shah (Mahimashah) and Khebru who fled from Alauddin Khilji after plundering Gujarat. Hammir refused to surrender his guest, famously stating: <em>'सिंह सवन, सत्पुरुष वचन, कदली फलत इक बार। तिरिया तेल हम्मीर हठ, चढ़े न दूजी बार॥'</em> leading to the historic 1301 AD 1st Saka of Rajasthan.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Mirza Hakim?</strong> Mirza Hakim was the half-brother of Akbar and governor of Kabul who rebelled in 1581 AD.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Nusrat Khan?</strong> Nusrat Khan was Alauddin Khilji's royal general who was killed by a flying rock missile (*Maghribi*) during the siege of Ranthambore.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Ulugh Khan?</strong> Ulugh Khan was Alauddin's general and brother who commanded the initial Mughal siege alongside Nusrat Khan.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Refugee rebel Mongol = Muhammad Shah; Khilji general killed in siege = Nusrat Khan; Hammir's Queen who led Jauhar = Rangdevi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


---

## 🎯 Chapter 1: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / College Lecturer 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following archaeological sites of Rajasthan is historically referred to as "Tamravati Nagari" (ताम्रवती नगरी)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Ahar (आहड़)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Bairath (बैराठ)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Bagor (बागोर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Ahar (आहड़)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> In 10th-11th century copper inscriptions, Ahar was called <em>Tamravati Nagari</em> due to widespread copper smelting hearths and copper celts. In local dialect it is called <em>Dhoolkot</em> (धूलकोट) or <em>Aghatpur</em> (आघाटपुर).<br>
        <strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> Ganeshwar is known as the "Mother of Copper Age Civilizations" (ताम्रयुगीन सभ्यताओं की जननी), NOT Tamravati Nagari.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022 / 2nd Grade 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Who among the following first discovered and identified the prehistoric site of Kalibangan in 1952?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Dayaram Sahni</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Amlanand Ghosh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> B.B. Lal</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> B.K. Thapar</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Amlanand Ghosh</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Amlanand Ghosh discovered Kalibangan in 1952 along the ancient dried bed of river Saraswati (Ghaggar) in Hanumangarh. Excavations were conducted between 1961–1969 by B.B. Lal and B.K. Thapar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The earliest evidence of animal husbandry (पशुपालन के प्राचीनतम साक्ष्य) in the Indian subcontinent has been excavated from which site in Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Bagor (बागोर - Bhilwara)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Ahar (आहड़ - Udaipur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Ganeshwar (गणेश्वर - Sikar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Tilwara (तिलवाड़ा - Barmer)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Bagor (बागोर - Bhilwara)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Excavations by Dr. V.N. Mishra at Mahasati mound on river Kothari in Bagor revealed Mesolithic animal domestication dating back to ~5000 BCE. Adamgarh (MP) and Bagor (Rajasthan) are the two earliest animal husbandry sites in India.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / RAS Pre 2013</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The Bhabru Rock Edict (भाबरू शिलालेख) of Emperor Ashoka was discovered at Bairath (विराटनगर) in 1837 by:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> James Prinsep</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Alexander Cunningham</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Dayaram Sahni</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Captain Burt</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Captain Burt</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Captain Burt discovered the Bhabru edict atop Bijak-ki-Pahari in 1837. In it, Ashoka expresses reverence for the Buddhist Trinity (Buddha, Dhamma, Sangha). It is currently preserved in the Kolkata Royal Asiatic Society Museum.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC Assistant Professor 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The historical 'Battle of Bhutala' (भूताला का युद्ध - ~1227 AD) was fought between which Mewar ruler and the Delhi Sultan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Jaitra Singh and Iltutmish</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Rana Sanga and Ibrahim Lodi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Bappa Rawal and Iltutmish</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Rawal Ratan Singh and Alauddin Khilji</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Jaitra Singh and Iltutmish</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Described in Jaysingh Suri's *Hammir Mada Mardana*. Jaitra Singh defeated Iltutmish. Because the former capital Nagda was destroyed in the conflict, Jaitra Singh fortified Chittorgarh and made it Mewar's new capital.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q6</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC SI 2021 / RAS Pre 2016</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The first Saka (प्रथम साका) of Chittorgarh took place in 1303 AD during the reign of which ruler?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Maharana Kumbha</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Maharana Pratap</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Rana Hammir</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Rawal Ratan Singh</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Rawal Ratan Singh</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Alauddin Khilji invaded Chittor in 1303 AD. Rawal Ratan Singh led the Kesariya alongside warriors Gora & Badal, and Queen Padmini committed Jauhar with 1600 women. Khilji renamed Chittor to *Khizrabad*.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q7</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The historical Jauhar of Rani Rang Devi (रानी रंगदेवी) was related to which fort?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Siwana</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Jalore</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Chittorgarh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Ranthambhore</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Ranthambhore</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> In 1301 AD, King Hammir Dev Chauhan of Ranthambore died in battle against Alauddin Khilji, and Queen Rang Devi performed the first documented Jauhar (water/fire Jauhar at Padmala pond) of Rajasthan.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q8</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which Rajput sovereign revived the ancient custom of 'Pati Perawan' (पाती परवण) to unite all Rajput rulers under one banner against Babur in the Battle of Khanwa (1527)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Maharana Pratap</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Rao Maldeo</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Maharana Kumbha</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Rana Sanga (Sangram Singh I)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Rana Sanga (Sangram Singh I)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Under *Pati Perawan*, Rana Sanga sent royal invitation letters to all clans. Rulers like Prithviraj (Amer), Maldeo (Marwar), Kalyanmal (Bikaner), Hasan Khan Mewati, and Mahmud Lodi fought together at Khanwa on 17 March 1527.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q9</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Who commanded the vanguard (हरावल सेना / Harawal) of Maharana Pratap's army in the Battle of Haldighati (1576)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Hakim Khan Sur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Jhala Bida</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Ramdas Rathore</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Man Singh I</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Hakim Khan Sur</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Afghan general Hakim Khan Sur led the Harawal (front attack division) of Pratap's forces, while Bhil King Rana Punja commanded the rearguard. Jhala Bida donned Pratap's royal crown (*Chhatra*) when the Maharana was wounded.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q10</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2013, 2018 / College Lecturer 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which ruler of Marwar is remembered in history as the "Forgotten Hero" (भूला-बिसरा राजा) and "The Precursor of Maharana Pratap" (प्रताप का अग्रगामी)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Rao Maldeo</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Rao Chandrasen</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Rao Jodha</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Maharaja Jaswant Singh I</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Rao Chandrasen</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Rao Chandrasen attended Akbar's Nagaur Durbar in 1570 but refused subservience. He abandoned Jodhpur fort and conducted guerrilla warfare from Bhadrajun and Siwana, pioneering the path later followed by Maharana Pratap.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q11</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2015 / 1st Grade 2020</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    "For a handful of millet, I would have lost the empire of all Hindustan." Who made this historic remark after which battle?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Sher Shah Suri after Battle of Giri Sumel (1544)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Akbar after Battle of Haldighati</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Alauddin Khilji after Siege of Jalore</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Babur after Battle of Khanwa</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Sher Shah Suri after Battle of Giri Sumel (1544)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> In January 1544 at Jaitaran (Pali), Marwar commanders Jaita and Kumpa launched a ferocious charge with just 12,000 warriors that nearly destroyed Sher Shah's 80,000-strong army before reserve cavalry saved him.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q12</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Reservoirs named "Nana Sahab ka Jhalra" (नाना साहब का झालरा) and "Ibrahim ka Jhalra" are located in which fort?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Gagron (Jhalawar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Jaigarh (Jaipur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Taragarh (Ajmer)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Bayana Fort (Bharatpur)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Taragarh (Ajmer)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Taragarh Fort in Ajmer (Garh Beetli) contains classic historical water storage reservoirs including Nana Sahab ka Jhalra, Ibrahim ka Jhalra, Gol Jhalra, and Bada Jhalra.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q13</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>

    Match List-I (Ruler) with List-II (Dynasty):<br>
    List-I: (A) Bauk, (B) Rao Siha, (C) Bappa, (D) Vasudev<br>
    List-II: (i) Pratihar, (ii) Rathore, (iii) Guhil, (iv) Chauhan
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A)-(iv), (B)-(iii), (C)-(ii), (D)-(i)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A)-(iii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(ii)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Bauk = Gurjara-Pratihara (Jodhpur inscription 837 AD), Rao Siha = Rathore (Founder of Marwar branch in 13th century), Bappa Rawal = Guhil (Mewar, 734 AD), Vasudev = Chauhan (Founding ruler at Shakambhari, 551 AD).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Vigrahraj IV bore the title *Kavibandhava* (कविबांधव). He authored *Harikeli* drama. Excerpts of this play were engraved on the walls of the Saraswati Kanthabharana Sanskrit school in Ajmer (later remodeled into Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra).

      </div>

# Chapter 2: Important Saints and Folk Deities of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के प्रमुख संत एवं लोक देवता)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: Why Folk Deities (Lok Devtas) Exist (लोक देवता क्यों पूजे जाते हैं?)</div>
  <em>"A student once asked: 'Sir, throughout India people worship Vedic gods like Vishnu, Shiva, and Durga. Why does Rajasthan worship local human beings like Tejaji (तेजाजी), Pabuji (पाबूजी), and Gogaji (गोगाजी)?' 
  <br>The answer lies in our history. In medieval Rajasthan, there was no standing police force to protect innocent villagers from desert bandits and invading armies. When plunderers came to steal a village's cows (which were a farmer’s entire livelihood), these brave men unsheathed their swords, fought against overwhelming odds, and gave their lives to protect the cows (गौ-रक्षा), the women, and the honor of their motherland. After their death, their supreme sacrifice turned them into <strong>Lok Devtas (लोक देवता)</strong> and <strong>Jujhar (जूझार - warrior martyrs)</strong>."</em>
</div>

---

## 1. The Sacred Panch Pirs of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के पंच पीर) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Repeated Every Exam</span>

<div class="mnemonic-box">
  <div class="mnemonic-title">🧠 Master Mnemonic: The Traditional Couplet (पंच पीर दोहा)</div>
  <blockquote>
    <strong>"पाबू, हड़भू, रामदे, मांगलिया, मेहा | पाँचूं पीर पधारज्यौ, गोगाजी जेहा"</strong>
  </blockquote>
  <strong>The 5 Pirs (Worshipped by both Hindus and Muslims):</strong>
  <ol>
    <li><strong>Pabuji (पाबूजी)</strong></li>
    <li><strong>Harbhuji (हड़भूजी)</strong></li>
    <li><strong>Ramdevji (रामदेवजी)</strong></li>
    <li><strong>Mehaji Manglia (मेहाजी मांगलिया)</strong></li>
    <li><strong>Gogaji (गोगाजी)</strong></li>
  </ol>
  <em>(⚠️ Trap Alert: Tejaji / तेजाजी, Devnarayanji / देवनारायण जी, and Mallinathji / मल्लीनाथ जी are great Lok Devtas, but they are NOT in the Panch Pirs!)</em>
</div>

---

### Detailed Analysis of the Panch Pirs & Major Lok Devtas

#### 1. Gogaji (गोगाजी - Chauhan Rajput) — "Jahir Pir" (जाहिर पीर) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Asked 5+ Times</span>
* **Birth & Lineage:** Born at **Dadrewa (ददरेवा - Churu / चूरू)** to King Zewar (जेवर सिंह) and Queen Bachchal (बाछल दे). Guru: **Gorakhnath (गुरु गोरखनाथ)**.
* **Battle & Sacrifice:** Fought against invading forces of **Mahmud Ghaznavi (महमूद गजनवी)** and greedy stepbrothers Arjan-Surjan (अर्जुन-सर्जन) to protect his cattle. Fought even after his head was severed in battle!
* **Shrines:**
  * **Shirshmedi (शीर्षमेड़ी - Dadrewa, Churu):** Where his head fell in battle.
  * **Ghurmedi / Gogamedi (धुरमेड़ी / गोगामेड़ी - Nohar, Hanumangarh):** Where his torso fell. The temple architecture resembles a mosque with the sacred Arabic word **"Bismillah" (बिस्मिल्लाह)** inscribed on the entrance gate (built by Feroz Shah Tughlaq; renovated by Maharaja Ganga Singh of Bikaner).
  * **Gogaji ki Oladi (गोगाजी की ओलडी):** Located at Sanchore (सांचौर - Jalore).
* **Worship & Fair:** Worshipped under **Khejdi (खेजड़ी / शमी)** trees (*"गांव-गांव गोगा, गांव-गांव खेजड़ी"*). Fair on **Bhadrapada Krishna Navami (भाद्रपद कृष्ण नवमी - Goga Navami / गोगा नवमी)**. Priests are called *Chhayal (चायल - Muslim)* for 11 months and Hindu Pujari for 1 month.

---

#### 2. Ramdevji (रामदेवजी - Tanwar Rajput) — "Ramsa-Pir / Runicha ra Dhani" (पीरों के पीर / रुणीचा रा धणी) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Asked 7+ Times</span>
* **Birth & Parents:** Born at **Undu-Kashmir (उण्डू-काश्मीर - Shiv Tehsil, Barmer)** to Ajmalji Tanwar (अजमल जी) and Mainade (मैणादे). Regarded as an incarnation of **Lord Krishna (भगवान कृष्ण का अवतार)**.
* **Main Shrine:** **Ramdevra / Runicha (रामदेवरा / रुणीचा - Pokhran, Jaisalmer)** where he took living Samadhi in 1458 AD on Bhadrapad Shukla Ekadashi.
* **Social Reform & Communal Harmony:**
  * Fierce opponent of caste discrimination and untouchability. Adopted Dalit girl **Dalibai (डालीबाई)** as his sister (Dalibai’s Samadhi is right in front of Ramdevji's).
  * Founded the **Kamadia Panth (कामुड़िया पंथ)**; female disciples perform the renowned **Terah Taali Dance (तेरह ताली नृत्य)** with 13 cymbals.
  * Composed the poetic sermon book **Chaubees Vaniyan (चौबीस वाणियां)** (the only Lok Devta who was also an accomplished poet!).
* **Key Terms Associated with Ramdevji:**
  * **Nezha (नेजा):** His 5-colored sacred flag (पचरंगी ध्वजा).
  * **Jamma (जम्मा / जंभा):** Night-long devotional singing wake (रात्रि जागरण).
  * **Rikhya (रिखिया):** His Dalit Meghwal devotees (मेघवाल भक्त).
  * **Pagliye (पगलिये):** Stone-carved holy footprints worshipped in village shrines (*Than / थान*).

---

#### 3. Pabuji (पाबूजी - Rathore Rajput) — "God of Camels & Plagues" (ऊंटों के देवता / प्लेग रक्षक) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 High-Yield</span>
* **Birth & Lineage:** Born in 1239 AD at **Kolu (कोलूमण्ड - Phalodi / फलोदी)** to Dhandhalji Rathore (धांधल जी). Regarded as the **incarnation of Lord Lakshman (लक्ष्मण जी का अवतार)**.
* **Sacrifice:** Interrupted his marriage pheras with Princess Sodhi of Amarkot to protect the cows of **Deval Charani (देवल चारणी)**. Mounted on the black mare **Kesar Kalami (केसर कालमी)**, he fought his brother-in-law Jindrao Khinchi (जिंदराव खींची) at village **Dhechu (देचू - 1276 AD)** and achieved martyrdom.
* **Worship & Phad:**
  * Credited with introducing the first camels (*Sands / सांड*) to Rajasthan. Chief deity of the camel-herding **Rebari / Raika (रेबारी / राइका)** community.
  * **Pabuji ki Phad (पाबूजी की फड़):** Rajasthan's most popular painted scroll, sung by Nayak / Bhopa (नायक/भोपा) priests at night accompanied by the **Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था)** instrument to cure ailing camels and livestock.

---

#### 4. Tejaji (तेजाजी - Nagvanshiya Jat) — "Kala-Bala ke Devta" (काला-बाला के देवता / गायों के मुक्तिदाता) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Very High Priority</span>
* **Birth:** Born in 1073 CE at **Kharnal (खरनाल - Nagaur)** to Taharji (ताहड़ जी) and Ramkunwari (रामकुंवरी). Married to **Pemal (पेमल दे)**.
* **Sacrifice at Sursura:** Rescued the cows of Lachha Gujari (लाछां गूजरी) from the Mer bandits. Kept his vow to a cobra and allowed it to bite his uninjured tongue at Sursura (सुरसुरा - Kishangarh, Ajmer).
* **Main Shrines:** 
  * **Parbatsar (परबतसर - Nagaur / Didwana-Kuchaman):** Site of the massive **Veer Teja Cattle Fair (वीर तेजा पशु मेला)** on *Bhadrapada Shukla Dashami (भाद्रपद शुक्ल दशमी - Teja Dashami / तेजा दशमी)*.
  * **Sendariya (सेंदड़िया - Bite site), Sursura (सुरसुरा - Samadhi site), Beawar (ब्यावर), Bansi Dugari (बांसी दुगारी - Bundi).**
* **Iconography:** Valiant warrior riding his mare **Lilan / Singhari (लीलण / सिणगारी)** with a drawn sword, and a snake striking his tongue.

---

#### 5. Devnarayanji (देवनारायण जी - Gurjar) — "Incarnation of Lord Vishnu" (विष्णु का अवतार)
* **Birth:** Born in 1243 AD to Bagadawat (बगड़ावत) chief **Bhoja (सवाई भोज)** and Sedu Gujari (सेडू गूजरी).
* **Shrines & Phad:** Main seat at **Goth Dhadawata / Asind (गोठ दड़ावता / आसींद - Bhilwara)** on Khari river (खारी नदी). Fair on **Bhadrapad Shukla Saptami (भाद्रपद शुक्ल सप्तमी)**.
* **Devnarayanji ki Phad (देवनारायण जी की फड़):** The longest, oldest, and most detailed Phad in Rajasthan (accompanied by the **Jantar / जन्तर** musical instrument). Indian Post issued a postal stamp in 1992. Neem leaves (नीम के पत्ते) and brick slabs are offered in worship.

---

## 2. Prominent Bhakti & Environmental Saints of Rajasthan (प्रमुख संत एवं सम्प्रदाय)

```
                                 BHAKTI SAINTS OF RAJASTHAN (प्रमुख संत)
                                                     │
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                                       │
    NIRGUN TRADITION (निर्गुण भक्ति)                                                        SAGUN TRADITION (सगुण भक्ति)
(Formless Divine / Social Reform)                                                       (Devotion to Idols / Avatars)
         │                                                                                       │
   • Jambhoji (जाम्भोजी - Vishnoi 29 Rules)                                                • Mirabai (मीराबाई - Krishna Bhakti)
   • Jasnathji (जसनाथ जी - Agni Dance - 36 Rules)                                         • Gavari Bai (गवरी बाई - "Meera of Vagad")
   • Dadudayal (दादूदयाल - "Kabir of Rajasthan")                                          • Ranabai (रानाबाई - "2nd Meera")
   • Saint Mavji (संत मावजी - Beneshwar / Chopada)                                         • Saint Durlabhji (संत दुर्लभजी)
   • Saint Ram Charan (रामचरण जी - Ramsnehi - Phuldol)
```

---

### Detailed Analysis of Major Saints

#### 1. Jambhoji (जाम्भोजी - 1451–1536 AD) — The World's First Environmental Saint <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Asked 6+ Times</span>
* **Birth:** Born on Bhadrapad Krishna Ashtami (Janmashtami / जन्माष्टमी) 1451 AD at **Pipasar (पीपासर - Nagaur)** to Lohatji Panwar (लोहट जी पंवार) and Hansa Devi (हंसा देवी). Childhood name: *Dhanraj (धनराज)*.
* **Founding of the Vishnoi Sect (बिश्नोई सम्प्रदाय - 1485 AD):**
  * Founded at **Samrathal Dhoro (समराथल धोरा - Bikaner)** by establishing **29 strict commandments** (*Bish-noi* = 20 [बीस] + 9 [नौ]).
  * Commandments emphasize non-violence, strict protection of **green trees (especially Khejdi / शमी / जांटी)**, prohibition of animal slaughter, personal hygiene, and truthfulness.
* **Royal Respect:** Sultans Sikander Lodhi and Rao Jodha respected his spiritual stature; Sikander Lodhi banned cow slaughter upon his advice.
* **Holy Texts:** *Jambha Samhita (जम्भ संहिता), Jambha Sagar (जम्भ सागर), Jambha Gita, 120 Shabad*. Main pilgrimage seat: **Mukam / Talwa (मुकाम / तालवा - Nokha, Bikaner)**.

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">📖 The Supreme Ecological Martyrdom: Khejarli Massacre (खेजड़ली बलिदान - 1730 AD)</div>
  <em><strong>[Verified Historical Event]</strong> In September 1730 AD (Bhadrapad Shukla Dashami / भाद्रपद शुक्ल दशमी), Maharaja Abhay Singh (अभयसिंह) of Jodhpur ordered his royal dewan Girdhar Das (गिरधर दास) to fell green Khejdi trees in Khejarli village (खेजड़ली गांव) to burn lime for palace construction. <strong>Amrita Devi Vishnoi (अमृता देवी बिश्नोई)</strong>, along with her three daughters, hugged the trees declaring: <strong>"सिर साठे रूंख रहे, तो भी सस्तो जाण" (Sir Saanthe Rookh Rahe, To Bhi Sasto Jaan)</strong> (If a tree is saved even at the cost of one's severed head, it is still a bargain!). The royal soldiers mercilessly chopped down the trees along with the people hugging them. A total of <strong>363 brave Vishnoi men and women</strong> laid down their lives before the Maharaja halted the felling. This remains the world's greatest environmental sacrifice, commemorated every year at the Khejarli Fair.</em>
</div>

---

#### 2. Jasnathji (जसनाथ जी - 1482–1506 AD) — The Fire Dancers of Katariasar
* **Birth:** Born in 1482 AD at **Katariasar (कतरियासर - Bikaner)**. Received land grant of 12 villages from Sultan Sikander Lodhi. Took living Samadhi at the young age of 24.
* **Sect & Principles:** Founded the **Jasnathi Sect (जसनाथी सम्प्रदाय)** based on **36 moral rules (36 नियम)**. Followers wear black woolen threads around their necks and revere the peacock feather and Jaal (जाल) tree.
* **Agni Dance (अग्नि नृत्य):** Performed exclusively by Jasnathi Jat Siddhs who dance barefoot over burning charcoal embers (*Dhoona / धूणा*) chanting *"Fateh Fateh" (फतेह-फतेह)*.

---

#### 3. Dadudayal (दादूदयाल - 1544–1603 AD) — The "Kabir of Rajasthan" (राजस्थान का कबीर) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 High-Yield</span>
* **Life & Headquarters:** Born in Ahmedabad; came to Sambhar in 1568; established his principal religious Peeth at **Naraina / Narena (नराणा / नरेना - Jaipur)**.
* **Met Emperor Akbar:** Preached spiritual truth to Akbar at Fatehpur Sikri (फतेहपुर सीकरी) in 1585 AD on invitation of Raja Bhagwan Das.
* **Teachings & Literature:** Preached in vernacular *Dhundhari-Hindi* (*सधुक्कड़ी भाषा*); compiled in *Dadu Dayal ri Vani (दादू दयाल री वाणी)* and *Dadu ra Duha (दादू रा दूहा)*. Worship hall is called **Alakh Dariba (अलख दरीबा)**.
* **5 Sub-Branches of Dadu Panth:** 1. *Khalsa (खालसा)*, 2. *Virakta (विरक्त)*, 3. *Uttardev / Sthankdhari (उत्तरादे / स्थानकधारी)*, 4. *Khaki (खाकी)*, 5. *Naga (नागा)* (founded by Sant Sundardas / सुंदरदास जी who maintained armed defense forces for Jaipur state).
* **Famous Disciples (52 Pillars / 52 थंभे):**
  * **Sant Rajjabji (संत रज्जब जी):** Traveled to get married, but upon hearing Dadu’s sermon (*"रज्जब तैं गजब कियौ, सिर पर बांध्यौ मोड़..."*), he gave up worldly life and remained in the bridegroom’s attire (*दूल्हे के वेश में*) for the rest of his life! Composed *Sarvangi (सर्वांगी)*.
  * **Sant Sundardas (संत सुंदरदास):** Great scholar who composed *Sundar Vilas (सुंदर विलास)*.

---

#### 4. Saint Mavji (संत मावजी - 1714–1784 AD) — The Prophet of Beneshwar
* **Birth & Peeth:** Born in **Saabla (साबला - Dungarpur)**. Regarded by disciples as the **Nishkalank (निष्कलंक सम्प्रदाय - spotless) 10th Kalki Avatar (कल्कि अवतार)** of Lord Vishnu.
* **Creation of Beneshwar Dham (बेणेश्वर धाम):** Constructed the holy shrine at the **Triveni Sangam (त्रिवेणी संगम)** of Som (सोम), Mahi (माही), and Jakham (जाखम) rivers in Dungarpur.
* **Prophetic Texts (Chopada / चोपड़ा):** Wrote 5 massive prophecy volumes containing third-world-war predictions and socio-environmental forecasts in the **Wagdi dialect (वागड़ी बोली)**.

---

#### 5. Saint Ram Charan (संत रामचरण जी) & Ramsnehi Sect (रामस्नेही सम्प्रदाय)
* Founded the **Ramsnehi Sect** (worship of the formless divine name *Ram / राम* without idol worship).
* **Main Headquarters:** **Shahpura (शाहपुरा - Bhilwara)** where the vibrant **Phuldol Festival (फूलडोल महोत्सव)** is held on *Chaitra Krishna Pratipada to Panchami (चैत्र कृष्ण प्रतिपदा से पंचमी)*.
* **4 Regional Branches & Founders (RPSC Direct Match Favorite):**
  1. **Shahpura (शाहपुरा - Bhilwara):** *Sant Ram Charan (संत रामचरण जी)*
  2. **Ren (मेड़ता, नागौर - Ren, Nagaur):** *Sant Daryavji (संत दरियाव जी)*
  3. **Sinthal (सिंहथल - Bikaner):** *Sant Hariramdasji (संत हरिरामदास जी)*
  4. **Kherapa (खेड़ापा - Jodhpur):** *Sant Ramdasji (संत रामदास जी)*

---

## 📝 Chapter 2: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Lok Devta & Sampradaya Deep-Dive</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following folk deities of Rajasthan is worshiped as the deity of cattle protection who sacrificed his life fighting against the Meenas of Mer (मेर के मीणा) to rescue the cows of Lachha Gujari (लाछां गूजरी)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Devnarayanji (देवनारायणजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Veer Tejaji (वीर तेजाजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Mangleeya Mehaji (मांगलिया मेहाजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Pabuji Rathore (पाबूजी)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Veer Tejaji (वीर तेजाजी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Tejaji protected the cows of Lachha Gujari from the Mers of Ajmer at Sursura / Sendariya. He was bitten by serpent Basak on his tongue.<br>
        <strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> Pabuji protected the cows of Deval Charni against Jind Rao Khichi of Jalore at Dechu (Jodhpur). Do not confuse Lachha Gujari (Tejaji) with Deval Charni (Pabuji).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Sant Sampradaya Conceptual Pattern</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Consider the following statements regarding the <strong>Dadu Panth</strong> (दादू पंथ):
    <br>1. Saint Dadu Dayal gave his spiritual sermons in 'Sadukkadi' or Dhundhari dialect.
    <br>2. The prayer hall of Dadu Panth is called 'Alakh Dariba' (अलख दरीबा).
    <br>3. The Naga branch of Dadu Panth was founded by disciple Saint Sundardas Ji (Junior).
    <br>4. Dadu Panthis traditionally cremate their dead immediately according to Vedic rituals.
    <br>Which of the statements given above are correct?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 1, 2 and 3 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 1, 2, 3 and 4</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 2 and 4 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 1 and 3 only</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) 1, 2 and 3 only</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Statement 4 is incorrect because Dadu Panthis do NOT cremate or bury their dead. Instead, they leave the mortal remains in the open forest (like Bhairana hill) to serve as food for animals and birds. Dadu Dayal's body was placed at Bhairana hill in Naraina (Jaipur). Statements 1, 2, and 3 are historically authentic.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Branches & Founders Matching</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Match List-I (Ramsnehi Branch) with List-II (Founding Saint):
    <br><strong>List-I (Branch)</strong>
    <br>(A) Shahpura (Bhilwara)
    <br>(B) Ren (Merta, Nagaur)
    <br>(C) Sinhthal (Bikaner)
    <br>(D) Khedapa (Jodhpur)
    <br><strong>List-II (Founder)</strong>
    <br>(i) Sant Daryavji (संत दरियावजी)
    <br>(ii) Sant Ramcharanji (संत रामचरणजी)
    <br>(iii) Sant Ramdasji (संत रामदासजी)
    <br>(iv) Sant Hariramdasji (संत हरीरामदासजी)
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> (A)-(iv), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iii)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(iii), (C)-(iv), (D)-(i)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong>
        <br>• Shahpura (Main Peeth) = Sant Ramcharanji (celebrates Phuldol festival).
        <br>• Ren (Nagaur) = Sant Daryavji (Ram = R for Raheem, M for Mohammed syncretism).
        <br>• Sinhthal (Bikaner) = Sant Hariramdasji (authored 'Nishani' yoga treatise).
        <br>• Khedapa (Jodhpur) = Sant Ramdasji.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Lok Devi Iconography & History</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which folk goddess of Rajasthan is known as the goddess of <strong>Bees (मधुमक्खियों की देवी / भंवर माता)</strong> whose temple at Rewasa (Sikar) possesses the longest folk hymn (गीत) in Rajasthani literature, and whose shrine Aurangzeb's army failed to desecrate due to a swarm of divine wasps?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Sakambhari Mata (शाकम्भरी माता)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Karni Mata (करणी माता)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Jeen Mata (जीण माता)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Shila Devi (शीला देवी)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Jeen Mata (जीण माता)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Jeen Mata's temple is on Kajla mountain in Rewasa (Sikar). Her brother Harsh Bhairav's temple is nearby. When Aurangzeb attacked, giant wasps repelled the imperial soldiers, leading Aurangzeb to pledge 1.25 mounds of oil for the akhand deepak. Her folk hymn (Chirja) is the longest in Rajasthani folklore.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Environmental & Ecological Ethics</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Saint <strong>Jambhoji</strong> founded the Bishnoi Sampradaya in 1485 CE at Samrathal Dhora (Bikaner) by laying down 29 tenets. Which of the following animals and trees are held most sacred by Bishnois?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Lion (सिंह) and Banyan tree (बरगद)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Camel (ऊंट) and Rohida tree (रोहिड़ा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Blackbuck (काला हिरण) and Khejri tree (खेजड़ी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Cow (गाय) and Peepal tree (पीपल)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Blackbuck (काला हिरण) and Khejri tree (खेजड़ी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Jambhoji considered Khejri (Prosopis cineraria) and blackbucks sacrosanct. This ecological consciousness led to the historic Khejarli massacre of 1730 CE where Amrita Devi Bishnoi and 363 Bishnois sacrificed their lives to protect green Khejri trees from the axe of Maharaja Abhai Singh's royal officials.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Lok Devis — Karni Mata & Deshnoke Rats (Kaba) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    At the world-famous marble temple of <strong>Karni Mata (Deshnoke, Bikaner)</strong>, what are the thousands of sanctified <strong>White and Black Rats</strong> roaming freely and fed by devotees known as, and which community strictly abstains from bathing in Kolayat Lake due to the drowning of Karni Mata's foster son Lakhan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Rats called 'Kaba' (काबा); Charan community (चारण समाज)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Rats called 'Mooshak'; Bishnoi community</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Rats called 'Sewak'; Gurjar community</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Rats called 'Bhomia'; Meena community</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Rats called 'Kaba' (काबा); Charan community (चारण समाज)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The rats of Karni Mata are revered as **'Kaba' (काबा)** (believed to be reincarnated Charan devotees). Seeing a white Kaba brings supreme blessings. Because Karni Mata's adopted son Lakhan (Lakhana) drowned in Kolayat Lake on Kartik Purnima, the **Charan community never visits or bathes in Kolayat Lake**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Mooshak is the general Sanskrit term for mouse, not the sacred Deshnoke name.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Gurjars revere Devnarayanji and Kailadevi.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Bhomiyas are martyr warrior deities.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Karni Mata rats = Kaba; Kolayat bath avoided by = Charan community; Child name of Karni Mata = Riddhi Bai; Foundation of Mehrangarh laid by = Karni Mata.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Lok Devis — Jin Mata of Rewasa (Sikar) & Deepest Chirja Hymns — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The temple of <strong>Jin Mata (जीण माता, रेवासा - सीकर)</strong> on the Kajal Shikhar hill is famous for possessing the <strong>longest devotional folk song / hymn (सबसे लम्बा लोकगीत - 'Chirja' / चिरजा)</strong> in Rajasthani folklore. Which Mughal Emperor attempted to destroy this temple but fled when attacked by swarms of divine bees (Bhaunre)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Akbar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Aurangzeb (औरंगजेब - who subsequently sent 1.25 maunds of pure ghee/oil annually from Delhi for the eternal lamp)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Alauddin Khilji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Jahangir</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Aurangzeb (औरंगजेब - who subsequently sent 1.25 maunds of pure ghee/oil annually from Delhi for the eternal lamp)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> When **Aurangzeb's Mughal army** attacked the Jin Mata temple at Rewasa (Sikar), millions of honeybees (*Bhaunre*) swarmed out of the sanctum, stinging the soldiers into retreat. A repentant Aurangzeb pledged to send **1.25 maunds (सव्वा मण) of pure oil/ghee from Delhi** to sustain the eternal flame (*Akhand Jyoti*), a royal custom continued for centuries. Jin Mata's devotional ballad (*Chirja*) is the longest song in Rajasthani folk literature (sung with *Damru* and *Chang* by her Jogi/Bhopa priests).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Akbar?</strong> Akbar walked barefoot to Amer and Jwala Ji.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Alauddin?</strong> Alauddin destroyed temples at Chittorgarh, Jalore, and Ranthambore.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Jahangir?</strong> Jahangir gave royal grants to Pushkar.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Longest Rajasthani Folk Song = Jin Mata Chirja; Attack by bees on Aurangzeb = Jin Mata (Rewasa, Sikar); Brother's shrine = Harshnath (Bhairon).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Lok Devis — Kailadevi of Karauli & Languriya Songs — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    At the <strong>Kailadevi Fair (कैलादेवी, करौली)</strong> on the banks of the Kalisil River during Chaitra Shukla Ashtami (Lakkhi Fair), what are the distinct folk songs and ecstatic devotional dances performed by Meena and Gurjar devotees in front of the <strong>Bohra Bhagat ki Chhatri</strong> called?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Ghoomar and Panihari</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Chari and Terah Taali</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Languriya songs and Jogania / Kanak-Dandavat dance (लांगुरिया गीत एवं जोगणिया नृत्य)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Gavari and Hathi Mana</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Languriya songs and Jogania / Kanak-Dandavat dance (लांगुरिया गीत एवं जोगणिया नृत्य)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Devotees of **Kailadevi (Karauli)** perform **Languriya (लांगुरिया)** songs and energetic dances honoring Lord Hanuman (*Languriya*). Devotees offer prostrations (*Kanak-Dandavat*) at the cenotaph of **Bohra Bhagat (बोहरा भगत की छतरी)** located right in front of the main temple complex to cure snakebites and childhood illnesses.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> *Panihari* and *Ghoomar* are general Marwari songs/dances.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> *Chari* is Gujjar dance of Kishangarh; *Terah Taali* is Kamad dance of Ramdevji.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> *Gavari* is Bhil tribal dance drama of Mewar.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Languriya songs & Bohra Bhagat Chhatri = Kailadevi (Karauli, Kalisil river); Fair = Chaitra Shukla 8 (Lakkhi Mela).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

  <div class="pyq-question">

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Brahmin priest; Lion vahana; Prashad</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Nayak priest; Camel vahana; Lapasi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Meghwal priest; Horse vahana; Churma</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Kumhar (Potter / कुम्हार) priest; Donkey (गधा) vahana; Basoda (बासोड़ा - stale cold food)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Kumhar (Potter / कुम्हार) priest; Donkey (गधा) vahana; Basoda (बासोड़ा - stale cold food)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Sheetla Mata is the patron goddess of smallpox/measles (*Chetak/Bodari*). Her vehicle is the **Donkey (गधा)**, her priest is from the **Kumhar (Potter) caste**, and her idol is a broken sacred stone. On **Chaitra Krishna Ashtami (Sheetlashtami)**, no kitchen fires are lit, and families consume cold food cooked the previous evening called **Basoda (बासोड़ा)**.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Nayak priests sing Pabuji's Phad.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Meghwal priests are *Rikhiya* of Ramdevji.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kumhar priest + Donkey vehicle + Cold stale food (Basoda) + Chaitra Krishna 8 = Sheetla Mata (Chaksu, Jaipur).

  </details>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">

<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Khwaja Moinuddin Hasan Chishti (<strong>Garib Nawaz</strong>), who arrived in Ajmer during the reign of Prithviraj Chauhan III (1192 AD), was initiated into the Chishti order by which spiritual master in Sanjar (Persia)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Sheikh Hamiduddin Nagauri</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Khwaja Usman Harooni (ख्वाजा उस्मान हारूनी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Bakhtiyar Kaki</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Nizamuddin Auliya</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Khwaja Usman Harooni (ख्वाजा उस्मान हारूनी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti was the direct disciple (*Murid*) of **Khwaja Usman Harooni**. He established the Chishti Sufi Silsila in India at Ajmer. His annual **Urs** is celebrated from **1st to 6th Rajab** at the Ajmer Dargah, inaugurated when the Gauri family of Bhilwara hoists the sacred flag on the Buland Darwaza.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Hamiduddin Nagauri?</strong> Sheikh Hamiduddin (*Sultan-ut-Tarikin* / King of Ascetics) was Khwaja's chief disciple who lived as a vegetarian farmer in Suwal, Nagaur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Bakhtiyar Kaki?</strong> Qutbuddin Bakhtiyar Kaki was Khwaja's disciple in Delhi for whom the Qutb Minar was named.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Nizamuddin Auliya?</strong> Hazrat Nizamuddin was the master of Amir Khusrau in Delhi.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Master of Khwaja Chishti = Khwaja Usman Harooni; Urs dates = 1 to 6 Rajab; Flag hoisted by = Gauri Family (Bhilwara); Sultan-ut-Tarikin = Hamiduddin Nagauri.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

He laid down his life fighting cattle raiders at **Rathjana** to protect a Brahmin's cows. He is worshipped as the supreme Kuldevta of the **Jakhar gotra Jats**. His annual fair is held on **14 October (Bhadrapad / Ashvin)** at Bigagaon.<br><br>

<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Baba Ramdevji (रामदेवजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Veer Tejaji Maharaj (वीर तेजाजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Gogaji Chauhan (गोगाजी)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Veer Tejaji Maharaj (वीर तेजाजी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Veer Tejaji</strong> is a supremely revered folk deity of Rajasthan (born in Kharnal, Nagaur), but he is **NOT one of the Panch Pirs**. The 5 Panch Pirs are: **Pabuji, Harbhuji, Ramdevji, Mehaji Manglia, and Gogaji** (*'पाबू, हड़बू, रामदे, मांगलिया मेहा। पांचू पीर पधारज्ये, गोगाजी जेहा॥'*).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Pabuji?</strong> Pabuji of Kolumand is one of the 5 Panch Pirs.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Ramdevji?</strong> Ramdevji of Runicha is one of the 5 Panch Pirs (worshipped as *Ramshah Pir* by Muslims).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Gogaji?</strong> Gogaji of Dadrewa is one of the 5 Panch Pirs (worshipped as *Jahir Pir*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Panch Pirs = Pabuji, Harbhuji, Ramdevji, Mehaji, Gogaji. (Tejaji & Devnarayanji are NOT in Panch Pirs!).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> His painted prayer scroll (Phad)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> His turban and silver sandals (खड़ाऊं)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> His miraculous wooden bullock cart (छकड़ा गाड़ी - used to fetch fodder for disabled cows)</div>

  <details class="pyq-details">

    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Harbhuji (son of Maharaj Mehaji Sankhla of Bhundel, Nagaur and cousin of Ramdevji) devoted his life to serving crippled, sick, and disabled cows. In his temple at Bengti (built by Maharaja Ajit Singh of Jodhpur in 1721), his **miraculous wooden cart (छकड़ा गाड़ी)** is enshrined and worshipped. He also gifted a blessed dagger (*Katari*) to Rao Jodha to reclaim Mandore.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Phads are worshipped for Pabuji and Devnarayanji.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Sandals (*Paglya*) are worshipped for Ramdevji, Jasnathji, and Dadu Dayal.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Harbhuji temple at Bengti = Worshipped through wooden bullock cart (छकड़ा गाड़ी); Temple built by Maharaja Ajit Singh.

  </details>
</div>

  <div class="pyq-question">
    During the 3rd Saka of Chittorgarh (1568 AD) against Mughal Emperor Akbar, which Rathore warrior earned the immortal title of <strong>'Four-Handed Deity' (चार हाथों वाले लोक देवता)</strong> after lifting his wounded uncle Jaimal onto his shoulders in combat?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Veer Kallaji Rathore (वीर कल्लाजी राठौड़)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Patta Chundawat (पत्ता चूंडावत)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Achaldas Khinchi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Durgadas Rathore</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Veer Kallaji Rathore (वीर कल्लाजी राठौड़)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> When **Jaimal Rathore** was incapacitated by Akbar's musket *Sangram*, his nephew **Kallaji Rathore** hoisted Jaimal onto his shoulders. Both warriors wielded two swords each, cutting through the Mughal lines with 4 flashing blades, causing spectators to hail Kallaji as the **'Four-Handed God' (चार हाथों वाले देवता / शेषनाग का अवतार)**. Kallaji's cenotaph is between Bhairon Pol and Hanuman Pol at Chittorgarh.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>

          <li><strong>Why not (B) Patta Chundawat?</strong> Patta of Kelwa was the supreme commander of Chittorgarh whose wife Phool Kanwar led the 1568 Jauhar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Achaldas Khinchi?</strong> Achaldas was the king of Gagron who sacrificed his life in the 1423 Saka against Hoshang Shah.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Durgadas Rathore?</strong> Durgadas fought the 30-Year War (1679–1709) for Maharaja Ajit Singh.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Four-Handed Deity & Sheshnag Avatar = Kallaji Rathore; Chhatri = Between Bhairon Pol & Hanuman Pol in Chittor.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Khetarpal (क्षेत्रपाल - Agricultural Guardian)</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Tallinathji (तल्लीनाथजी)</strong> (real name Gangdev Rathore, son of Viramdev of Shergarh) established his spiritual seat on **Panchmukha Hill at Gadoi (Jalore)**. He strictly prohibited cutting any green trees in designated forest tracts called **'Oran' (ओरण)**, making him Rajasthan's pioneer deity of sacred forest conservation.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (C) Devbaba?</strong> Devbaba is the veterinarian and cattle-healer deity of Nagla Jahaz (Bharatpur).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Khetarpal?</strong> Khetarpal is the general village territory protector deity.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Tallinathji = Oran / Tree conservation + Panchmukha Hill (Jalore); Mallinathji = Tilwara (Barmer) + Kunda Panth.

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which folk deity of eastern Rajasthan (Bharatpur) is revered especially by the Gurjar and Meena communities as the <strong>Supreme Physician of Cattle & Animals (पशु चिकित्सा के ज्ञाता / ग्वालों के देवता)</strong>, worshipped with offerings of <strong>Lapasi and Jhadu (लापसी एवं झाडू)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Mama Dev (मामा देव - Rain God)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Iloji (इलोजी - Bachelor God of Barmer)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Devbaba of Nagla Jahaz (देवबाबा, नगला जहाज - भरतपुर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Jhunjharji (झूंझारजी - Sikar)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Devbaba of Nagla Jahaz (देवबाबा, नगला जहाज - भरतपुर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Devbaba** has his main shrine at **Nagla Jahaz (Bharatpur)**. He possessed supernatural knowledge of veterinary herbs (*Ayurvedic animal healing*). Devotees feast seven cowherds (*Gwalas*) and offer sweet porridge (*Lapasi*) and brooms (*Jhadu*) at his semi-annual fairs held on **Bhadrapad Shukla Panchami and Chaitra Shukla Panchami**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Mama Dev?</strong> Mama Dev is the Rain God whose wooden Toran is installed outside village gates.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Iloji?</strong> Iloji is the deity of humor and fertility in Barmer.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Jhunjharji?</strong> Jhunjharji is the protector martyr deity of Imloha/Syalodara (Sikar).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Cattle Veterinarian deity = Devbaba (Nagla Jahaz, Bharatpur); Rain deity = Mama Dev (Wooden Toran); Bachelor/Fertility deity = Iloji.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> 52 Dhwajadhari</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> 52 Mahants</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> 52 Pir</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> 52 Stambha / Thambas (52 स्तम्भ या थांबे)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) 52 Stambha / Thambas (52 स्तम्भ या थांबे)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Sant Dadu Dayal (1544–1603 AD) had 152 total disciples, of whom 100 were free ascetics (*Vitaragi*) and **52 chief disciples were appointed as heads of branch monasteries**, famously designated as the **'52 Stambha' (52 स्तम्भ)** of the sect. The main seat is at **Naraina (Jaipur)**, and prayer grounds are called *Alakh Dariba*.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> *Dhwajadhari* is not a formal Dadu Panth designation.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> *Mahants* are general heads of Hindu maths.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> *Pirs* are Sufi/folk masters.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> 52 Stambha = Dadu Panth (Dadu Dayal); Meeting with Akbar in 1585 at Fatehpur Sikri = Dadu Dayal; Discourses = Dhundhari (Sadhu-bhasha).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Pathan nobleman **Sant Rajjabji** of Sanganer was riding a mare in groom attire to Amer for his wedding when he heard Dadu's verse: <em>'रज्जब तैं गजब कियौ, सिर पर बांध्यौ मोड़। आया था हरि भजन कौ, करै नरक की दौड़॥'</em>. Rajjab immediately abandoned his marriage, remained in groom clothes for life, preserved Dadu's verses in *'Dadu Dayal ri Vani'*, and authored his own spiritual masterpiece **'Sarvangi'**.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (C) Daryavji?</strong> Sant Daryavji founded the Ren (Nagaur) seat of the Ramsnehi sect.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Ramdasji?</strong> Sant Ramdasji founded the Kherapa (Jodhpur) seat of the Ramsnehi sect.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Groom dress for life + 'Sarvangi' = Sant Rajjabji (Sanganer); Naga Branch + 'Sundar Vilas' = Sant Sundardas (Dausa).

He foretold Mughal Emperor Muhammad Shah Rangeela about the catastrophic sack of Delhi by **Nadir Shah in 1739**. His prominent scholarly female disciples were **Sahjo Bai** (author of *Sahaj Prakash*) and **Daya Bai** (author of *Daya Bodh* and *Vinay Malika*).<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (C) Pipa?</strong> Sant Pipa was the Khinchi ruler of Gagron who became a tailor and disciple of Ramananda.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Dhanna?</strong> Sant Dhanna was the Jat saint of Dhuan Kalan (Tonk).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> 42 Rules + Predicted Nadir Shah's 1739 invasion + Disciples Sahjo Bai & Daya Bai = Sant Charan Das.

He is worshipped by the **Darzi (Tailor) community**. He established the pilgrim center **Beneshwar Dham** (*'Kumbh of the Tribals'*) on the Som-Mahi-Jakham Triveni. He penned 5 prophetic texts called **'Chopada'** in the local Vagadi dialect (containing 3.5 crore words and illustrations of future global events).<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (C) Ramcharanji?</strong> Sant Ramcharanji founded the Shahpura branch of the Ramsnehi sect.</li>
        </ul>

When his brother-in-law Jindraj Khichi of Nagaur raided Deval's cows, Pabuji left his wedding ceremony midway (*Chauri pheras*) and sacrificed his life in the **Battle of Dhechu (1276 AD)** to protect the cattle.<br><br>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> When Sultan Mahmud of Ghazni invaded northern Rajasthan, Gogaji (born at Dadrewa, Churu to Jewar and Bachhal) fought heroically with his 47 sons. Stunned by Gogaji's valor appearing simultaneously across the battlefield, Ghaznavi exclaimed <em>'Zahir Pir' (Manifest Deity)</em>. Goga Navami is celebrated on **Bhadrapada Krishna Navami** across North India under Khejdi trees (*गांव-गांव खेजड़ी, गांव-गांव गोगो*).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Muhammad Ghori fought Prithviraj Chauhan III in the Battles of Tarain (1191 & 1192 AD).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Akbar ruled 500 years later during the 16th century.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Chaitra Shukla Navami is Ram Navami (birth of Lord Rama).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Gogaji titled Jahir Pir by = Mahmud Ghaznavi; Tithi = Bhadrapad Krishna 9 (Goga Navami); Head shrine = Shirshmedi (Dadrewa); Body shrine = Gogamedi (Nohar).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

In the 20th century, **Maharaja Ganga Singh of Bikaner** reconstructed the present grand temple surrounding it.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (D) Sikandar Lodi?</strong> Sikandar Lodi was contemporary to Sant Jambhoji and Sant Jasnathji, granting land at Katariasar.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Gogamedi Mosque shape & 'Bismillah' = Built by Firoz Shah Tughlaq; Modern Temple façade = Maharaja Ganga Singh.

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Jamma (जम्मा) — Night-long devotional hymn vigil (रात्रि जागरण)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Rikhia (रिखिया) — Meghwal Dalit devotees of Ramdevji</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Chopada (चोपड़ा)</strong> was authored by **Sant Mavji of Beneshwar**, containing 5 prophetic volumes. The devotional poetic work authored by Baba Ramdevji is **'Chaubees Vaniyan' (चौबीस वाणियां)**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
