          <li><strong>Why not (A) Neja?</strong> *Neja* is the sacred 5-colored flag hoisted by devotees during the Ramdevra fair.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jamma?</strong> *Jamma Jagaran* is the sacred all-night musical vigil organized by devotees of Ramdevji.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Rikhia?</strong> *Rikhia* is the traditional honorific term for Meghwal community disciples of Ramdevji, led historically by his adopted sister Sant Dalibai.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Ramdevji's poem = Chaubees Vaniyan; Sant Mavji's prophecy = Chopada; Ramdevji's flag = Neja; Meghwal devotees = Rikhia.

  </div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Sursura (सुरसुरा, किशनगढ़ - Spot where Tejaji breathed his last)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Sendariya (सेंदरिया, ब्यावर/अजमेर - Snake-bite spot)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Parbatsar (परबतसर - Venue of grand cattle fair)</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> While marching to rescue Lachha Gujari's cows, Tejaji promised serpent Basak that he would return after fulfilling his duty. Upon victory, when whole body was wounded, he offered his uninjured tongue at **Sendariya (सेंदरिया)** for the snakebite. He breathed his last at **Sursura (सुरसुरा)** near Kishangarh.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Kharnal?</strong> Kharnal (Nagaur) is Tejaji's **birthplace (जन्मस्थली)** where his ancestral seat is located.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Sursura?</strong> Sursura is the place of **martyrdom (वीरगति स्थल)** where his mare Leelan brought the news of his death to his wife Pemalde.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Parbatsar?</strong> Parbatsar (Nagaur) is the site of the massive annual **Veer Teja Cattle Fair** held on Bhadrapad Shukla Dashami (*Teja Dashami*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Tejaji: Birth = Kharnal; Snake bite = Sendariya; Demise = Sursura; Cattle Fair = Parbatsar; Cow owner = Lachha Gujari.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Devnarayanji's Phad relies on the resonant Jantar; Pabuji's Phad uses Ravanhattha.</li>
        </ul>

<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    During the reign of which Marwar Maharaja did the immortal <strong>Khejarli Massacre (खेजड़ली बलिदान - 1730 AD / VS 1787)</strong> occur, where <strong>Amrita Devi Vishnoi</strong> and 363 men and women sacrificed their lives hugging Khejri trees?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Maharaja Abhay Singh (महाराजा अभय सिंह - Marwar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Maharaja Jaswant Singh I</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Maharaja Man Singh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Maharaja Takht Singh</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Maharaja Abhay Singh (महाराजा अभय सिंह - Marwar)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> In 1730 AD (Bhadrapad Shukla Dashami), royal minister Girdhar Das Bhandari under **Maharaja Abhay Singh of Jodhpur** ordered the felling of green Khejri trees in Khejarli village for royal palace lime kilns. Amrita Devi, her 3 daughters, and 363 Vishnois sacrificed their heads (*'सिर साठे रूंख रहे तो भी सस्तो जाण'*). The world's only **Tree Fair (वृक्ष मेला)** is held here on Bhadrapad Shukla Dashami.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Jaswant Singh I?</strong> Maharaja Jaswant Singh I ruled earlier (1638–1678) during the Mughal war of succession (Battle of Dharmat 1658).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Man Singh?</strong> Maharaja Man Singh (1803–1843) was the patron of the Nath Sect who built the Maha Mandir in Jodhpur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Takht Singh?</strong> Maharaja Takht Singh was the ruler of Jodhpur during the **1857 Revolt**.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Khejarli Sacrifice (1730 AD) = Ruler: Maharaja Abhay Singh; Leader: Amrita Devi; Martyrs: 363; Tithi: Bhadrapad Shukla 10 (Tree Fair).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

  </div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> 'Jai Gopinathji' (जय गोपीनाथ जी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> 'Bolo Tejaji Maharaj ki Jai'</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The Jasnathi Siddhs of Katariasar (Bikaner) perform the thrilling **Agni Nritya (अग्नि नृत्य)** atop glowing red-hot charcoal embers (*Dhoona*). They enter the fire pit chanting the ecstatic spiritual invocation **'Fateh-Fateh' (फ़तेह-फ़तेह!)** and perform agricultural actions like churning milk and grinding grain (*Matira fodna*) without sustaining any burns.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jai Gopinathji?</strong> *Gopinathji* is a Vaishnava deity of Jaipur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> *Tejaji's name* is invoked by farmers while sowing crops.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Agni Dance = Katariasar (Bikaner) + Jasnathi Siddhs + 'Fateh-Fateh!' chant; Vishnoi = 29 Rules (Jambhoji); Jasnathi = 36 Rules (Jasnathji).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">

  <div class="pyq-question">
    Match the 4 principal monastic seats (पीठें) of the <strong>Ramsnehi Sampradaya (रामस्नेही सम्प्रदाय)</strong> with their respective founder saints:<br><br><strong>Which of the following pairs is INCORRECT?</strong>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Ren Branch (रेण, मेड़ता - नागौर) — Sant Daryavji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Kherapa Branch (खेड़ापा, जोधपुर) — Sant Mavji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Sinthal Branch (सिंहथल, बीकानेर) — Sant Hariramdasji (Author of 'Nishani')</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Kherapa Branch (खेड़ापा, जोधपुर) — Sant Mavji</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Kherapa (Jodhpur)** seat of Ramsnehi Sampradaya was founded by **Sant Ramdasji (संत रामदास जी)** (disciple of Sant Hariramdasji), NOT Sant Mavji. Sant Mavji founded the **Nishkalank Sampradaya** at Beneshwar (Dungarpur).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Shahpura?</strong> Sant Ramcharanji founded the main Shahpura seat, celebrating the famous **Phool Dol Mahotsav (फूलडोल महोत्सव)** from Chaitra Krishna Pratipada to Panchami.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Ren?</strong> Sant Daryavji (born Jaitaran) founded the Ren seat in Nagaur, harmonizing 'Ra' (Ram) and 'Ma' (Muhammad).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Sinthal?</strong> Sant Hariramdasji founded the Sinthal seat in Bikaner and authored the iconic yoga-bhakti text *'Nishani'* (निशानी).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Ramsnehi 4 Seats: Shahpura = Ramcharanji; Ren = Daryavji; Sinthal = Hariramdasji ('Nishani'); Kherapa = Ramdasji.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Pabuji (पाबूजी महाराज)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Gogaji (गोगाजी चौहान)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Mehaji Manglia (मेहाजी मांगलिया)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Ramdevji (रामदेवजी तंवर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Baba Ramdevji (Tanwar Rajput, born Undu-Kashmir, Barmer) established the egalitarian Kamadiya Panth, adopted Dalit devotee Dalibai as his sister, and composed <em>'Chaubees Vaniyan'</em> (he is the only Lok Devta who was also a celebrated poet). Female disciples perform the Terah Taali dance in his honor.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Pabuji?</strong> Pabuji (Rathore of Kolumand) is revered as the God of Camels and Plagues who defended Deval Charani's cows; his Phad is recited by Nayak Bhopas using the Ravanhattha.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Gogaji?</strong> Gogaji (Chauhan of Dadrewa) fought Mahmud Ghaznavi and is worshipped as Jahir Pir across northern India under Khejdi trees on Goga Navami.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Mehaji Manglia?</strong> Mehaji Manglia (Panch Pir) had his main shrine at Bapini (Jodhpur); his horse was named *Kiran Kabra* and his fair falls on Krishna Janmashtami.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Poet Lok Devta + Kamadiya Panth = Ramdevji; God of Camels = Pabuji; Jahir Pir = Gogaji; Kiran Kabra horse = Mehaji Manglia.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Environmental Saints — Jambhoji & Vishnoi Commandments — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In 1485 AD at Samrathal Dhora (Bikaner), <strong>Sant Jambhoji (जाम्भोजी)</strong> laid down <strong>29 strict religious and ecological commandments</strong> that led to the founding of which sacred sect?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Jasnathi Sect (जसनाथी सम्प्रदाय - 36 Rules)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Vishnoi Sect (बिश्नोई सम्प्रदाय - 29 Rules)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Ramsnehi Sect (रामस्नेही सम्प्रदाय)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Nishkalank Sect (निष्कलंक सम्प्रदाय)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Vishnoi Sect (बिश्नोई सम्प्रदाय - 29 Rules)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Sant Jambhoji founded the Vishnoi Sect (<em>Bish</em> = 20 + <em>Noi</em> = 9) in 1485 AD at Samrathal Dhora. The 29 commandments strictly prohibit cutting green trees (especially Khejdi) and killing animals, culminating in the historic 1730 AD Khejarli sacrifice where Amrita Devi and 363 martyrs laid down their lives.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Jasnathi Sect?</strong> The Jasnathi sect was founded at Katariasar (Bikaner) by Sant Jasnathji with <strong>36 rules</strong>, famous for the Agni Nritya over live coals.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Ramsnehi Sect?</strong> The Ramsnehi sect was founded at Shahpura (Bhilwara) by Sant Ramcharanji, dedicated to the formless divine name Ram without idol worship.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Nishkalank Sect?</strong> The Nishkalank sect was founded at Beneshwar (Dungarpur) by Sant Mavji who authored the futuristic prophecy texts called *Chopada*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> 29 Rules & Khejdi = Vishnoi (Jambhoji); 36 Rules & Fire Dance = Jasnathi (Jasnathji); Chopada Prophecies = Nishkalank (Mavji).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Bhakti Tradition — Dadu Dayal & Disciples — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which disciple of Sant Dadu Dayal remained dressed in <strong>bridegroom's wedding attire (दूल्हे के वेश में)</strong> for his entire life after hearing Dadu's spiritual discourse, authoring the classic text <strong>'Sarvangi' (सर्वांगी)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Sant Sundardas (संत सुंदरदास - Sundar Vilas)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Sant Rajjabji (संत रज्जब जी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Sant Haridas (संत हरिदास - Niranjani Sect)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Sant Bakhnaji (संत बखना जी)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Sant Rajjabji (संत रज्जब जी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Sant Rajjabji (born Sanganer, Jaipur) was on his way to get married when he heard Dadu Dayal's sermon: <em>'रज्जब तैं गजब कियौ, सिर पर बांध्यौ मोड़। आया था हरि भजन कौं, करै नरक की दौड़॥'</em>. He immediately renounced worldly life, stayed in bridegroom attire for the rest of his life, and compiled Dadu's teachings in *Sarvangi* and *Rajjab Vani*.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Sant Sundardas?</strong> Sant Sundardas (born Dausa) was a highly educated Brahmin disciple of Dadu who founded the Naga branch of armed ascetics and composed *Sundar Vilas* and *Gyan Samudra*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Sant Haridas?</strong> Sant Haridas (originally bandit Hari Singh Sankhla of Kaprod, Didwana) was transformed into the 'Valmiki of Kalyug' and founded the Niranjani Sampradaya.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Sant Bakhnaji?</strong> Sant Bakhnaji of Naraina was another of the 52 chief disciples ('52 Pillars') who composed devotional padas in Dhundhari.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Groom's attire & Sarvangi = Rajjabji; Naga branch & Sundar Vilas = Sundardas; Valmiki of Kalyug = Haridas Niranjani.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

## 🎯 Chapter 2: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / 2nd Grade 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following is NOT included among the traditional "Panch Pirs" (पंच पीर) of Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Tejaji (तेजाजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Pabuji (पाबूजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Gogaji (गोगाजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Ramdevji (रामदेवजी)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Tejaji (तेजाजी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> The classic mnemonic verse is: <em>पाबू, हड़बू, रामदे, मांगलिया मेहा। पांचू पीर पधारज्ये, गोगाजी जेहा॥</em> (Pabuji, Harbhuji, Ramdevji, Mehaji Mangaliya, Gogaji). Tejaji is a legendary Lok Devta but is not categorized under the Panch Pirs.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC SI 2021 / Constable 2020</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which folk deity is revered in Rajasthan as the "Protector of Camels" (ऊंटों के देवता) and the deity for curing plague?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Pabuji (पाबूजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Devnarayanji (देवनारायणजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Mehaji (मेहाजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Mallinathji (मल्लीनाथजी)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Pabuji (पाबूजी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Pabuji Rathore brought camels (Sandein) to Marwar for the first time. The Raika / Rebari community considers Pabuji their patron deity. When a camel falls sick, devotees vow to recite *Pabuji ki Phad*.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / RAS 2013</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Sultan Mahmud of Ghaznavi conferred the title of "Jahir Pir" (जाहिर पीर - Living/Visible Saint) upon which folk deity?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Harbhuji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Tejaji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Gogaji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Ramdevji</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Gogaji</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Gogaji Chauhan fought heroically against Mahmud of Ghaznavi to liberate stolen cows. Impressed by his supernatural combat abilities, Ghaznavi proclaimed him *Jahir Pir*. His shrine is at Gogamedi (Hanumangarh).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / Patwari 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The grand state-level animal cattle fair in honor of Veer Tejaji is held annually at:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Asind (Bhilwara)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Kolumand (Phalodi)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Kharnal (Nagaur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Parbatsar (Didwana-Kuchaman)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Parbatsar (Didwana-Kuchaman)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Veer Tejaji was born in Kharnal, but the grand state revenue-generating cattle fair (*Veer Teja Animal Fair*) is organized at Parbatsar on Bhadrapada Shukla Dashami (Teja Dashami).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022 / 2nd Grade 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Where is the Chhatri / Samadhi of the folk deity Kallaji Rathore (चार हाथों वाले देवता - Four-Armed Deity) located?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Chittorgarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Gagron Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Mehrangarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Kumbhalgarh Fort</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Chittorgarh Fort</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> During the 3rd Saka of Chittorgarh (1567–68), Veer Kallaji carried his injured uncle Jaimal Rathore on his shoulders, wielding four swords at once against Akbar's forces. His cenotaph stands between Bhairav Pol and Hanuman Pol at Chittorgarh.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q6</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Guru Jambhoji established the Bishnoi sect in 1485 AD at Samrathal Dhora by preaching how many religious rules (नियम)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 20 rules</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 42 rules</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 36 rules</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 29 rules</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) 29 rules</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> 20 (Bees) + 9 (Noi) = 29 rules. These include environmental protection (never felling green Khejri trees), wildlife compassion (protecting blackbucks), and moral purity. (Note: Jasnathi sect has 36 rules; Charandasi sect has 42 rules).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q7</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / REET 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The famous 'Fire Dance' (अग्नि नृत्य) over live burning embers while chanting "Fateh-Fateh" is performed by the disciples of:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Ramsnehi Sect of Shahpura</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Bishnoi Sect of Mukam</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Jasnathi Sect of Katariasar (Bikaner)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Dadupanth of Naraina</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Jasnathi Sect of Katariasar (Bikaner)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Founded by Saint Jasnathji (1482–1506 AD). The Siddha performers dance barefoot on glowing embers (*Dhoona*) in divine frenzy, tossing embers and demonstrating agricultural acts (*Matira Phodna*).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q8</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2019 / College Lecturer 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following Bhakti saints is renowned as the "Kabir of Rajasthan" (राजस्थान का कबीर)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Saint Raidas (संत रैदास)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Saint Dadu Dayal (संत दादू दयाल)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Saint Mavji (संत मावजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Saint Pipa (संत पीपा)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Saint Dadu Dayal (संत दादू दयाल)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Dadu Dayal (1544–1603 AD) taught Nirguna Bhakti, social casteless unity, and non-sectarian devotion (*Nipakh / निपक्ष मार्ग*). His principal seat is Naraina (Jaipur), and his 52 prominent disciples are called *Bawan Stambha* (52 स्तंभ).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q9</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The prophetic text *Chopra* (चोपड़ा ग्रंथ), containing predictions about the future in Vagadi dialect, is associated with:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Saint Sundardas</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Saint Charandas</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Saint Mavji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Saint Haridas</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Saint Mavji</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Saint Mavji founded the *Nishkalank Sect* at Beneshwar (Dungarpur). His 5 volumes of prophecies (*Chopra*) are brought out for public viewing exclusively on Diwali.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q10</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC SI 2021 / RAS Pre 2016</span>
  </div>

    The annual 'Phool Dol Mahotsav' (फूलडोल महोत्सव) celebrated from Chaitra Krishna Pratipada to Panchami is the signature festival of:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Pranami Sect of Panna</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Ramsnehi Sect of Shahpura (Bhilwara)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Nimbarka Sect of Salemabad</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Founded by Saint Ramcharan Ji (1719–1798 AD). Followers chant "Ram" and wear pink robes (*Gulabi Bhesh*). The Phool Dol festival takes place immediately after Holi at their Ramdwara in Shahpura.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

# Chapter 3: Major Festivals, Fairs and Celebrations of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के प्रमुख त्यौहार, मेले एवं उत्सव)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Rhythm of Rajasthani Festivals (त्यौहारों का चक्र)</div>
  <em>"There is an old saying in Rajasthan: <strong>'Saat Vaar, Nau Tyohar' (सात वार, नौ त्यौहार)</strong> (In seven days of the week, Rajasthan celebrates nine festivals!). When life in the desert was tough, festivals provided color, community joy, and emotional rejuvenation. But from an RPSC examiner's perspective, this is a pure calendar test. If you master the Hindu Lunar Calendar (Chaitra to Phalguna with Krishna and Shukla Pakshas / कृष्ण पक्ष व शुक्ल पक्ष), this chapter becomes your highest-scoring area."</em>
</div>

<div class="mnemonic-box">
  <div class="mnemonic-title">🧠 Master Mnemonic: The Cycle of Festivals (त्यौहारों का आरम्भ व अंत)</div>
  <blockquote>
    <strong>"तीज त्यौहारां बावड़ी, ले डूबी गणगौर" (Teej Tyoharan Bawdi, Le Doobi Gangaur)</strong>
  </blockquote>
  <ul>
    <li>The annual cycle of festivals <strong>begins with Chhoti Teej (छोटी तीज)</strong> (Shravana month).</li>
    <li>The annual cycle of festivals <strong>ends with Gangaur (गणगौर)</strong> (Chaitra month).</li>
  </ul>
</div>

---

## 1. Major Traditional Festivals

### A. Teej (तीज) & Gangaur (गणगौर) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Repeated in Every Single Exam</span>

```
                                  TEEJ & GANGAUR CALENDAR (तीज व गणगौर)
                                                     │
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                           │                                           │
   CHHOTI TEEJ (छोटी तीज)                      BADI TEEJ (बड़ी तीज)                        GANGAUR (गणगौर)
(Shravana Shukla 3 / श्रावण शुक्ल 3)       (Bhadrapada Krishna 3 / भाद्रपद कृष्ण 3)       (Chaitra Shukla 1-3 / चैत्र शुक्ल 1-3)
         │                                           │                                           │
 • Celebrated in Jaipur (जयपुर)              • Celebrated in Bundi (बूंदी)              • Jaipur & Udaipur (जयपुर व उदयपुर)
 • Nature & Swings (झूला)                    • Kajali / Satudi / Boorhi Teej            • 18-day Puja of Isar-Parvati
 • Delicacy: Ghewar (घेवर)                   • (कजली / सातुड़ी / बूढ़ी तीज)              • Pink Gangaur: Nathdwara (Chaitra Shukla 5)
 • Gift: Sinjara (सिंजारा)                   • Grand royal procession in Bundi          • Without Isar: Jaisalmer (Chaitra Shukla 4)
```

1. **Chhoti Teej / Hariyali Teej (छोटी तीज / हरियाली तीज - Shravana Shukla Tritiya):**
   * Dedicated to Goddess **Parvati (पार्वती)** uniting with Lord Shiva (शिव). Marks the onset of the monsoon.
   * Royal procession taken out from the City Palace in **Jaipur**. Special sweet: **Ghewar (घेवर)**.
   * Day before is **Sinjara (सिंजारा)** (gifts of clothes, sweets, and henna sent to daughters/fiancées).
2. **Badi Teej / Kajali Teej / Satudi Teej (बड़ी तीज / कजली तीज / सातुड़ी तीज - Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Asked 6+ Times</span>
   * Celebrated 15 days after Chhoti Teej. Women fast and worship the sacred neem (नीम) tree.
   * World-renowned grand royal procession held in **Bundi (बूंदी)**.
3. **Gangaur (गणगौर - Chaitra Shukla Pratipada to Tritiya):**
   * The festival of 18 days celebrating marital bliss. **Isar (ईसर)** = Lord Shiva; **Gauri / Gavar (गौरी / गवर)** = Goddess Parvati.
   * Royal processions in **Jaipur and Udaipur** (Col. Tod gave an enchanting description of Udaipur's Gangaur procession).
   * **Special Regional Variations (Examiner's Favorite!):**
     * **Pink Gangaur / Chunari Gangaur (गुलाबी गणगौर / चुनड़ी गणगौर):** Celebrated on **Chaitra Shukla Panchami (चैत्र शुक्ल पंचमी)** at **Nathdwara (नाथद्वारा - Rajsamand)**.
     * **Gangaur without Isar (बिना ईसर की गवर):** Celebrated on **Chaitra Shukla Chaturthi (चैत्र शुक्ल चतुर्थी)** exclusively in **Jaisalmer (जैसलमेर)** (Bikaner's army snatched the Isar idol during a historical raid, so only Gauri is taken out in procession).
     * **Dhinga Gavar / Bentmar Gangaur (धींगा गवर / बेंतमार गणगौर):** Celebrated on **Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya (वैशाख कृष्ण तृतीया)** in **Jodhpur (जोधपुर)** (initiated by Maharana Raj Singh; women dress in fancy costumes and playfully strike men with sticks).

---

### B. Other Critical Hindu Festivals

* **Sheetlashtami / Basoda (शीतलाष्टमी / बासोड़ा - Chaitra Krishna Ashtami):**
  * Worship of Goddess **Sheetla Mata (शीतला माता)** (Shrine atop *Seel-ki-Doongri / शील की डूंगरी*, Chaksu / चाकसू, Jaipur; priest is a Kumhar / कुम्हार, vehicle is a donkey / गधा).
  * No fire is lit in kitchens; families consume cold, stale food (**Basoda / बासोड़ा**) cooked the previous evening to protect from seasonal viral illnesses.
* **Akshaya Tritiya / Akha Teej (अक्षय तृतीया / आखा तीज - Vaishakha Shukla Tritiya):**
  * The foremost *Abujh Muhurat (अबूझ सावा)* (most auspicious un-choghadiya day for mass weddings).
  * **Bikaner Foundation Day** (Rao Bika founded Bikaner on this day in 1488 AD); marked by massive kite-flying competitions in Bikaner and Jaipur. Seven food grains (*Satnaja*) and *Khichda (खींचड़ा)* are cooked.
* **Holi (होली - Phalguna Purnima / फाल्गुन पूर्णिमा) — Regional Varieties (RPSC Match Table):**
  * **Lathmar Holi (लट्ठमार होली):** *Shri Mahavirji (श्री महावीर जी - Karauli)* (women beat men with bamboo sticks).
  * **Kodamar Holi (कोड़ामार होली):** *Bhinai (भिणाय - Ajmer/Kekri)* (leather/cloth whips).
  * **Patharmar Holi (पत्थरमार होली):** *Barmer (बाड़मेर)* (stone pelting).
  * **Gobar ke Kandon ki Holi (गोबर के कण्डों की होली):** *Galiakot (गलियाकोट - Dungarpur)* (cow-dung cakes).
  * **Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली):** *Kekri (केकड़ी) & Lalsot (लालसोट - Dausa)* (walking on burning coals).
  * **Devar-Bhabhi Holi (देवर-भाभी होली):** *Beawar (ब्यावर - Ajmer)*.
  * **Illuji ki Savari (इलोजी की सवारी):** Taken out during Holi in *Barmer* (Illuji is the folk deity of romance and bachelors).

---

## 2. Prominent Fairs of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के प्रमुख मेले)

```
                                  MAJOR FAIRS OF RAJASTHAN (प्रमुख मेले)
                                                     │
         ┌───────────────────┬───────────────────────┴───────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
         │                   │                                               │                   │
    PUSHKAR FAIR      BENESHWAR FAIR                                  KARNI MATA FAIR     KAILADEVI FAIR
   (पुष्कर मेला)       (बेणेश्वर मेला)                                 (करणी माता मेला)    (कैलादेवी मेला)
 (Kartika Purnima)    (Magha Purnima)                               (Chaitra/Ashvin Nav) (Chaitra Shukla 8)
         │                   │                                               │                   │
   • Ajmer / 52 Ghats  • Dungarpur (Triveni Sangam)                  • Deshnok (Bikaner) • Karauli (Kalisil River)
   • Camel Trade       • "Vagad / Tribal Kumbh" (आदिवासियों का कुम्भ)  • "Temple of Mice"  • Languriya Songs (लांगुरिया)
   • Dipdan (दीपदान)   • Broken Shivling Worship (खण्डित शिवलिंग)     • White Mice (काबा) • Lakhi Mela (लक्खी मेला)
```

* **Pushkar Fair (पुष्कर मेला - Ajmer - Kartika Purnima / कार्तिक पूर्णिमा):** Known as the **"Rose of Rajasthan"** and the most colorful fair in India. 52 bathing ghats; huge camel and cattle trade; Brahma temple worship; sacred Dipdan (दीपदान).
* **Beneshwar Fair (बेणेश्वर मेला - Dungarpur - Magha Purnima / माघ पूर्णिमा):** Revered as the **"Kumbh of Tribals" (आदिवासियों का महाकुम्भ)** and *Vagad ka Pushkar*. Located at the Triveni Sangam of Som, Mahi, and Jakham rivers in Nawata-para / Sabla (नवाटापरा / साबला). Only place in the world where a **broken/fragmented Shivling (खण्डित शिवलिंग)** is worshipped.
* **Karni Mata Fair (करणी माता मेला - Deshnok, Bikaner / देशनोक, बीकानेर):** Celebrated twice a year during Navratris. Known as the **"Temple of Mice" (चूहों वाली देवी)**. Sacred white mice are called **Kaba (काबा)**; sighting a Kaba is considered a direct blessing.
* **Kailadevi Fair (कैलादेवी मेला - Karauli - Chaitra Shukla Ashtami / चैत्र शुक्ल अष्टमी):** Major **Lakhi Mela (लक्खी मेला)** on Trikoot Hill (त्रिकूट पर्वत) along River Kalisil (कालीसिल नदी). Devotees sing **Languriya devotional songs (लांगुरिया गीत)** and dance in front of Bohra Bhagat's Chhatri (बोहरा भगत की छतरी).
* **Sitabari Fair (सीताबाड़ी मेला - Kelwara, Baran / केलवाड़ा, बारां - Jyeshtha Amavasya / ज्येष्ठ अमावस्या):** Revered as the **"Kumbh of the Sahariya Tribe" (सहरिया जनजाति का कुम्भ)** (associated with Goddess Sita and Maharishi Valmiki).
* **Khatushyamji Fair (खाटूश्यामजी मेला - Sikar - Phalguna Shukla Ekadashi/Dwadashi):** Worship of Barbarik (बर्बरीक) as **"Sheesh ke Dani" (शीश के दानी)** and **"Haare ka Sahara" (हारे का सहारा)**.
* **Urs of Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti (ख्वाजा मोईनुद्दीन चिश्ती का उर्स - Ajmer - 1st to 6th Rajab / रजब माह):** Hoisting of ceremonial flag on **Buland Darwaza (बुलन्द दरवाजा)** by Ghori family of Bhilwara. Prasad cooked in massive cauldrons: **Badi Deg (बड़ी देग)** (4,800 kg - Akbar) and **Chhoti Deg (छोटी देग)** (2,240 kg - Jahangir).

---

| **Sheetlashtami (शीतलाष्टमी)** | Chaitra Krishna Ashtami | Chaksu / Cold food (Basoda) |
| **Akha Teej (आखा तीज)** | Vaishakha Shukla Tritiya | Bikaner Day / Abujh Muhurat / Kites |
| **Pushkar Fair (पुष्कर मेला)** | Kartika Purnima | Ajmer / Dipdan / Camel trade / 52 Ghats |
| **Beneshwar Fair (बेणेश्वर मेला)** | Magha Purnima | Dungarpur / Tribal Kumbh / Broken Shivling |
| **Kailadevi Fair (कैलादेवी मेला)** | Chaitra Shukla Ashtami | Karauli / Languriya songs / Lakhi Mela |
| **Sitabari Fair (सीताबाड़ी मेला)** | Jyeshtha Amavasya | Baran / Sahariya Kumbh |
| **Lathmar Holi (लट्ठमार होली)** | Phalguna Purnima | Shri Mahavirji (Karauli) |




## 📝 Chapter 3: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Tithi & Calendar Precision Standard</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    On which specific lunar tithi is the sacred festival of <strong>Karwa Chauth (करवा चौथ)</strong> observed by married Hindu women in Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Kartik Shukla Chaturthi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Kartik Krishna Chaturthi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Bhadrapada Krishna Chaturthi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Ashwin Krishna Chaturthi</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Kartik Krishna Chaturthi</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Karwa Chauth falls on <em>Kartik Krishna Chaturthi</em>. Married women observe a strict nirjala fast praying for the longevity and health of their husbands, breaking the fast upon sighting the moon through a sieve.<br>
        <strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> Most festivals around Diwali (Dhanteras, Roop Chaudas, Diwali) fall in the Krishna Paksha of Kartik month, NOT Shukla Paksha.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

    The famous <strong>Sitabari Fair (सीताबारी मेला)</strong>, celebrated on Jyeshtha Amavasya and considered the "Kumbh of the Sahariya Tribe" (सहरिया जनजाति का कुंभ), is organized at which location?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Kelwara (Baran)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Gogunda (Udaipur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Kushalgarh (Banswara)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Shahbad (Baran)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Kelwara (Baran)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Sitabari Fair is held at Kelwara in Baran district on Jyeshtha Amavasya. It features the Sita Kund, Laxman Kund, and Valmiki Ashram. It is the premier socio-cultural congregation of the Sahariya community.

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Comparative Festivals Analysis</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Consider the following pairs of Teej festivals and their respective celebration tithis:
    <br>1. Haryali Teej (Chhoti Teej, Jaipur) — Shravan Shukla Tritiya
    <br>2. Kajli Teej (Badi Teej / Satoodi Teej, Bundi) — Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya
    <br>3. Akshaya Tritiya (Akha Teej) — Vaishakh Shukla Tritiya
    <br>Which of the pairs given above are correctly matched?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 1 and 3 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 1, 2 and 3 (All correctly matched)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 2 and 3 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 1 and 2 only</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) 1, 2 and 3 (All correctly matched)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> All three are perfectly matched:
        <br>• Chhoti Teej (Jaipur procession) marks the arrival of monsoons on Shravan Shukla Tritiya.
        <br>• Kajli Teej of Bundi is famous for its grand royal procession on Bhadrapada Krishna Tritiya.
        <br>• Akha Teej (Vaishakh Shukla Tritiya) is the most auspicious day for child marriages (Abujh Muhurat) and marks the foundation day of Bikaner (1488 CE).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Fairs & Religious Traditions</span>

  <div class="pyq-question">
    At which sacred lake fair organized on <strong>Kartik Purnima</strong> do members of the <strong>Charan caste (चारण जाति)</strong> traditionally NOT bathe or participate due to the legendary drowning of Karni Mata's adopted son Lakhan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Pushkar Lake (Ajmer)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Jaisamand Lake (Udaipur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Kolayat Lake (Bikaner)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Nakki Lake (Mount Abu)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Kolayat Lake (Bikaner)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Kolayat Lake (Bikaner) hosts the ashram of sage Kapil Muni (founder of Sankhya philosophy). On Kartik Purnima, a massive Deepdaan fair is held with 52 ghats. Charans strictly avoid this lake because Lakhan (son of Karni Mata's sister Gulab Bai) drowned here on Kolayat fair day.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Festivals — Regional Variations of Holi in Rajasthan — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Match the unique regional varieties of <strong>Holi Celebrations</strong> with their specific locations in Rajasthan:<br><br><strong>Which of the following pairings is INCORRECT?</strong>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Lathmar Holi (लट्ठमार होली) — Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Kodamar Holi (कोड़ामार होली) — Bhinai (Ajmer / Kekri)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Patthar-mar Holi (पत्थरमार होली) — Barmer (बाड़मेर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली) — Jodhpur (जोधपुर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली) — Jodhpur (जोधपुर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Angaron ki Holi (अंगारों की होली)** is celebrated in **Kekri (Ajmer)** and **Lalsot (Dausa)** (NOT Jodhpur), where revellers walk over glowing red-hot coals. Jodhpur is famous for *Gair Holi* and *Dhinga Gavar*.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Lathmar Holi?</strong> *Lathmar Holi* is celebrated at **Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Kodamar Holi?</strong> *Kodamar Holi* is celebrated at **Bhinai (Kekri/Ajmer)**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Patthar-mar Holi?</strong> *Patthar-mar Holi* is celebrated in **Barmer** (along with the *Iloji procession*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Lathmar = Mahavirji; Kodamar = Bhinai; Patthar-mar = Barmer; Angaron ki = Kekri & Lalsot; Devar-Bhabhi = Beawar; Doli-Chhint = Pushkar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Festivals — Dhinga Gavar / Betmar Mela of Jodhpur — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & College Lecturer</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    On <strong>Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya</strong>, the unique carnival of <strong>Dhinga Gavar (धींगा गवर / बेंतमार मेला)</strong> is celebrated with women roaming the streets at night in various fancy disguises and playfully striking men with decorated wooden canes (*Bênt*) in which historic city of Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Started under Maharana Raj Singh of Mewar & adopted by Marwar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Jaipur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Bikaner</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Kota</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Started under Maharana Raj Singh of Mewar & adopted by Marwar)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Dhinga Gavar (धींगा गवर)** festival on **Vaishakh Krishna 3** is celebrated with immense fervor in **Jodhpur and Udaipur**. Started initially in Udaipur by Maharana Raj Singh I for his queen, it became a massive urban spectacle in Jodhpur where women take out midnight processions (*Teejnis*) wielding wooden canes (*Bênt*) to mock-strike men, bestowing good marriage luck.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Jaipur?</strong> Jaipur is famous for the Chhoti Teej and Gangaur royal processions.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Bikaner?</strong> Bikaner is famous for *Dolchi-mar Holi* (water buckets of Harsh & Vyas communities).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Kota?</strong> Kota is famous for its royal *Dussehra Fair*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Dhinga Gavar / Betmar Mela (Vaishakh Krishna 3) = Jodhpur & Udaipur (Only fast observed by widows too!).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">

Women observe rigorous fast, prepare traditional *Sattu* sweets, worship the Neem tree, and witness the centuries-old royal chariot procession carrying Goddess Teej atop caparisoned elephants.<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (B) Shravan Shukla 3?</strong> Shravan Shukla Tritiya is **Chhoti Teej (छोटी तीज)** celebrated in **Jaipur**, marking the onset of the monsoon and the festival calendar (*'तीज त्योहारां बावड़ी, ले डूबी गणगौर'*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Bhadrapad Shukla 3?</strong> Bhadrapad Shukla Tritiya is **Haritalika Teej** when women fast to commemorate Goddess Parvati's penance to win Lord Shiva.</li>

        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Chhoti Teej = Jaipur (Shravan Shukla 3); Kajali / Badi Teej = Bundi (Bhadrapad Krishna 3); Haritalika Teej = Bhadrapad Shukla 3; Gangaur = Chaitra Shukla 3.

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Sitabari Fair (Kelwara, Baran)** is held on **Jyeshtha Amavasya** in the Hadoti region. Revered as the **'Kumbh of the Sahariya Tribe'**, it marks the sacred site where Maharishi Valmiki's ashram was located and where Mata Sita gave birth to Luv and Kush. Sahariyas perform sacred immersion of ancestor ashes in the cold natural springs (*Sita Kund, Lakshman Kund, Suraj Kund*).<br><br>

          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Jambhoji fairs are held at Mukam (Talwa, Nokha) and Khejarli.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kumbh of Sahariyas = Sitabari (Kelwara, Baran on Jyeshtha Amavasya); Kumbh of Vagad/Tribals = Beneshwar (Dungarpur on Magh Purnima).

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Magha Purnima (माघ पूर्णिमा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Kartik Purnima (कार्तिक पूर्णिमा - Full moon of Kartik)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Vaishakha Purnima (बुद्ध पूर्णिमा)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The Pushkar Fair begins on **Kartik Shukla Ekadashi** and reaches its grand spiritual climax on **Kartik Purnima (कार्तिक पूर्णिमा)**. Pilgrims take holy dips across the **52 Ghats** (including *Brahma Ghat* and *Varaha Ghat*) of the crescent-shaped sacred Pushkar Lake and offer lamps (*Deepdan*). The only ancient world temple of Lord Brahma with Savitri temple on Ratnagiri hill is situated here.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>

          <li><strong>Why not (A) Sharad Purnima?</strong> Sharad Purnima (Ashvin Purnima) is famous for the Raas Lila and Moon rays bathing *Kheer*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Magha Purnima?</strong> Magha Purnima is the culmination tithi of the **Beneshwar Tribal Fair** in Dungarpur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Vaishakha Purnima?</strong> Vaishakha Purnima is Buddha Purnima / Matrikundiya Fair.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Pushkar Fair & Kolayat Fair = Kartik Purnima; Beneshwar Fair = Magha Purnima; Sitabari Fair = Jyeshtha Amavasya.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Jaipur (जयपुर - Traditional Royal Gangaur on Chaitra Shukla 3)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Jodhpur (जोधपुर - Dhinga Gavar on Vaishakha Krishna 3)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Jaisalmer (जैसलमेर - Gangaur without Isar on Chaitra Shukla 4)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">

    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> In **Jaisalmer**, the Gangaur procession features only Goddess Gauri (Gavar) without Lord Isar (Shiva) on **Chaitra Shukla Chaturthi**. Historically, the Bikaner army snatched the idol of Isar during the festival, so only Gavar has been worshipped since.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Nathdwara?</strong> Nathdwara is famous for **Pink Gangaur (Gulabi Gangaur)** celebrated on **Chaitra Shukla Panchami**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Jaipur?</strong> Jaipur celebrates the standard state royal Gangaur procession with both Isar and Gauri on **Chaitra Shukla Tritiya**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jodhpur?</strong> Jodhpur is celebrated for **Dhinga Gavar / Bentmar Gangaur** started by Maharaja Ajit Singh on **Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya**.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Gangaur without Isar = Jaisalmer (Chaitra Shukla 4); Pink Gangaur = Nathdwara (Chaitra Shukla 5); Dhinga Gavar = Jodhpur (Vaishakh Krishna 3); Royal Gangaur = Jaipur (Chaitra Shukla 3).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

          <li><strong>Why not (D) Lathmar Holi?</strong> Lathmar Holi is played at **Shri Mahavirji (Karauli)** where women strike men with decorated wooden sticks.</li>

        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Lathmar = Mahavirji; Kodamar = Bhinai; Patharmar = Barmer; Gobar Kande = Galiakot (Dungarpur); Angaron ki Holi = Kekri & Lalsot; Devar-Bhabhi = Beawar.

          <li><strong>Why not (A) Jaipur?</strong> Jaipur is world-renowned for the royal procession of **Chhoti Teej / Hariyali Teej** on Shravana Shukla Tritiya (where Ghewar is the signature sweet).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jodhpur?</strong> Jodhpur is celebrated for **Dhinga Gavar / Bentmar Gangaur** held on Vaishakha Krishna Tritiya.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Nathdwara?</strong> Nathdwara in Rajsamand is famous for celebrating **Pink Gangaur (Gulabi Gangaur)** on Chaitra Shukla Panchami.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Chhoti Teej = Shravan Shukla 3 (Jaipur); Badi/Kajali Teej = Bhadrapad Krishna 3 (Bundi); Gangaur = Chaitra Shukla 3 (Jaipur/Udaipur).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Fairs & Tribal Culture — Beneshwar Dham — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & Assistant Professor</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>Beneshwar Fair (बेणेश्वर मेला)</strong>, hailed as the <em>'Kumbh of the Tribals' (आदिवासियों का महाकुम्भ)</em> and <em>'Pushkar of Vagad'</em>, is held on <strong>Magha Purnima (माघ पूर्णिमा)</strong> at the confluence of which three rivers?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Chambal, Banas, and Seep (चम्बल, बनास एवं सीप - Rameshwaram)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Som, Mahi, and Jakham (सोम, माही एवं जाखम - Dungarpur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Banas, Berach, and Menal (बनास, बेड़च एवं मेनाल - Bigod, Bhilwara)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Ahu, Kali Sindh, and Parwan (आहू, कालीसिंध एवं परवन)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Som, Mahi, and Jakham (सोम, माही एवं जाखम - Dungarpur)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Beneshwar Dham at Nawata-para (Sabla, Dungarpur) is situated at the sacred **Triveni Sangam of Som, Mahi, and Jakham rivers**. Established by Sant Mavji, it is the only place in the world where a **broken/fragmented Shivling (खण्डित शिवलिंग)** is worshipped during the Magha Purnima fair.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Chambal, Banas, Seep?</strong> Chambal, Banas, and Seep form the sacred confluence at **Rameshwaram Ghat (Sawai Madhopur)**, revered as the pilgrimage center for Meena devotees.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Banas, Berach, Menal?</strong> These three rivers meet at **Bigod (Bhilwara)** forming the Bigod Triveni Sangam.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Ahu & Kali Sindh?</strong> Ahu and Kali Sindh meet at Gagron Fort in Jhalawar (Jal Durg).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Beneshwar Triveni = Som + Mahi + Jakham (Dungarpur); Rameshwaram Triveni = Chambal + Banas + Seep; Bigod Triveni = Banas + Berach + Menal.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

## 🎯 Chapter 3: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Bundi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Jaipur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Jodhpur</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Shravan Krishna Tritiya</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Vaishakh Shukla Tritiya</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Bhadrapada Shukla Tritiya</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chaitra Shukla Tritiya</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Chaitra Shukla Tritiya</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Gangaur worship starts the day after Holika Dahan (Chaitra Krishna Pratipada) and continues for 18 days, culminating on Chaitra Shukla Tritiya with elaborate processions of Gauri (Isar-Gauri). (Note: Dhinga Gangaur is celebrated in Udaipur/Jodhpur on Vaishakh Krishna Tritiya!).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Sheetlashtami' (शीतलाष्टमी / बासोड़ा) is celebrated on which date of the Hindu calendar?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Vaishakh Krishna Ashtami</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Chaitra Krishna Ashtami</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Ashwin Shukla Ashtami</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chaitra Shukla Ashtami</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Chaitra Krishna Ashtami</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Celebrated on Chaitra Krishna Ashtami. Stale food (*Basoda*) prepared on Saptami night is offered to Sheetla Mata (Seel ki Dungri, Chaksu, Jaipur) to ward off pox and fevers. No fresh fires are lit in households on this day.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Kali Sindh, Ahu and Parwan</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Chambal, Banas and Morel</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Som, Mahi and Jakham</div>
  </div>

    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Located at Sabla/Nawapara in Dungarpur at the Triveni Sangam of Som, Mahi, and Jakham. Devotees venerate the fractured Shiva Lingam (*Khandit Shivling*) and immerse mortal remains (*Asthi Visarjan*).
      </div>

  </details>
</div>

        <strong>Explanation:</strong> The fair spans Kartik Shukla Ekadashi to Kartik Purnima (October/November), featuring sacred lake bathing, Maha Aarti at Brahma temple, deepdan, and the world's largest camel and horse trading congregation.

# Chapter 4: Costumes and Ornaments of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के वस्त्र एवं आभूषण)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Language of Turbans & Jewels (पगड़ी और आभूषणों की भाषा)</div>
  <em>"In ancient Rajasthan, you didn’t need to ask a man his caste, region, or marital status—you only had to look at his head! The way a turban (<strong>Pagri / Safa - पगड़ी / साफा</strong>) was tied revealed whether he came from the hills of Mewar (compact flat pagri) or the deserts of Marwar (tall flowing safa). A saffron turban meant he was marching to war (केसरिया बाणा); a mothara turban meant he was off to a wedding; and a lahariya turban meant the monsoon rains had arrived. Similarly, traditional Rajasthani jewelry was an intricate science of acupressure and social status."</em>
</div>

---

## 1. Traditional Male & Female Costumes (पारंपरिक वेशभूषा)

### A. Male Costumes (पुरुष वेशभूषा)
* **Turbans (Pagri / Safa / Paag - पगड़ी / साफा / पाग / फेंटा):**
  * **Mewari Pagri (मेवाड़ी पगड़ी):** Flat, compact, tied low on the head (e.g., *Chundawatshahi / चूण्डावतशाही, Amarshahi / अमरशाही, Shambhushahi / शंभूशाही*).
  * **Marwari Safa (मारवाड़ी साफा):** High, broad, long tail (*Chhogadar / छोगों वाला*).
  * **Jaipuri Pagri (जयपुरी पगड़ी):** *Khirkiya Pagri (खिड़कीदार पगड़ी), Man-shahi (मानशाही)*.
* **Occasion-Specific Turbans (RPSC Match Table):**
  * **Lahariya Pagri (लहरिया पगड़ी):** Shravana / Monsoon season (सावन माह).
  * **Mothara Pagri (मोठड़ा पगड़ी):** Wedding ceremonies (विवाह अवसर - criss-cross check dyes).
  * **Madil Pagri (मदील पगड़ी):** Dussehra / विजयादशमी (interwoven gold and silver *Zari* threads).
  * **Phaganiya Pagri (फागणिया पगड़ी):** Holi / Spring festival (होली व फाल्गुन).
  * **Kasumal (कसूम्बल - Bright Red):** Auspicious festive gatherings.
  * **Khakha / White (खाखा / सफेद):** Mourning attire (शोक के समय).
* **Upper & Lower Body:** **Angarakhi (अंगरखी)** (inner coat, also called *Bugatari / बुगतरी, Mirzai / मिरजई, Bandi / बांडी*), **Choga (चोगा)** (embroidered royal robe over Angarakhi), **Jama (जामा)** (flared gown), **Dhoti / Pajama (धोती / पाजामा)**.

### B. Female Costumes (महिला वेशभूषा)
* **Traditional Ensemble:** **Ghaghra (घाघरा)** (flared skirt with 80 kalis/panels), **Kanchli (कांचली)** (back-tied fitted inner bodice), **Kurti (कुर्ती)** (sleeveless tunic over Kanchli), and **Odhani (ओढ़नी)** (drape / veil).
* **Types of Odhani / Veils (ओढ़नी के प्रकार):**
  * **Pila Pomcha (पीला पोमचा - Yellow):** Lotus motif drape worn by a mother on the birth of a **baby boy (पुत्र जन्म)**.
  * **Gulabi Pomcha (गुलाबी पोमचा - Pink):** Worn on the birth of a **baby girl (पुत्री जन्म)**.
  * **Tara-Bhaat ki Odhani (तारा-भांत की ओढ़नी) / Kareel ki Odhani / Jawar-Bhaat (ज्वार-भांत):** Celebrated traditional drapes worn by tribal (Adivasi / आदिवासी) women.

---

## 2. Comprehensive Head-to-Toe Ornaments Decoder (सिर से पैर तक आभूषण) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Direct Exam Match Questions</span>

```
                         HEAD-TO-TOE ORNAMENTS OF RAJASTHAN (सिर से पैर के आभूषण)
                                                    │
 ┌─────────────────┬─────────────────┬──────────────┴──────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────┐
 │                 │                 │                             │                 │                 │
HEAD & FOREHEAD   NOSE & TEETH      EARS                      NECK & CHEST      ARMS & WRISTS     FEET & TOES
(सिर व मस्तक)     (नाक व दांत)      (कान)                        (गला व वक्ष)      (बाजू व कलाई)     (पैर व उंगलियां)
 │                 │                 │                             │                 │                 │
• Rakhdi (रखड़ी)  • Chop (चोप - Nose) • Oganiya (ओगण्या)           • Timaniya        • Gokhru (गोखरू)  • Ramjhol (रमझोल)
• Borla (बोरला)   • Nath (नथ)        • Peepal-Patra (पीपल-पत्रा)   • Aad (आड़)       • Bangadi (बंगड़ी)• Nevri (नेवरी)
• Sheeshphool     • Besar (बेसर)     • Jhela (झेला)                • Champakali      • Nogri (नौगरी)   • Bichhiya (बिछिया)
• Memand (मेहन्द) • Chhup (चूंपा-Teeth)• Surliya (सुरलिया)          • Hansli (हंसली)  • Hathphool       • Payjeb (पायजेब)
• Tiddi-Balko     • Rakhankha (रखन)  • Toti (टोटी)                 • Mandliya        • Damna (दामणा)   • Hirnamain (हिरनामैन)
```

<div class="term-box">
  <div class="term-title">📖 Complete Head-to-Toe Identification Guide (आभूषण शब्दावली)</div>
  <table>
    <tr>
      <th>Body Part (अंग)</th>
      <th>Traditional Ornaments (पारंपरिक आभूषण)</th>
      <th>Key Identifying Features (विशेष पहचान)</th>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Head & Forehead (सिर व मस्तक)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Rakhdi (रखड़ी), Borla (बोरला), Sheeshphool (शीशफूल), Tikka (टीका), Memand (मेमंद), Damini (दामिनी), Tiddi-Balko (टीडी-भलको), Sankali (सांकली)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Borla</em> (spherical bell on crown), <em>Memand</em> (forehead crest), <em>Tiddi-Balko</em> (hanging along hairline).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Nose (नाक)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Nath (नथ), Long (लौंग), Besar (बेसर), Chop (चोप), Chuni (चुन्नी), Fini (फीणी), Bhanwariya (भंवरिया)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Besar</em> (featured in Bani-Thani painting), <em>Chop</em> (golden clove in nose wing).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Teeth (दांत)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Chhup / Chumpa (चूंपा), Rakhankha (रखन)</strong></td>
      <td>Gold/silver pins or plates embedded inside front incisors.</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Ears (कान)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Jhumka (झुमका), Kundal (कुण्डल), Bali (बाली), Jhela (झेला), Oganiya (ओगण्या), Toti (टोटी), Karnaphool (कर्णफूल), Peepal-Patra (पीपल-पत्रा), Surliya (सुरलिया)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Oganiya & Peepal-Patra</em> (leaf-shaped clips on top ear cartilage), <em>Toti</em> (round plug).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Neck & Chest (गला व छाती)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Timaniya (तिमणिया), Aad (आड़), Hansli (हंसली), Thussi (ठुस्सी), Tulsi (तुलसी), Kanthi (कंठी), Champakali (चम्पाकली), Mandliya (मांदलिया), Galpatta (गलपट्टा), Har (हार)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Aad</em> (heavy rectangular royal Rajput necklace), <em>Timaniya</em> (triangular plate), <em>Champakali</em> (jasmine buds).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Arms & Wrists (बाजू व कलाई)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Bhujbandh (भुजबन्ध), Kada (कड़ा), Bangadi (बंगड़ी), Hathphool (हथफूल), Nogri (नौगरी), Gokhru (गोखरू), Poonchi (पूंछी), Chudi (चूड़ी), Anant (अनंत)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Bangadi</em> (gold bangles with round beads), <em>Gokhru</em> (spiked bangle), <em>Nogri</em> (pearl band).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Fingers & Thumbs (उंगलियां व अंगूठा)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Damna (दामणा), Arsi (आरसी), Bitholi (बींठी), Mundri (मुंदरी)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Damna</em> (ring joining two fingers), <em>Arsi</em> (large thumb ring fitted with a mirror).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Waist (कमर)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Kandora (कंदोरा), Kardhani (करधनी), Tagdi (तगड़ी), Kanakti (कणकती), Satka (सटका), Zanjeer (जंजीर)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Tagdi / Kandora</em> (jingling silver/gold chain belts).</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td><strong>Ankles & Feet (पैर व उंगलियां)</strong></td>
      <td><strong>Payal (पायल), Payjeb (पायजेब), Kada (कड़ा), Nupur (नूपुर), Jhanjhar (झांझर), Ramjhol (रमझोल), Todar (तोड़ा), Nevri (नेवरी), Bichhiya (बिछिया), Angoota (अंगूठा)</strong></td>
      <td><em>Nevri</em> (worn with pajeb), <em>Ramjhol</em> (heavy bells on cloth), <em>Bichhiya</em> (married toe ring).</td>
    </tr>
  </table>
</div>

<div class="trap-box">
  <div class="trap-title">⚠️ Examiner Trap: The #1 Most Confused Question in RPSC (चोप बनाम चूंपा)</div>
  <blockquote>
    <strong>Chop (चोप)</strong> is worn in the <strong>NOSE (नाक)</strong>!
    <br><strong>Chhup / Chumpa (चूंपा)</strong> is worn in the <strong>TEETH (दांत)</strong>!
  </blockquote>
  <em>(Examiners intentionally put both in options to trap hurried candidates!)</em>
</div>

---

## ⚡ Chapter 4: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

| Ornament / Dress | Location / Body Part | Critical Exam Key |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Borla / Memand (बोरला / मेमंद)** | Head / Forehead (सिर व मस्तक) | Round spherical ornament / Forehead line |
| **Chop vs Chhup (चोप बनाम चूंपा)** | Chop = Nose \| Chhup = Teeth | High-frequency elimination trap |
| **Oganiya / Peepal-Patra (ओगण्या / पीपल-पत्रा)**| Ears (कान) | Leaf-shaped top helix ornaments |
| **Aad / Timaniya (आड़ / तिमणिया)** | Neck (गला) | Royal rectangular necklace / Triangular piece |
| **Gokhru / Bangadi (गोखरू / बंगड़ी)** | Wrist / Forearm (कलाई) | Spiked gold bangle & beaded bracelet |
| **Damna (दामणा)** | Fingers (उंगलियां) | Single ornament bridging two fingers |
| **Arsi (आरसी)** | Thumb (अंगूठा) | Thumb ring with small mirror for self-viewing |
| **Tagdi / Kandora (तगड़ी / कंदोरा)** | Waist (कमर) | Jingling waist chain |
| **Ramjhol / Nevri (रमझोल / नेवरी)** | Ankle / Feet (पैर) | Jingling ankle band |
| **Pila Pomcha (पीला पोमचा)** | Veil (ओढ़नी) | Yellow drape on birth of a baby boy |
