<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Textiles & Traditional Attire</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The traditional textile <strong>"Pabla / Pompcha" (पोमचा)</strong> with a yellow background (Peela) is presented to a woman by her maternal family (Pihar) on which occasion?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Upon the birth of a male child (Son)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> During Teej festival celebrations</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Upon the birth of a female child (Daughter)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Upon marriage engagement (Sagai)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Upon the birth of a male child (Son)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> <em>Peela Pompcha</em> (yellow) is gifted on the birth of a son during the Jalkwa / Kua Pujan ceremony. <em>Gulabi Pompcha</em> (pink) is gifted on the birth of a daughter.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Teeth & Ear Ornaments Special</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    What is the key difference between the traditional teeth ornaments <strong>"Chump" (चूँप)</strong> and <strong>"Rakhn" (रखन)</strong> in Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Chump is worn in the ear, while Rakhn is worn in the nose.</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Chump is worn by men only, while Rakhn is worn by unmarried girls.</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Chump is made of ivory, while Rakhn is made of copper.</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chump is a gold pin studded into a tooth, while Rakhn is a gold/silver cap/plate covering the tooth.</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Chump is a gold pin studded into a tooth, while Rakhn is a gold/silver cap/plate covering the tooth.</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong>
        <br>• <strong>चूँप (Chump):</strong> A tiny gold pin/nail embedded by drilling a small pit in the front upper teeth.
        <br>• <strong>रखन (Rakhn):</strong> A gold or silver plate or cap fitted over the teeth.
        <br><strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> Do not confuse <em>चूँप (Chump - Tooth)</em> with <em>चोंप (Chomp - Nose)</em>!
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Tribal Odhanis & Motif Design</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The famous <strong>"Tara Bhaat ki Odhani" (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी)</strong> with star motifs on a reddish-brown ground is the quintessential traditional veil worn by women of which community?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Bishnoi women</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Maheshwari women</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Adivasi / Tribal women (आदिवासी महिलाएं)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Rajput royal women</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Adivasi / Tribal women (आदिवासी महिलाएं)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Traditional tribal women odhanis include <em>Tara Bhaat</em> (star print), <em>Jwar Bhaat</em> (jowar grain print), <em>Ker Bhaat</em>, and <em>Lehar Bhaat</em>. <em>Kachhaboo</em> (घाघरा) is the traditional skirt worn by Bhil women.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Costumes — Male Body Garments (Angarakhi Varieties) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    What are <strong>'Bugatari', 'Mirzai', 'Tanzeeb', 'Gadar', and 'Dootai'</strong> in traditional Rajasthani attire?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Varieties of Rajputi daggers and shields</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Types of silk turbans worn in Marwar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Regional synonyms and seasonal tailored varieties of the traditional male upper-body vest / shirt called 'Angarakhi' (अंगरखी के विभिन्न प्रकार)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Traditional designs of silver chokers</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Regional synonyms and seasonal tailored varieties of the traditional male upper-body vest / shirt called 'Angarakhi' (अंगरखी के विभिन्न प्रकार)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Angarakhi (अंगरखी)** (a close-fitting collarless shirt tied with cords across the chest) is known in various regions as **Bugatari (बुगतरी), Mirzai (मिर्जई), Tanzeeb (तनजीब), Gadar, Dootai, and Kasan**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Daggers include *Katar*, *Chhura*, and *Khanjar*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Turbans include *Amarshahi, Jaswantshahi, Vijayashahi*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Chokers include *Thussi, Timaniya, Hansli*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Bugatari, Mirzai, Tanzeeb, Gadar, Dootai = Types of Angarakhi (अंगरखी); Choga = Overcoat worn over Angarakhi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Ornaments — Head & Forehead (Tidi-Bhalako & Memand) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following specific jewelry items is an authentic <strong>Forehead / Head ornament (माथा / सिर का आभूषण)</strong> worn by married women in Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Oganiya and Pippalpatra (Ears)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Tagdi and Kardhani (Waist)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Gokhru and Pachheli (Wrists)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Tidi-Bhalako (टीडी-भलको) and Memand (मेमंद)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Tidi-Bhalako (टीडी-भलको) and Memand (मेमंद)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Tidi-Bhalako (टीडी-भलको)</strong> (a delicate pendant hanging right beneath the hairline in the center of the forehead) and <strong>Memand (मेमंद)</strong> (a central hair-parting jewel) are prominent **Head & Forehead ornaments**, alongside *Rakhdi, Borla, Shishphool, Bindi, Tikka, and Sankli*.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> *Oganiya* and *Pippalpatra* are worn in the upper **Ears (कान)**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> *Tagdi* and *Kardhani* are worn around the **Waist (कमर)**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> *Gokhru* and *Pachheli* are worn on the **Wrists / Hands (कलाई/हाथ)**.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Tidi-Bhalako, Memand, Rakhdi, Shishphool = Head/Forehead; Oganiya, Surliya, Pippalpatra = Ears; Gokhru, Bangdi = Wrist; Tagdi = Waist.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Traditional Costumes — Rajputi Turbans (Amarshahi, Vijayashahi) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    What are <strong>'Amarshahi', 'Vijayashahi', 'Jaswantshahi', 'Mansinghshahi', and 'Bhimshahi'</strong> in Rajasthani cultural heritage?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Traditional varieties of sword tempering techniques</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Traditional folk theatre scripts of Mewar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Forms of silver cattle ornaments</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Distinct royal stylistic modes of tying Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ी / साफ़ा बांधने की विभिन्न राजपूती शैलियां)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Distinct royal stylistic modes of tying Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ी / साफ़ा बांधने की विभिन्न राजपूती शैलियां)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> These are famous regional styles of **Pagris / Turbans (पगड़ी / साफे की शैलियां)** named after respective rulers: **Amarshahi & Chundawatshahi** (Mewar), **Vijayashahi, Jaswantshahi & Mansinghshahi** (Jodhpur/Marwar), **Bhimshahi & Madhoshahi** (Jaipur/Kota).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Famous sword varieties include *Sirohi Talwar* and *Tegh*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Folk theatre forms are *Khyal*, *Tamasha*, and *Rammat*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Cattle ornaments include *Gorband* (camel neck) and *Ghungroo*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Amarshahi, Vijayashahi, Jaswantshahi, Mansinghshahi = Turban styles (पगड़ी शैलियां); Pachranga Safa = Jodhpur; Khunchewali Pagri = Mewar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Traditional Costumes — Female Textiles (Mothda, Pomcha, Lahariya) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In traditional Rajasthani textiles, what is the tie-dye pattern called when the diagonal striped lines of <strong>Lahariya (लहरिया)</strong> intersect and cross each other in a <strong>checkered / grid fashion</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Mothda (मोठड़ा - Cross-striped checkered tie-dye)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Pomcha (पोमचा - Lotus print veil)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Dhabla (धाबला - Woolen rural skirt)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Rejha (रेझा - Coarse tribal cloth)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Mothda (मोठड़ा - Cross-striped checkered tie-dye)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> When the diagonal wavy lines of *Lahariya* intersect each other at right angles to form diamond/checkered grids, the textile is called **Mothda (मोठड़ा)** (celebrated in Jodhpur turbans and odhnis). **Pomcha** is a traditional lotus-roundel veil (yellow Pomcha on birth of a baby boy; pink Pomcha on birth of a baby girl).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Pomcha?</strong> *Pomcha* is a yellow/pink central-circle veil gifted by maternal grandparents (*Peehar*) upon childbirth.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Dhabla?</strong> *Dhabla* is a thick handspun woolen skirt worn by rural women in Marwar/Bikaner.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Rejha?</strong> *Rejha* is the thick white cotton costume worn by Sahariya women.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Diagonal parallel waves = Lahariya; Diagonal crossing lines (checkered) = Mothda; Son's birth veil = Yellow Pomcha; Daughter's birth veil = Pink Pomcha.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Ornaments — Feet & Ankle Jewels (Anwala & Nevari) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & 2nd Grade</span>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Timaniya and Hansli</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Anwala (आंवला) and Nevari (नेवरी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Gokhru and Bangdi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Oganiya and Pippalpatra</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Anwala (आंवला) and Nevari (नेवरी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Anwala (आंवला)</strong> is a thick ribbed silver anklet. <strong>Nevari (नेवरी)</strong> is an ornate silver ring worn directly over the Anwala around the ankles, along with *Ramjhol* (musical tinkling bell-pad) and *Hirnamain*.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Timaniya & Hansli?</strong> Both are **Neck (गला)** ornaments.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Gokhru & Bangdi?</strong> Both are **Wrist / Hand (कलाई/हाथ)** ornaments.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Oganiya & Pippalpatra?</strong> Both are **Ear (कान)** cartilage ornaments.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Anwala & Nevari = Feet/Ankles; Timaniya & Hansli = Neck; Gokhru & Bangdi = Wrist; Oganiya & Surliya = Ear; Chump = Teeth; Chop = Nose.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Ornaments — Head to Toe (Teeth Ornaments: Chump vs Chop) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In Rajasthani culture, what is the critical anatomical difference between the ornaments <strong>'Chump' (चूंम्प)</strong> and <strong>'Chop' (चोप)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Chump is worn on the neck; Chop is worn on the ankle</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Chump is worn on the forehead; Chop is worn on the wrist</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Chump (चूंम्प) is a gold pin studded inside the teeth; Chop (चोप) is an ornament worn on the nose</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Both are identical types of Rajasthani silver earrings</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Chump (चूंम्प) is a gold pin studded inside the teeth; Chop (चोप) is an ornament worn on the nose</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Chump (चूंम्प)</strong> and <strong>Rakkan (रखन)</strong> are ornaments of the **Teeth (दांत)** (Chump is a gold stud embedded in the tooth; Rakkan is a gold/silver cap). In contrast, <strong>Chop (चोप)</strong>, **Long**, **Bari**, and **Besari** are ornaments worn on the **Nose (नाक)**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Neck ornaments include *Timaniya*, *Hansli*, and *Madaliya*; ankle ornaments include *Nevari* and *Anwala*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Forehead ornaments include *Memand* and *Borla*; wrist ornaments include *Gokhru* and *Bangdi*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Ear ornaments include *Oganiya*, *Pippalpatra*, and *Surliya*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Chump (चूंम्प) & Rakkan = Teeth (दांत); Chop (चोप), Besari & Bari = Nose (नाक); Memand = Forehead (माथा); Oganiya = Ear (कान).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Ornaments — Upper Ear & Finger Jewels (Oganiya & Damna) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & SI</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following pairs of traditional Rajasthani ornaments is correctly matched with the respective body parts on which they are worn?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Bari — Waist & Kandhora — Ear</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Memand — Ankle & Timaniya — Nose</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Gokhru — Forehead & Nevari — Neck</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Oganiya (ओगण्या) — Ear cartilage (कान के ऊपरी हिस्से में) & Damna (दामणा) — Fingers of the hand (हाथ की अंगुलियों में)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Oganiya (ओगण्या) — Ear cartilage (कान के ऊपरी हिस्से में) & Damna (दामणा) — Fingers of the hand (हाथ की अंगुलियों में)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Oganiya (ओगण्या)</strong> (along with *Pippalpatra* and *Surliya*) is worn on the upper ear rim/cartilage. <strong>Damna (दामणा)</strong> is a double-ring ornament linking two fingers of the hand together simultaneously.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> *Bari* is worn on the nose; *Kandhora* is worn around the waist.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> *Memand* is worn on the forehead hair; *Timaniya* is a triangular necklace on the neck.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> *Gokhru* is worn on the wrist; *Nevari* is worn around the ankles.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Oganiya & Pippalpatra = Ear; Damna = Two fingers; Gokhru = Wrist; Nevari & Anwala = Feet/Ankle; Kandhora & Tagdi = Waist.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Traditional Headgear — Turbans of Rajasthan — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Match the princely royal turban (पगड़ी / साफा) styles of Rajasthan with their respective royal court kingdoms:<br><br><strong>Which of the following pairs is INCORRECTLY matched?</strong>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Amarshahi Turban (अमरशाही पगड़ी) — Mewar (मेवाड़)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Vijayashahi & Mansinghshahi (विजयशाही व मानसिंहशाही साफा) — Jodhpur / Marwar (जोधपुर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Bhimshahi Turban (भीमशाही पगड़ी) — Mewar (मेवाड़)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Chundawatshahi Turban (चूंडावतशाही पगड़ी) — Bikaner (बीकानेर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Chundawatshahi Turban (चूंडावतशाही पगड़ी) — Bikaner (बीकानेर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The <strong>Chundawatshahi Turban (चूंडावतशाही पगड़ी)</strong> belongs to the Salumbar (सलूम्बर) thikana of **Mewar**, NOT Bikaner. Bikaner was famous for its distinctive high, light-weight Bikaneri Safa and Khunchewali Pagri.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Amarshahi?</strong> Amarshahi Pagri was introduced by Maharana Amar Singh II of Mewar and is one of the classic royal turbans of Udaipur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Vijayashahi & Mansinghshahi?</strong> Vijayashahi (introduced by Maharaja Vijay Singh) and Mansinghshahi (by Maharaja Man Singh) are famous Marwar/Jodhpur royal safas.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Bhimshahi?</strong> Bhimshahi Pagri was introduced by Maharana Bhim Singh of Mewar (featuring a broader base and prominent crown).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Amarshahi, Bhimshahi, Udayashahi, Chundawatshahi, Nathdwara Pagri = Mewar; Vijayashahi, Mansinghshahi, Jaswantshahi = Jodhpur.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Traditional Attire — Odhnis & Motifs — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC Senior Teacher & SI</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following traditional Rajasthani printed Odhnis is predominantly worn by <strong>tribal and rural women featuring angular cross-check wave patterns (छपाई)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Lahariya (लहरिया - Diagonal stripes worn during Shravan/Teej)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Tara Bhat ki Odhni (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी - Tribal women)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Mothda (मोठड़ा - Two-way criss-cross grid pattern)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Pila Pomcha (पीला पोमचा - Gifted on birth of male child)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Tara Bhat ki Odhni (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी - Tribal women)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Tara Bhat ki Odhni (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी)</strong> features star-shaped motifs with red-brown borders on a dark background, worn with great pride by tribal and rural women in Rajasthan (along with Jwar Bhat and Keri Bhat).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Lahariya?</strong> Lahariya features one-directional diagonal wave-like colored stripes, traditionally worn during the monsoon month of Shravana on Teej festival.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Mothda?</strong> Mothda is created when diagonal stripes cross each other in two opposite directions forming small checked squares (Mothda turbans are worn during wedding ceremonies).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Pila Pomcha?</strong> Pomcha is an auspicious lotus-print veil with a yellow center (Pila) sent by parents upon the birth of a baby boy, while a pink Pomcha is sent for a girl.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Tara Bhat, Keri Bhat, Jwar Bhat = Tribal Odhnis; Single diagonal wave = Lahariya; Cross diagonal grid = Mothda; Birth of boy = Pila Pomcha.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

## 🎯 Chapter 4: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Timaniya' (तिमणिया) is a traditional ornament worn on which part of the body by Rajasthani women?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Neck / Throat (गला)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Hand / Wrist (हाथ)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Head / Forehead (सिर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Waist (कमर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Neck / Throat (गला)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Timaniya is a gold necklace studded with gems and small golden beads. Other neck ornaments include Aad, Hansli, Champakali, Mandliya, Mangtika, Galpatta, and Tulsi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / College Lecturer 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Chump' (चूंप) is a traditional ornament worn in the:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Nose (नाक)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Tooth (दांत)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Ear (कान)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Finger (अंगुली)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Tooth (दांत)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> <em>Chump (चूंप)</em> and <em>Rakkhan (रखन)</em> are gold/silver inserts in teeth.<br>
        <strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> <em>Chop (चॉप)</em> is worn in the nose, whereas <em>Chump (चूंप)</em> is in the tooth!
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Memand' (मेमंद) and 'Rakhdi' (रखड़ी) are traditional ornaments of:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Ankles and Feet</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Waist and Hips</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Nose and Ears</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Head and Forehead (सिर व मस्तक)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Head and Forehead (सिर व मस्तक)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Memand, Rakhdi, Borla, Shishphool, Teeka, and Tikda are all worn across the head, hairline (*Maang*), and forehead.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Kandhora' (कंदोरा) and 'Kankati' (कणकती) are worn around which body part?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Neck (गला)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Waist (कमर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Arms (बाजू)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Feet (पैर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Waist (कमर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Kandhora, Kankati, Tagdi, Kardhani, and Zanjir are metallic belt ornaments clasped tightly around the waist.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2013, 2018 / Patwari 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Tara Bhat ri Odhni' (तारा भांत की ओढ़नी) is a signature traditional attire worn by:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Rajput Women</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Brahmin Women</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Tribal / Adivasi Women</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Jat Women</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Tribal / Adivasi Women</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Tribal women in Rajasthan wear tie-dye patterned Odhanis: *Tara Bhat* (Star motif on red-brown ground), *Kairi Bhat* (Mango/Paisley motif), and *Jawar Bhat* (Millet seed motif).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q6</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Amarshahi', 'Vijayashahi', 'Mansinghshahi', and 'Bhimshahi' are regional styles of:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Gold coins</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Sword fighting styles</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Royal palanquins</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Traditional Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ियां)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Traditional Turbans / Pagris (पगड़ियां)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Turbans represent regional honor and royal lineage: Amarshahi (Mewar), Vijayashahi & Mansinghshahi (Marwar/Jodhpur), Jaswantshahi, Shahjahanshahi, and Khangshedi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q7</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2019 / REET</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Peela / Pomcha' (पीला / पोमचा) is a traditional ceremonial veil presented to a mother:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> During wedding engagement only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> During Teej dance only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> During mourning ceremony</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> On the birth of a newborn child (Matritva ritual)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) On the birth of a newborn child (Matritva ritual)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Yellow Pomcha (*Peela*) is gifted by maternal grandparents on the birth of a baby boy, while Pink Pomcha (*Gulabi Pomcha*) is given upon the birth of a baby girl during the *Kuan Pujan / Jalwa Pujan* ceremony.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Nose (नाक)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Bari, Bhogli, Nath, Kaanta, Long, Fini, and Chop are all traditional nose ornaments worn by Rajasthani women.

# Chapter 5: Painting Styles and Folk Arts of Rajasthan (राजस्थान की चित्रकला शैलियां एवं लोक कलाएं)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: How Did Rajasthani Painting Evolve? (चित्रकला का उद्भव)</div>
  <em>"Before the 16th century, Indian painting was dominated by the rigid, flat lines of the Western Indian (Apabhramsa / अपभ्रंश / Jain) style. But when Mughal imperial stability united the country, Rajput rulers visited Agra and Lahore, bringing Persian miniaturists back to their desert courts. The fusion that resulted was magical: the precision, fine brushwork, and realism of Mughal court art merged with the raw devotion, bright indigenous colors, and natural landscapes of Rajasthan, giving birth to the <strong>Four Great Schools of Rajasthani Painting</strong>."</em>
</div>

---

## 1. The Four Major Painting Schools (राजस्थानी चित्रकला की 4 शैलियां)

```
                              4 SCHOOLS OF RAJASTHAN PAINTING (चित्रकला शैलियां)
                                                      │
         ┌───────────────────────────┬────────────────┴───────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
         │                           │                                            │                           │
    MEWAR SCHOOL                MARWAR SCHOOL                                DHUNDHAR SCHOOL             HADOTI SCHOOL
   (मेवाड़ स्कूल)              (मारवाड़ स्कूल)                                (ढूंढाड़ स्कूल)             (हाड़ौती स्कूल)
         │                           │                                            │                           │
 • Mewar Main (Sahibdin)     • Kishangarh (Bani-Thani - Nihal Chand)      • Amer/Jaipur (आमेर/जयपुर) • Bundi (बूंदी - Bird/Nature)
 • Nathdwara (Pichhwai)      • Jodhpur (Yellow, Clouds, Camels)           • Alwar (अलवर - Courtesans)• Kota (कोटा - Hunting with Queens)
 • Chavand (Nasiruddin)      • Bikaner (Usta & Matheerna Art)             • Shekhawati Frescoes
 • Deogarh (Thikana Style)
```

---

### A. Mewar School (मेवाड़ स्कूल) — The Root of Rajput Art
* **Earliest Illustrated Manuscript:** **Savaga Padhikkamana Sutta Chunni (श्रावक प्रतिक्रमण सूत्र चूर्णी)** (painted on palm-leaf in 1260 AD at Ahar during the reign of Rawal Tej Singh / तेजसिंह by artist **Kamal Chandra / कमलचन्द्र**).
* **Mewar Main Style:** 
  * Golden Era under **Maharana Jagat Singh I (जगतसिंह प्रथम - 1628–1652 AD)**; established the royal art academy **Chiteron ki Ovari (चितेरों की ओवरी)** (popularly called *Tasveera ro Karkhano / तस्वीरों रो कारखानो*).
  * Master Artists: **Sahibdin (साहिबदीन)** (painted *Ragamala, Rasikapriya, Geet Govinda*) and **Manohar (मनोहर)** (painted *Bal Kanda* of Ramayana).
* **Nathdwara Sub-Style (नाथद्वारा शैली):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 RPSC Favorite</span>
  * Fusion of Mewar and Braj / Vallabh (बल्लभ सम्प्रदाय) sect traditions.
  * **Pichhwai (पिछवाई):** Large cloth backdrops hung behind the Shrinathji (श्रीनाथजी) idol depicting Krishna’s *Baal-Leela*, cows, lotus flowers, and Kadamba / banana trees.
  * Dominant Colors: **Bright Yellow and Green (पीला व हरा रंग)**.
  * **Notable Female Painters (महिला चित्रकार):** **Kamala and Ilaichi (कमला एवं इलायची)** (frequently asked in RPSC!).
* **Chavand Sub-Style (चावण्ड शैली):** Originated under **Maharana Pratap** (1585 AD); master artist **Nasiruddin / Nisaradin (निसारदीन)** painted the famous *Ragamala (रागमाला)* set in 1605 AD under Amar Singh I.
* **Deogarh Sub-Style (देवगढ़ शैली):** Discovered and brought to global fame by art critic **Dr. Shridhar Andhare (डॉ. श्रीधर अंधारे)** (mix of Mewar, Marwar, and Dhundhar).

---

### B. Marwar School (मारवाड़ स्कूल) — Desert Realism & Ethereal Grace

#### 1. Kishangarh Style & Bani-Thani (किशनगढ़ शैली एवं बणी-ठणी) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Highest Exam Priority</span>
* **Golden Period:** Reign of King **Sawant Singh (सावंत सिंह)** (who adopted the pen-name **Nagardas / नागरीदास**).
* **Masterpiece:** **Bani-Thani (बणी-ठणी)** painted by court artist **Nihal Chand (निहालचन्द)**.
* **Global Acclaim:** Art historians **Eric Dickinson (एरिक डिकिंसन)** and **Dr. Fayaz Ali (डॉ. फैयाज अली)** discovered this style and hailed Bani-Thani as the **"Mona Lisa of India" (भारत की मोनालिसा)**. Government of India issued a commemorative postage stamp in 1973.
* **Distinct Features:** Long curved lotus eyes (*Khonjanayan / खंजन नयन*), arched thin eyebrows, sharp pointed nose with **Besar (बेसर)** ornament, transparent odhani, romantic Krishna-Radha themes around Gundolav Lake (गुंदोलाव झील).

#### 2. Jodhpur & Bikaner Styles
* **Jodhpur Style (जोधपुर शैली):** Bold lines, bright yellow and red colors, desert dunes, swirling dark clouds, mango trees, and camels. Peak under **Maharaja Man Singh (मानसिंह)** (Nath sect paintings like *Nath Charitra / नाथ चरित्र*).
* **Bikaner Style (बीकानेर शैली):** Heavy Mughal refinement, soft pastel shades. Famous for:
  * **Usta Art (उस्ता कला / मुनब्बती कला):** Intricate embossed gold filigree work on cured camel hide (**Padma Shri Hisamuddin Usta / हिसामुद्दीन उस्ता**).
  * **Matheerna Art (मथेरणा कला):** Wet lime plaster religious fresco murals painted by traditional Jain Matheerna families.

---

### C. Dhundhar School (ढूंढाड़ स्कूल)
* **Amer / Jaipur Style (आमेर / जयपुर शैली):** Founded by Sawai Jai Singh (established 36 royal workshops including *Suratkhana / सूरतखाना*); peak under **Sawai Pratap Singh (सवाई प्रताप सिंह)**.
  * **Arayash Technique (आलागीला / मोराकसी / फ्रेश्को बुनो):** Traditional fresco-buono technique where mineral pigments are applied to wet lime-marble plaster and buffed to a mirror finish.
  * Master Artists: **Sahibram (साहिबराम)** (pioneered life-size portraits — *Aadamkad / आदमकद चित्र* of Maharaja Ishwari Singh) and **Lalchand (लालचन्द)** (master of animal-fight scenes).
* **Alwar Style (अलवर शैली):** Illustrated copies of Sheikh Saadi’s *Gulistan (गुलिस्तां)* (by *Baldev & Ghulam Ali* under Raja Vinay Singh); unique for portraits of courtesans (*Tawaifs / वेश्याओं के चित्र*).
* **Shekhawati Frescoes (शेखावाटी के भित्ति चित्र):** Havelis of Nawalgarh (नवलगढ़), Mandawa (मंडावा), and Fatehpur (फतेहपुर) decorated with magnificent external wall murals; known globally as the **"Open Air Art Gallery"** of India.

---

### D. Hadoti School (हाड़ौती स्कूल)
* **Bundi Style (Bird & Animal Style / पशु-पक्षी शैली):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Repeated PYQ</span>
  * Characterized by lush forest vegetation, rain clouds, water springs, dancing peacocks, and monkeys.
  * Maharao Ummed Singh (उम्मेद सिंह) constructed the world-famous **Chitrashala / Rangmahal (चित्रशाला / रंगमहल)** inside Bundi Fort—a celestial gallery of wall paintings.
* **Kota Style (Hunting Style / शिकार शैली):**
  * Peak under **Maharao Ummed Singh I (उम्मेद सिंह प्रथम)**.
  * **Unique RPSC Question:** The only painting school in India where **queens and royal ladies are depicted hunting wild lions and tigers (रानियों को शिकार करते हुए दिखाया गया)** in dense jungles alongside kings!

---

## 2. Major Folk Arts & Master Handicrafts of Rajasthan (प्रमुख लोक कलाएं एवं हस्तशिल्प)

* **Phad Painting (फड़ चित्रकला - Shahpura, Bhilwara):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 High-Yield</span>
  * Painted scrolls on coarse Khadi cloth illustrating epics of folk deities (Pabuji, Devnarayanji).
  * Practiced exclusively by the **Joshi family of Shahpura (शाहपुरा का जोशी परिवार)** (**Padma Shri Shree Lal Joshi / श्रीलाल जोशी**; **Parvati Joshi / पार्वती जोशी** was India’s first female Phad painter).
* **Thewa Art (थेवा कला - Pratapgarh):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Global GI Tag</span>
  * Delicate 23-carat gold filigree and engraving on colored Belgian glass (*Kanch / कांच*).
  * Practiced exclusively by the **Rajsoni family of Pratapgarh (राजसोनी परिवार)** (pioneered by **Nathu Ji Soni / नाथू जी सोनी**; family secrets strictly guarded).
* **Blue Pottery (ब्लू पॉटरी - Jaipur):**
  * Ceramic glaze art made without clay (using quartz powder, raw glass, soda, and copper oxide).
  * Revived and globally popularized by **Padma Shri Kripal Singh Shekhawat (कृपाल सिंह शेखावत)** (developed *Kripal Kumbh* with 25 distinct color shades).
* **Terracotta Art (टेराकोटा - Molela, Rajsamand / मोलेला, राजसमंद):**
  * Hollow relief plaques of folk deities (*Devnarayanji, Goraji*) molded from Banas clay and donkey dung by Kumhars (**Padma Shri Mohan Lal Kumhar / मोहनलाल कुम्हार**).
* **Kavad Art (कावड़ - Bassi, Chittorgarh / बस्सी, चित्तौड़गढ़):**
  * Portable traveling wooden shrine (*चलता-फिरता काष्ठ मन्दिर*) with multi-layered folding painted doors narrating mythological epics; made by Suthar artisans (**Prabhatji Suthar / प्रभात जी सुथार**).

---

| School / Folk Craft | Masterpiece / Key Identifier | Key District / Master Artist |

| **Nathdwara Style (नाथद्वारा)** | Pichhwai (Shrinathji backdrops) | Kamala & Ilaichi (कमला व इलायची) |
| **Kishangarh Style (किशनगढ़)**| Bani-Thani ("Mona Lisa of India") | Nihal Chand / Sawant Singh (Nagardas) |
| **Bundi Style (बूंदी शैली)** | Bird & Animal Style, Chitrashala | Rao Ummed Singh / Bundi Fort |
| **Kota Style (कोटा शैली)** | Hunting scenes with Queens (रानियों द्वारा शिकार)| Maharao Ummed Singh |
| **Thewa Art (थेवा कला)** | 23-karat gold on Belgian glass | Pratapgarh / Nathu Ji Soni (Rajsoni) |
| **Phad Painting (फड़ कला)**| Painted scrolls on Khadi cloth | Shahpura / Shree Lal Joshi |
| **Blue Pottery (ब्लू पॉटरी)**| Quartz & glass ceramic craft | Jaipur / Kripal Singh Shekhawat |
| **Terracotta (टेराकोटा)** | Clay religious plaques | Molela (Rajsamand) / Mohan Lal |
| **Kavad Art (कावड़ कला)** | Portable wooden multi-door shrine | Bassi (Chittorgarh) / Prabhatji Suthar |

        <strong>Explanation:</strong>
        <br>• Thewa Art = Pratapgarh (Soni family, fine gold inlay work on colored Belgian glass).
        <br>• Usta Art = Bikaner (Hisamuddin Usta, camel hide gold embossing).
        <br>• Blue Pottery = Jaipur (Turquoise/cobalt glazing on clay-less dough).
        <br>• Kavad Art = Bassi, Chittorgarh (Prabhatji Suthar, portable wooden story shrines).

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Shrilal Joshi was awarded the Padma Shri for revitalizing Phad painting.</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Devnarayanji's Phad is the longest and oldest Phad, on which a 5-rupee postage stamp was issued in 1992.</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Shahpura (Bhilwara) is the primary center, maintained by the traditional Chhipa Joshi family.</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Pabuji's Phad is sung by Nayak Bhopas accompanied by the Jantar musical instrument.</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Pabuji's Phad is sung by Nayak Bhopas accompanied by the Jantar musical instrument.</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Pabuji's Phad is accompanied by the <strong>Ravanhattha</strong> (रावणहत्था) instrument. <strong>Jantar</strong> (जंतर) is used by Gujjar Bhopas during the narration of <em>Devnarayanji's Phad</em>.<br>
        <strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> Always remember: Pabuji = Ravanhattha; Devnarayanji = Jantar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Painting Schools — Kishangarh School & 'Chandni Raat ri Sangeet Gosthi' — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    While master painter Nihal Chand immortalized *Bani-Thani*, which other court artist of the <strong>Kishangarh School of Painting</strong> created the world-famous masterpiece titled <strong>'Chandni Raat ri Sangeet Gosthi' (चांदनी रात की संगीत गोष्ठी - Moonlight Musical Concert)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Amar Chand (अमरचन्द)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Sahibdin (साहिबदीन)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Nisardin (निसारदीन)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Ruknuddin (रुक्नुद्दीन)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Amar Chand (अमरचन्द)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The celebrated painting **'Chandni Raat ri Sangeet Gosthi'** (depicting royal lovers and musicians floating under starry moonlit silver waters) was painted by master artist **Amar Chand** during the reign of Sawant Singh / Sardar Singh in Kishangarh.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Sahibdin?</strong> Sahibdin was the master painter of Mewar who painted *Ragamala (1628), Geet Govinda (1629), and Arsh Ramayana (1651)*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Nisardin?</strong> Nisardin painted the *Chawand Ragamala (1605)* in Mewar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Ruknuddin?</strong> Ruknuddin was the court master of the Bikaner School (*Usta / Rasikapriya*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Chandni Raat ri Sangeet Gosthi = Amar Chand (Kishangarh); Bani-Thani = Nihal Chand (Kishangarh); Chawand Ragamala (1605) = Nisardin (Mewar).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Painting Schools — Devgarh Sub-school & Dr. Shridhar Andhare — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>Devgarh Sub-school of Painting (देवगढ़ उपशैली)</strong>, established in 1680 AD under Rawat Dwarkadas Chundawat and blending stylistic elements of Mewar, Marwar, and Jaipur, was brought to international acclaim by which pioneering art historian?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Ananda Coomaraswamy</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Dr. Shridhar Andhare (डॉ. श्रीधर अंधारे)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Eric Dickinson</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Dr. Fayaz Ali</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Dr. Shridhar Andhare** discovered and brought the **Devgarh School of Painting** into the global spotlight. Famous court painters of Devgarh were **Bagta (बगता), Chokha (चोखा), and Kavala (कवला)**. The school is renowned for royal hunting scenes, court durbars, and yellow/green hues.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Ananda Coomaraswamy?</strong> Coomaraswamy wrote *'Rajput Painting'* in 1916 (first scientific classification of Rajasthani paintings).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Eric Dickinson?</strong> Eric Dickinson (along with Dr. Fayaz Ali) brought Kishangarh's **Bani-Thani** to international acclaim, dubbing it the *'Indian Mona Lisa'*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Devgarh School discovered by = Dr. Shridhar Andhare (Painters: Bagta & Chokha); Bani-Thani brought to light by = Eric Dickinson & Dr. Fayaz Ali.

    </div>
  </details>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Bikaner School (बीकानेर शैली - Usta & Matheran art)</div>

    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Kota School of Painting (कोटा चित्रकला शैली)** (developed under Maharao Ram Singh, Ummed Singh, and Durjanshal) specialized in dense forest wildlife and **hunting scenes (शिकार के दृश्य)** where royal women, queens, and court maidens are depicted perched on tree machans shooting tigers, leopards, and wild boars.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Bundi School?</strong> Bundi School is famous as the *'Bird & Animal Painting School'* (*पशु-पक्षियों की चित्रशैली*) with famous *Chitrashala* frescoes.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Jaipur School?</strong> Jaipur School is famous for life-size royal portraits (*Aadamkad Chitra*) painted by master Sahibram.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Bikaner School?</strong> Bikaner School is renowned for Muslim Usta painters signing their names and dates (*Usta & Matheran Kala*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Women hunting tigers = Kota School; Animals/Birds & Chitrashala = Bundi School; Life-size portraits = Jaipur (Sahibram); Signed paintings with dates = Bikaner.
      </div>

The Soni family (honored with the title *Rajsoni* and multiple Padma Shri / National awards including Mahesh Raj Soni) keeps the exact heat-fusion gold-on-green-glass technique closely guarded, teaching it only to daughters-in-law.<br><br>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Chump (चूंम्प)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Rakkan (रखन)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Memand (मेमंद)</div>

Art critic Eric Dickinson christened it the *'Mona Lisa of India'*, and the Government of India issued a 20-paise postage stamp on it on 5 May 1973.<br><br>

        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Bani Thani nose ring = Besari; Painter = Nihal Chand; Ruler = Sawant Singh (Nagardas); Postage Stamp = 1973; Title = Mona Lisa of India.

    </div>
  </details>
</div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Usta Artisan Family of Bikaner (बीकानेर का उस्ता परिवार - Camel hide art)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Mohanlal Kumhar Family of Molela (मोलेला का कुम्हार परिवार - Terracotta)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Rajsoni / Soni Family of Pratapgarh (प्रतापगढ़ का राजसोनी परिवार)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Thewa Art was pioneered during the reign of Maharawat Samant Singh by **Nathuji Soni (नाथूजी सोनी)** in Pratapgarh. The Soni family received the royal title 'Rajsoni' and keeps the secret technique of fusing gold onto Belgian glass strictly within the family.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Joshi Family?</strong> The Joshi family of Shahpura (Bhilwara) is world-famous for **Phad Painting (फड़ चित्रकला)**, practiced by master artists Shrilal Joshi and Pratik Joshi.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Usta Family?</strong> The Usta artisan family of Bikaner is world-famous for **Usta Kala (उस्ता कला / मुनब्बती कला)** (gold embossing on camel leather), pioneered by Padma Shri Hisamuddin Usta.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Molela Family?</strong> The Kumhar artisans of Molela (Nathdwara, Rajsamand), notably Padma Shri Mohanlal Kumhar, are famous for **Molela Terracotta (मोलेला टेराकोटा)** clay plaques of Devnarayanji and Dharmraj.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Thewa Art = Pratapgarh (Nathuji Soni); Phad Painting = Shahpura (Joshi family); Usta Art = Bikaner (Hisamuddin Usta); Terracotta = Molela (Mohanlal Kumhar).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

# Chapter 6: Architecture of Rajasthan — Forts, Palaces, Temples & Stepwells (राजस्थान का स्थापत्य — दुर्ग, महल, मंदिर एवं बावड़ियां)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Anatomy of a Rajasthani Fort (दुर्गों की संरचना)</div>
  <em>"In Sukra Niti (शुक्र नीति), a kingdom is compared to a human body, and forts are called its <strong>Arms (भुजाएं)</strong>. In Rajasthan, a fort was not just a military barracks; it was a self-sustaining fortified city containing royal palaces, granaries holding years of food, deep stepwells, weapon foundries, temples, and artisan quarters. That is why enemies had to lay siege for 6 to 12 months just to starve a fort out!"</em>
</div>

<div class="term-box">
  <div class="term-title">📖 Terminology Decoder: Traditional Architectural Terms (स्थापत्य शब्दावली)</div>
  <ul>
    <li><strong>Pol (पोल / दरवाजा):</strong> Massive fortified entry gate fitted with iron spikes to prevent battering rams/war elephants from breaching (e.g., *Suraj Pol / सूरज पोल, Bhairon Pol / भैरव पोल, Laxman Pol / लक्ष्मण पोल*).</li>
    <li><strong>Burj (बुर्ज):</strong> Semicircular bastion projecting outward from the ramparts allowing archers and musketeers to shoot sideways at attackers.</li>
    <li><strong>Jharokha (झरोखा):</strong> Overhanging enclosed stone balcony with latticework (*Jali / जाली*) allowing royal women to view courtyards while observing Purdah.</li>
    <li><strong>Baradari (बारादरी):</strong> Open marble pavilion with 12 arched doorways designed for cooling breezes by lakes (e.g., at Anasagar Lake).</li>
    <li><strong>Baori / Vav (बावड़ी / वाव):</strong> Architectural stepwell with artistic stone stairs descending to the groundwater level.</li>
  </ul>
</div>

---

## 1. The 6 UNESCO World Heritage Hill Forts (2013) (यूनेस्को विश्व धरोहर के 6 पहाड़ी दुर्ग) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Inscribed at Phnom Penh</span>

<div class="mnemonic-box">
  <div class="mnemonic-title">🧠 Master Mnemonic: The Fruit Basket (चीकू - गाजर - आम)</div>
  <blockquote>
    <strong>"चीकू - गाजर - आम" (Chikoo - Gajar - Aam)</strong>
  </blockquote>
  <ul>
    <li><strong>Chi (चि):</strong> <strong>Chi</strong>ttorgarh Fort (चित्तौड़गढ़ दुर्ग)</li>
    <li><strong>Ku (कु):</strong> <strong>Ku</strong>mbhalgarh Fort (कुम्भलगढ़ दुर्ग)</li>
    <li><strong>Ga (गा):</strong> <strong>Ga</strong>gron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग - Jhalawar)</li>
    <li><strong>Ja (ज):</strong> <strong>Ja</strong>isalmer Fort (जैसलमेर दुर्ग - Sonar Qila / सोनार किला)</li>
    <li><strong>R (र):</strong> <strong>R</strong>anthambore Fort (रणथंभौर दुर्ग - Sawai Madhopur)</li>
    <li><strong>Aam (आम):</strong> <strong>Am</strong>er Fort (आमेर दुर्ग - Jaipur)</li>
  </ul>
</div>

---

## 2. Comprehensive Fort Profiles & History

### A. Chittorgarh Fort (चित्तौड़गढ़ दुर्ग) — "The King of Forts" (दुर्गों का सिरमौर)
* **Superlative:** *"गढ़ तो चित्तौड़गढ़, बाकी सब गढ़ैया"* (Largest living fort in India, spanning $8\text{ km} \times 2\text{ km}$ atop the Mesha Plateau / मेसा का पठार at the confluence of **Gambhiri (गम्भीरी) and Berach (बेड़च)** rivers). Built by Mauryan King **Chitrangad Mori (चित्रांगद मोरी)**.
* **Seven Main Gates (Pols / सात पोल):** *Padan Pol (पाडन पोल), Bhairon Pol (भैरव पोल), Hanuman Pol (हनुमान पोल), Ganesh Pol (गणेश पोल), Jorla Pol (जोड़ला पोल), Laxman Pol (लक्ष्मण पोल), Ram Pol (राम पोल)*.
* **Three Legendary Sakas (चित्तौड़ के तीन साके):**
  1. **1303 AD:** Alauddin Khilji vs Rawal Ratan Singh (Gora & Badal; Rani Padmini Jauhar).
  2. **1535 AD:** Bahadur Shah of Gujarat vs Vikramaditya (Rawat Bagh Singh of Deoliya / रावत बाघ सिंह; Rani Karmavati / कर्मावती Jauhar).
  3. **1567–68 AD:** Akbar vs Maharana Uday Singh (Jaimal Rathore / जयमल राठौड़ & Patta Sisodia / फत्ता सिसोदिया; Phool Kanwar / फूल कंवर Jauhar).
* **Vijay Stambh (विजय स्तम्भ - Victory Pillar):** 9 stories (122 feet high, 157 stairs); built by **Maharana Kumbha (1440–1448)** to celebrate the Battle of Sarangpur (1437). Chief Architect: **Jaita (जैता) and his sons Napa, Poma, Punja (नापा, पोमा, पूंजा)**. Emblem of **Rajasthan Police (राजस्थान पुलिस)** and **Board of Secondary Education (माध्यमिक शिक्षा बोर्ड - RBSE)**.
* **Kirti Stambh (कीर्ति स्तम्भ - Jain Tower of Fame):** 7 stories (75 feet high); built in 12th century by Jain merchant **Jija Bhagerwala (जीजा शाह बघेरवाल)**; dedicated to 1st Tirthankara Lord Adinath (आदिनाथ).

---

### B. Kumbhalgarh Fort (कुम्भलगढ़ दुर्ग - Rajsamand) — "The Invincible Bastion"
* **Builder & Architect:** Built by **Maharana Kumbha (1448–1458)** atop the Aravallis; designed by chief architect **Mandan (मण्डन)**.
* **The Great Wall of India (भारत की महान दीवार):** The perimeter fortification wall is **36 km long** and so wide that **4 to 8 horsemen can ride abreast** on top (second longest continuous wall in the world after the Great Wall of China).
* **Katargarh (कटारगढ़ - The Eye of Mewar):** The upper inner citadel perched at the highest apex where Kumbha resided; birthplace of **Maharana Pratap** (9 May 1540). Abul Fazl wrote: *"यह दुर्ग इतनी बुलन्दी पर बना है कि नीचे से ऊपर देखने पर सिर से पगड़ी गिर जाती है!" (It is perched at such an astonishing height that looking up at it knocks the turban off one’s head!).*

---

### C. Gagron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग - Jhalawar) — Premier Water Fort (जल दुर्ग) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Repeated PYQ</span>
* Built without foundations directly on a solid natural rock at the sacred confluence of **Ahu (आहू) and Kali Sindh (कालीसिंध)** rivers.
* Contains the Dargah of Sufi Saint **Meethe Shah (मीठे शाह / हमीदुद्दीन चिश्ती)** and the penance cave/memorial of **Saint Pipa (संत पीपा की छतरी)**.
* **Two Sakas (दो साके):** 1423 AD (Achaldas Khinchi / अचलदास खींची vs Hoshang Shah) and 1444 AD (Mahmud Khilji I renamed it *Mustafabad / मुस्तफाबाद*).

---

### D. Ranthambore Fort (रणथंभौर दुर्ग - Sawai Madhopur) — The Armored Fort (बख्तरबंद दुर्ग)
* Surrounded by 7 concentric forested hills. Historian Abul Fazl famously remarked: *"अन्य सब दुर्ग नंगे हैं, जबकि यह दुर्ग बख्तरबंद है!" (All other forts in Hindustan are bare, but this fort is armored).*
* Houses the famous **Trinetra Ganesh Temple (त्रिनेत्र गणेश मन्दिर)** and the 32-pillared cenotaph (**Battis Khambhon ki Chhatri / बत्तीस खम्भों की छतरी** / *Nyay ki Chhatri / न्याय की छतरी* built by Hammir).
* **1st Saka of Rajasthan (1301 AD):** Hammir Dev Chauhan vs Alauddin Khilji (Rani Rangdevi led Jauhar).

---

### E. Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग - Jodhpur) — The Citadel of the Sun
* Built by **Rao Jodha in 1459 AD** on **Chidiya-tunk hill (चिड़ियाटूंंक पहाड़ी)**; known as *Mayurdhwajgarh (मयूरध्वजगढ़)* (peacock-shaped).
* Rudyard Kipling (रुडयार्ड किपलिंग) exclaimed: *"A palace that might have been built by Titans and colored by the morning sun... the work of fairies and giants!" (परियों और देवताओं द्वारा निर्मित).*
* Famous Cannons on Ramparts (तोपें): **Kilkila (किलकिला), Shambhuban (शंभूबाण), Gajni Khan (गजनी खां), Kadak Bijli (कड़क बिजली)**.

---

### F. Lohagarh Fort (लोहागढ़ दुर्ग - Bharatpur) — The Invincible Mud Fort (अजय मिट्टी का किला)
* Constructed in **1733 AD by Maharaja Surajmal (महाराजा सूरजमल - Plato of Jats / जाटों का प्लेटो)**.
* Engineered with a double defensive wall: inner stone wall surrounded by an outer 8-meter-thick mud rampart, encircled by a deep moat (**Sujan Ganga Canal / सुजान गंगा नहर** fed by River Banganga / Moti Jheel).
* In 1805, British Commander **Lord Lake (लॉर्ड लेक)** attacked Lohagarh 5 consecutive times with heavy artillery but failed to breach the shock-absorbing mud ramparts!

---

## 3. Masterpieces of Temples, Stepwells & Royal Cenotaphs

* **Dilwara Jain Temples (देलवाड़ा के जैन मन्दिर - Mount Abu / माउंट आबू):**
  * **Vimal Vasahi Temple (विमल वसही - 1031 AD):** Dedicated to 1st Tirthankara Rishabhdev (built by Vimal Shah / विमल शाह, architect: Kirtidhar).
  * **Luna Vasahi Temple (लूण वसही - 1230 AD):** Dedicated to 22nd Tirthankara Neminath (built by brothers Vastupala & Tejapala / वास्तुपाल व तेजपाल; features *Devrani-Jethani ka Gokhra / देवरानी-जेठानी का गोखड़ा*).
* **Ranakpur Jain Temple (रणकपुर जैन मन्दिर - Pali / पाली):** Dedicated to Lord Adinath; built by **Dharanashah (धरणक शाह)** during Rana Kumbha's reign (architect: **Depak / देपाक**). Famous worldwide for its **1,444 uniquely carved marble pillars (1444 खम्भों का वन)** (no two pillars are identical!).
* **Chand Baori (चाँद बावड़ी - Abhaneri, Dausa / आभानेरी, दौसा):** World's deepest and most visually stunning stepwell (built in 8th century by King Chanda / राजा चन्द in Gurjara-Pratihara style); 13 stories deep with **3,500 maze-like symmetrical stone steps** opposite Harshat Mata Temple (हर्षत माता मन्दिर).
* **Rani-ji ki Baori (रानी जी की बावड़ी - Bundi):** Acclaimed as the **"Queen of Stepwells" (बावड़ियों का सिरमौर)**; built in 1699 AD by Rani Nathavati (रानी नाथावती).
* **Major Royal Cenotaphs (प्रमुख छतरियां):**
  * **84-Pillared Cenotaph (चौरासी खम्भों की छतरी):** Bundi (Rao Anirudh Singh for foster-brother Dhabhai Deva).
  * **80-Pillared Cenotaph / Moosi Maharani (अस्सी खम्भों की छतरी / मूसी महारानी):** Alwar (Maharaja Vinay Singh).
  * **Jaswant Thada (जसवंत थड़ा):** Jodhpur (White marble cenotaph built by Maharaja Sardar Singh for Jaswant Singh II; called the **"Taj Mahal of Marwar" / मारवाड़ का ताजमहल**).

---

## ⚡ Chapter 6: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

| Monument / Site | Key Architectural Distinction | District / Master Builder |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Chittorgarh Fort (चित्तौड़गढ़)**| Largest living fort / 3 Sakas / Vijay Stambh | Chitrangad Mori / Kumbha |
| **Kumbhalgarh (कुम्भलगढ़)** | 36 km Great Wall / Katargarh citadel | Rajsamand / Mandan (Architect) |
| **Gagron Fort (गागरोन)** | Water Fort (Ahu & Kali Sindh) / No foundations | Jhalawar / Meethe Shah Dargah |
| **Ranthambore (रणथंभौर)** | "Armored Fort" / Trinetra Ganesh | Sawai Madhopur / Hammir Dev |
| **Mehrangarh (मेहरानगढ़)** | "Work of fairies & giants" (Kipling) | Jodhpur / Rao Jodha (1459) |
| **Lohagarh (लोहागढ़)** | Invincible Mud Fort (Lord Lake defeated) | Bharatpur / Maharaja Surajmal |
| **Ranakpur Temple (रणकपुर)**| 1,444 carved marble pillars, Adinath | Pali / Dharanashah (Depak) |
| **Dilwara Temples (देलवाड़ा)**| Vimal Vasahi & Luna Vasahi (Pure marble) | Mount Abu / Vimal Shah & Tejapala |
| **Chand Baori (चाँद बावड़ी)** | 3,500 symmetrical steps, 13 stories | Abhaneri (Dausa) / King Chanda |
| **Jaswant Thada (जसवंत थड़ा)** | "Taj Mahal of Marwar" (White marble) | Jodhpur / Maharaja Sardar Singh |




## 📝 Chapter 6: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

<div class="pyq-card">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 UNESCO Heritage & Fort Architecture</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following hill forts of Rajasthan was NOT included in the prestigious <strong>UNESCO World Heritage List in 2013 (Phnom Penh 37th Session)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Kumbhalgarh Fort (Rajsamand)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Jaisalmer Fort (Sonar Qila)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Gagron Fort (Jhalawar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Mehrangarh Fort (Jodhpur)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Mehrangarh Fort (Jodhpur)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> The 6 UNESCO Hill Forts of Rajasthan are remembered by the famous mnemonic <strong>"चिकू गाजर आम" (CHIKU GAJAR AAM)</strong>:
        <br>• <strong>चि</strong> = Chittorgarh
        <br>• <strong>कु</strong> = Kumbhalgarh
        <br>• <strong>गा</strong> = Gagron
        <br>• <strong>ज</strong> = Jaisalmer
        <br>• <strong>र</strong> = Ranthambhore
        <br>• <strong>आम</strong> = Amer
        <br>Mehrangarh and Junagarh are NOT in the UNESCO list.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Stepwells & Historical Baoris</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The world-renowned <strong>Chand Baori (चांद बावड़ी)</strong>, known for its intricate geometrical maze of 3,500 narrow steps and adjacent Harshat Mata temple, is located at:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Neemrana (Kotputli-Behror)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Abhaneri (Dausa)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Osian (Jodhpur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Bundi</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Abhaneri (Dausa)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Chand Baori at Abhaneri (Dausa) was built in the 8th-9th century by King Chandra (Nikumbha Rajput) in the Gurjara-Pratihara style. It is one of the deepest and most visually stunning stepwells in the world.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Water Fort & Unlaid Foundations</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which fort of Rajasthan is classified as a prime example of an <strong>Audak Durg (Jal Durg / Water Fort)</strong>, resting directly upon bare rocky foundations without mortar or excavated foundation trenches at the confluence of the Ahu and Kali Sindh rivers?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Bhainsrorgarh Fort (Chittorgarh)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Shergarh Fort (Baran)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Gagron Fort (Jhalawar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Manohar Thana Fort (Jhalawar)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Gagron Fort (Jhalawar)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Gagron Fort (Jhalawar) stands on a solid rocky ridge encircled by Ahu and Kali Sindh rivers on three sides. It has no foundation trench (बिना नींव का दुर्ग). It is famous for Achaldas Khichi's heroic saka in 1423 CE against Hoshang Shah of Malwa, and houses the Dargah of Sufi saint Mithe Shah (Hamiduddin).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Chhatris & Cenotaphs Architecture</span>

    <br><strong>List-I (Chhatri)</strong>
    <br>(A) 84-Pillared Chhatri (चौरासी खंभों की छतरी)
    <br>(B) 80-Pillared Chhatri / Moosi Maharani Chhatri
    <br>(C) 32-Pillared Chhatri of Hammir Chauhan
    <br>(D) 16-Pillared Chhatri of Amar Singh Rathore
    <br><strong>List-II (Location)</strong>
    <br>(i) Alwar
    <br>(ii) Bundi
    <br>(iii) Nagaur Fort
    <br>(iv) Ranthambhore Fort
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A)-(iv), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iii)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)</div>

    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong>
        <br>• 84-Pillared Chhatri = Bundi (Built by Rao Raja Aniruddh Singh in memory of foster brother Dhaabhai Deva).
        <br>• 80-Pillared Chhatri = Alwar (Moosi Maharani & Maharaja Bakhtawar Singh, built by Vinay Singh).
        <br>• 32-Pillared Chhatri = Ranthambhore (Built by Hammir Dev Chauhan for his father Jaitra Singh's 32-year rule).
        <br>• 16-Pillared Chhatri = Nagaur Fort (Amar Singh Rathore).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Forts Architecture — Gagron Fort (Ahu & Kali Sindh Confluence) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which magnificent hill-cum-water fort (<strong>Jal Durg / Audak Durg</strong>) of Rajasthan stands directly on solid rock without any foundation trenches at the confluence of the <strong>Ahu and Kali Sindh rivers</strong> in Jhalawar, and houses the Dargah of Sufi Saint <strong>Mithe Shah (Dewan Shah)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Bhaisrodrgarh Fort (Chittor - Chambal & Bamani)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Shergarh Fort (Dholpur - Chambal)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Gagron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग, झालावाड़ - UNESCO World Heritage Fort)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Kanor Fort</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Gagron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग, झालावाड़ - UNESCO World Heritage Fort)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Gagron Fort (Jhalawar)** is India's prime specimen of an **Audak / Jal Durg (जल दुर्ग)**. Surrounded on 3 sides by the waters of the **Ahu and Kali Sindh**, it was constructed by Dod Rajputs (Bijaldeva) and later ruled by Khinchi Chauhans (Achaldas Khinchi). It contains the tomb of **Sant Pipa**, Dargah of **Mithe Shah**, and the colossal **Buland Darwaza** built by Aurangzeb.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Bhaisrodrgarh?</strong> Bhaisrodrgarh (Chittorgarh) is located at the confluence of the **Chambal and Bamani** rivers ('Vellore of Rajasthan').</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Shergarh?</strong> Shergarh is located on the Chambal river in Dholpur (houses the colossal *Hunhunkar cannon*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Kanor?</strong> Kanor is in Udaipur.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Jal Durg at Ahu + Kali Sindh = Gagron Fort (Jhalawar); Jal Durg at Chambal + Bamani = Bhaisrodrgarh (Chittor).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
