  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which world-famous 19th-century haveli in Jaisalmer, adorned with life-size yellow stone elephants (named *Lalu* and *Hathi*) at the entrance, was carved simultaneously from left and right halves by two architect brothers named <strong>Hathi and Lalu</strong> without using any drawn blueprints?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Patwon ki Haveli (पटवों की हवेली - 5 Storeys)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Rampuria Haveli (बीकानेर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Salim Singh ki Haveli (सालिम सिंह की हवेली - 9 Storeys / Moti Mahal)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Nathmal ki Haveli (नथमल की हवेली, जैसलमेर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Nathmal ki Haveli (नथमल की हवेली, जैसलमेर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Built in 1885 for Diwan Nathmal, **Nathmal ki Haveli** was carved by two master architect brothers, **Hathi and Lalu**. Working independently on two separate halves (left and right) without a unified plan, they achieved near-perfect visual symmetry with miniature stone carvings of trains, bicycles, and floral lace.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Patwon ki Haveli?</strong> Patwon ki Haveli is a cluster of 5 mansions built by Guman Chand Patwa for his 5 sons.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Rampuria Haveli?</strong> Rampuria Havelis are located in Bikaner crafted in red Dulmera sandstone.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Salim Singh ki Haveli?</strong> Salim Singh ki Haveli (9 storeys) has a top floor designed like a peacock-shaped glass ship (*Jahaz Mahal / Moti Mahal*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Architect brothers Hathi & Lalu = Nathmal Haveli; 5 Mansions for 5 sons = Patwon Haveli; 9 Storeys ship shape = Salim Singh Haveli (All in Jaisalmer).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Forts Architecture — Kumbhalgarh & Abul Fazl's Quote — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Regarding which formidable hill-citadel of Rajasthan did Mughal historian <strong>Abul Fazl</strong> write in the <em>Akbarnama</em>: <em>'यह दुर्ग इतनी बुलन्दी पर बना हुआ है कि नीचे से ऊपर देखने पर सिर से पगड़ी गिर जाती है'</em>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Chittorgarh Fort (चित्तौड़गढ़ दुर्ग)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Kumbhalgarh Fort — Katargarh (कुम्भलगढ़ दुर्ग / कटारगढ़)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Ranthambore Fort (रणथम्भौर दुर्ग)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Kumbhalgarh Fort — Katargarh (कुम्भलगढ़ दुर्ग / कटारगढ़)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Abul Fazl made this famous observation about the inner citadel **Katargarh (कटारगढ़ - Eye of Mewar)** inside **Kumbhalgarh Fort (Rajsamand)**. Designed by architect **Mandan (मण्डन)** for Maharana Kumbha (1448–1458 AD), Kumbhalgarh stands on 13 mountain peaks and possesses a **36-km-long defense wall** ('The Great Wall of India'). It is also the birthplace of Maharana Pratap (*Badal Mahal / Juna Kacheri*).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Chittorgarh?</strong> Chittorgarh is praised as *'Garh to Chittorgarh, Baki sab Garhaiya'* situated on the flat Mesha plateau.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Mehrangarh?</strong> Rudyard Kipling described Mehrangarh as *'Built by angels, fairies and giants'*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Ranthambore?</strong> Abul Fazl described Ranthambore as *'All other forts are bare, but this fort is armored (बख्तरबंद)'*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Pugree falls when looking up = Kumbhalgarh (Katargarh); Armored fort (बख्तरबंद) = Ranthambore; Built by angels/fairies = Mehrangarh.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Forts Architecture — Ranthambore & Nyaya ki Chhatri — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>32-Pillared Cenotaph (32 खम्भों की छतरी)</strong> inside Ranthambore Fort, constructed by <strong>Rana Hammir Dev Chauhan</strong> to commemorate the 32-year reign of his father <strong>Jaitra Singh</strong>, is popularly known as which of the following?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Chaurasi Khambhon ki Chhatri (84 Pillars - Bundi)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Moosi Maharani ki Chhatri (80 Pillars - Alwar)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Nyaya ki Chhatri / Umbrella of Justice (न्याय की छतरी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Gora-Badal ki Chhatri (Chittor)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Nyaya ki Chhatri / Umbrella of Justice (न्याय की छतरी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Rana Hammir Dev built the magnificent red stone **32-Pillared Cenotaph** inside Ranthambore Fort in honor of his father Jaitra Singh's 32-year rule. Hammir sat beneath this grand domed pavilion to deliver royal judicial verdicts to his subjects, earning it the immortal name **'Nyaya ki Chhatri' (न्याय की छतरी)**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) 84 Pillars?</strong> The 84-Pillared Cenotaph (*Chaurasi Khambhon ki Chhatri*) is located in **Bundi**, built by Rao Raja Anirudh Singh for foster brother Deva.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Moosi Maharani?</strong> The 80-Pillared Cenotaph of *Moosi Maharani* is located in **Alwar**, built by Maharaja Vinay Singh in 1815.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Gora-Badal?</strong> Gora-Badal's dome is located inside Chittorgarh Fort.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Nyaya ki Chhatri (32 pillars) = Ranthambore (Hammir for father Jaitra Singh); 84 Pillars = Bundi; 80 Pillars = Alwar (Moosi Maharani).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Temples Architecture — Kiradu 'Khajuraho of Rajasthan' — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which magnificent cluster of 5 Hindu temples in Hathma village (<strong>Barmer</strong>), built in the 11th–12th century <strong>Gurjara-Pratihara / Mahu-Maru style</strong> and featuring the grand <strong>Someshwara Temple</strong>, is celebrated as the <strong>'Khajuraho of Rajasthan' (राजस्थान का खजुराहो)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Bhanddevra Temple (भांडदेवरा - Ramgarh, Baran)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Harshat Mata Temple (हर्षत माता मंदिर - Abhaneri)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Jagat Ambika Temple (जगत अम्बिका मंदिर - Udaipur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Kiradu Temples (किराडू के मंदिर, बाड़मेर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Kiradu Temples (किराडू के मंदिर, बाड़मेर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Kiradu Temples (किराडू)** near Barmer are universally recognized as the **'Khajuraho of Rajasthan'** for their intricate erotic and mythological stone sculptures depicting life, war, and spiritual cosmos. The largest and most ornate temple is dedicated to **Lord Someshwara (Shiva)**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Bhanddevra?</strong> Bhanddevra (Baran) is celebrated as the **'Khajuraho of Hadoti'** or *'Mini Khajuraho'*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Harshat Mata?</strong> Harshat Mata is an 8th-century Pratihara temple in Abhaneri (Dausa) adjacent to Chand Baori.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jagat Ambika?</strong> Jagat Ambika (Udaipur) is celebrated as the **'Khajuraho of Mewar'**.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Khajuraho of Rajasthan = Kiradu (Barmer); Khajuraho of Mewar = Jagat Ambika (Udaipur); Khajuraho of Hadoti = Bhanddevra (Baran).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Forts Architecture — Gagron Water Fort (Ahu & Kali Sindh) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Why is <strong>Gagron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग, झालावाड़)</strong> classified as the premier <strong>Jal Durg / Audak Durg (जल दुर्ग / औदक दुर्ग)</strong> of Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Because it has a massive underground swimming pool built by Akbar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Because it was built by the British Navy as a naval dockyard</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Because it is situated in the middle of Lake Pichola</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Because it is surrounded on three sides by the deep waters of the Ahu and Kali Sindh rivers and rests directly on a solid rock cliff without any artificial foundation</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Because it is surrounded on three sides by the deep waters of the Ahu and Kali Sindh rivers and rests directly on a solid rock cliff without any artificial foundation</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Gagron Fort (built by King Bijaldev Doda in the 12th century and ruled by Khinchi Chauhans like Achaldas Khinchi) is situated at the confluence (*Sangam*) of the **Ahu and Kali Sindh rivers** in Jhalawar. It is constructed directly on natural stone without foundation trenches, houses the tomb of Sufi saint **Mitheshah (Hamiduddin Chishti)**, and witnessed 2 historic Sakas (1423 AD & 1444 AD).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Akbar did not build Gagron; he granted it to Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (who composed *Veli Krishan Rukmani ri* here).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Gagron is a medieval Rajput fort, not a colonial dockyard.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Lake Pichola in Udaipur contains the *Jag Niwas (Lake Palace)* and *Jag Mandir*, not Gagron.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Premier Jal Durg = Gagron (Ahu + Kali Sindh); Author of Veli Krishan Rukmani ri at Gagron = Prithviraj Rathore; Tomb of Mitheshah = Gagron.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Temples Architecture — Ranakpur Chaumukha Jain Temple — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The architectural wonder <strong>Ranakpur Chaumukha Jain Temple (रणकपुर जैन मंदिर, पाली)</strong>, built in 1439 AD during the reign of Maharana Kumbha on the banks of the Maghai River, is dedicated to which Tirthankara and features how many intricately carved stone pillars?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Lord Rishabhdev / Adinath (भगवान आदिनाथ); 1,444 pillars (no two pillars have identical carving)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Lord Mahavira; 84 pillars</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Lord Parshvanatha; 108 pillars</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Lord Neminatha; 500 pillars</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Lord Rishabhdev / Adinath (भगवान आदिनाथ); 1,444 pillars (no two pillars have identical carving)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Ranakpur Temple was commissioned by Jain merchant **Dharnak Shah (धरणक शाह)** and designed by master architect **Depak (देपाक)** in 1439 AD under Maharana Kumbha. It is dedicated to the 1st Jain Tirthankara **Adinath / Rishabhdev (भगवान आदिनाथ)** and is renowned as the *'Forest of Pillars'* (*स्तम्भों का वन*) with **1,444 unique sculpted marble pillars**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Lord Mahavira's premier temple is at Shri Mahavirji (Karauli); 84 refers to the 84-pillared cenotaph (*Chhatri*) of Bundi.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Parshvanatha temples exist at Lodhruva and Nakoda; 108 is a common prayer bead count.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Neminatha's famous Luna Vasahi temple is at Dilwara (Mount Abu).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Ranakpur = 1st Tirthankara Adinath + 1,444 Pillars + Architect Depak + Dharnak Shah (under Kumbha) on Maghai river.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Hill Forts — Kumbhalgarh & Katargarh — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which innermost citadel of <strong>Kumbhalgarh Fort</strong> was the personal residence of Maharana Kumbha and was described by Abul Fazl: <em>'This fort is built at such a tremendous height that when one looks up from below, the turban falls off one's head'</em>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Vijay Stambh (विजय स्तम्भ - Chittorgarh)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Katargarh (कटारगढ़ - "Eye of Mewar")</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Supari Mahal (सुपारी महल - Ranthambore)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Chokhelao Mahal (चोखेलाव महल - Mehrangarh)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Katargarh (कटारगढ़ - "Eye of Mewar")</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Katargarh is the highest innermost fort (citadel) of Kumbhalgarh, serving as Maharana Kumbha's private sanctuary and administrative viewpoint (hence nicknamed the *'Eye of Mewar'*). Maharana Pratap was born here on 9 May 1540 in the Badal Mahal.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Vijay Stambh?</strong> Vijay Stambh is the 9-story victory monument inside Chittorgarh Fort built by Kumbha to celebrate the 1437 Battle of Sarangpur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Supari Mahal?</strong> Supari Mahal is a historic palace inside Ranthambore Fort that uniquely houses a Hindu temple, Islamic mosque, and Christian church within the same complex.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Chokhelao Mahal?</strong> Chokhelao Mahal is a 16th-century palace inside Mehrangarh Fort (Jodhpur) built by Rao Maldeo, decorated with Ramayana-themed battle frescoes.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Turban falls off looking up = Katargarh (Kumbhalgarh); Supari Mahal (Temple-Mosque-Church) = Ranthambore; Chokhelao Mahal = Mehrangarh.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Temple Architecture — Gurjara-Pratihara Style — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>Someshwara Temple of Kiradu (किराडू का सोमेश्वर मंदिर, बाड़मेर)</strong>, hailed as the <em>'Khajuraho of Rajasthan' (राजस्थान का खजुराहो)</em>, is a sublime architectural masterpiece built in which classical Indian temple style?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Dravidian Style (द्रविड़ शैली - South Indian Vimana)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Gurjara-Pratihara / Mahu-Maru Style (गुर्जर-प्रतिहार / महा-मारू शैली)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Bhumija Style (भूमिज शैली - Udayeshwara style)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Indo-Saracenic Style (इंडो-सारसेनिक शैली)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Gurjara-Pratihara / Mahu-Maru Style (गुर्जर-प्रतिहार / महा-मारू शैली)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The Kiradu group of 5 temples (dating to the 11th–12th centuries under Parmar feudatories) represents the pinnacle of the **Gurjara-Pratihara / Mahu-Maru style** in western Rajasthan, especially the Someshwara Shiva Temple with its erotic sculptures and ornate pillars.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Dravidian Style?</strong> Dravidian style originated in South India; in Rajasthan, the Rangji Temple in Pushkar is built in the Dravidian Gopuram architectural style.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Bhumija Style?</strong> Bhumija is a sub-style of Nagara architecture characterized by miniature decorative spirelets (*Urushringas*); prime examples in Rajasthan are the **Mahanaleshwar Temple at Menal** and **Undeshwar Temple at Bijolia**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Indo-Saracenic?</strong> Indo-Saracenic is the late 19th-century colonial blend of British, Rajput, and Mughal architecture seen in Albert Hall Museum (Jaipur) and Umaid Bhawan (Jodhpur).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kiradu (Khajuraho of Raj) = Gurjara-Pratihara / Mahu-Maru; Pushkar Rangji = Dravidian; Menal Mahanaleshwar = Bhumija; Albert Hall = Indo-Saracenic.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


---

## 🎯 Chapter 6: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2016 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which six hill forts of Rajasthan were inscribed on the UNESCO World Heritage list in 2013?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Kumbhalgarh, Siwana, Jalore, Amer, Gagron, Lohagarh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Chittorgarh, Kumbhalgarh, Ranthambore, Gagron, Amer, Jaisalmer</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Ranthambore, Jaigarh, Nahargarh, Amer, Chittor, Gagron</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chittorgarh, Mehrangarh, Taragarh, Amer, Jaisalmer, Junagarh</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Chittorgarh, Kumbhalgarh, Ranthambore, Gagron, Amer, Jaisalmer</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> In June 2013 at Phnom Penh (Cambodia), 6 hill forts were inscribed.<br>
        <strong>Mnemonic:</strong> <strong>चीकू गाजर आम</strong> (Chikoo-Gajar-Aam) -> Chi (Chittorgarh), Ku (Kumbhalgarh), Ga (Gagron), Jar (Jaisalmer), R (Ranthambore), Aam (Amer).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC SI 2021 / 1st Grade 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Gagron Fort' (गागरोन दुर्ग - Jhalawar) is the finest prototype of which classification of fort (Durg) in Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Giri Durg (गिरि दुर्ग)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Vana Durg (वन दुर्ग)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Dhanvan Durg (धान्वन दुर्ग)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Jal Durg / Audak Durg (जल/औदक दुर्ग)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Jal Durg / Audak Durg (जल/औदक दुर्ग)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Gagron Fort is surrounded on three sides by the confluence of the **Ahu and Kali Sindh** rivers in Jhalawar. It stands directly on a natural rock ridge without foundation masonry.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / Patwari 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which fort has a 36-km-long defense wall (second longest in the world) and contains the inner citadel 'Katargarh' (कटारगढ़ - The Eye of Mewar)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Achalgarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Mehrangarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Chittorgarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Kumbhalgarh Fort</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Kumbhalgarh Fort</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Built by Maharana Kumbha on the designs of royal architect Mandan. The upper citadel *Katargarh* was Kumbha's personal residence and the birthplace of Maharana Pratap. Abul Fazl wrote: <em>"Looking up at this fort causes the turban to fall off one's head."</em>
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / RAS Pre 2015</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Lalgarh Palace' (लालगढ़ पैलेस) of Bikaner was constructed in red sandstone by Maharaja Ganga Singh in memory of:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> His father Maharaja Lal Singh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Maharaja Surat Singh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Rao Bika</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Maharaja Anup Singh</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) His father Maharaja Lal Singh</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Built between 1902–1926 by Maharaja Ganga Singh in memory of his father Maharaja Lal Singh. Designed by British architect Sir Samuel Swinton Jacob in Indo-Saracenic style.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following temple(s) is/are located in Amer? (A) Jagat Shiromani Temple, (B) Ambikeshwar Mahadev Temple, (C) Raktdantika Mata Temple
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Only (B)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A) and (B)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Only (A)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A), (B) and (C)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) (A) and (B)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Jagat Shiromani Temple was built by Rani Kanakwati (enshrining the Krishna idol worshipped by Meera Bai). Ambikeshwar Mahadev is the ancestral shrine from which Amer derives its name. Raktdantika Mata is situated in Satur, Bundi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q6</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2021 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Rani ji ki Baori' (रानी जी की बावड़ी) in Bundi — hailed as the "Queen of Stepwells" — was built in 1699 AD by:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Rani Padmini</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Rani Karmavati</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Rani Nathavati (रानी नाथावती)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Rani Kanakwati</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Rani Nathavati (रानी नाथावती)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Rani Nathavati, wife of Rao Raja Anirudh Singh and mother of Maharao Raja Budh Singh, built this ornate 3-storeyed stepwell in Bundi (the "City of Stepwells").
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q7</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / Constable 2020</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The breathtaking 8th-century stepwell 'Chand Baori' (चांद बावड़ी) featuring 3,500 geometric steps is located in:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Abhaneri (Dausa)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Osian (Jodhpur)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Neemrana (Kotputli-Behror)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Todaraisingh (Tonk)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Abhaneri (Dausa)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Built by King Chandra of the Nikumbha dynasty adjacent to the Harshat Mata temple in Abhaneri (Dausa). It is 13 storeys deep.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which fort constructed atop 'Cheel ka Tola' (Eagle's Hill) in Jaipur housed the cannon foundry and Asia's largest cannon-on-wheels, 'Jaivana' (जयबाण तोप)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Nahargarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Bala Qila</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Jaigarh Fort</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Amer Fort</div>
  </div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Built by Sawai Jai Singh II as a military fortress with a canon foundry (*Topkhana*). Jaivana cannon has a barrel length of 20 feet and was test-fired only once, creating the Golelao pond in Chaksu.

    </div>
  </details>
</div>

# Chapter 7: Folk Music, Folk Dances and Folk Drama of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के लोक संगीत, लोक नृत्य एवं लोक नाट्य)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Melodic Deserts (मरुधरा का संगीत)</div>
  <em>"When the searing hot Loo winds blow across Jaisalmer and Barmer, life seems quiet. But as dusk falls and the desert cools, the haunting, resonant notes of the <strong>Kamayacha (कामायचा)</strong> and <strong>Sarangi (सारंगी)</strong> rise from the dunes. Folk singing in Rajasthan was preserved by hereditary musician castes—the <strong>Langas (लांगा)</strong>, the <strong>Manganiyars (मांगणियार)</strong>, the <strong>Dhadhis (ढाढ़ी)</strong>, and the <strong>Kalbeliyas (कालबेलिया)</strong>. Their songs are not mere entertainment; they are living oral archives of medieval history, romance, separated lovers, and desert ecology."</em>
</div>

---

## 1. Classical Gharanas & Traditional Folk Singing

* **Kathak Dance (कथक नृत्य - Jaipur Gharana / जयपुर घराना):** Pioneer: **Bhanuji Maharaj (भानुजी महाराज)**; known for high-speed pirouettes (*Chakkars*), complex footwork (*Tatkar*), and *Bhav* (classical dance originated from Rajasthan temples).
* **Jaipur-Atrauli Gharana (जयपुर-अतरौली घराना):** Founded by **Ustad Alladiya Khan (उस्ताद अल्लादिया खां)** (exponents: *Kishori Amonkar, Kesarbai Kerkar*).
* **Maand Singing (मांड गायकी) & Official State Song:**
  * **"Kesariya Balam Aao Ni Padharo Mhare Desh" (केसरिया बालम आवो नी पधारो म्हारे देस):** Official State Song of Rajasthan (inviting the beloved/traveler to the motherland).
  * **Allah Jilai Bai (अल्लाह जिलाई बाई - Bikaner):** Immortalized *Kesariya Balam*; awarded **Padma Shri in 1982** and Royal Albert Hall Court Singer award. Other famous Maand singers: **Gavari Devi (गवरी देवी - Jodhpur/Pali)** and **Banno Begum (बन्नो बेगम - Jaipur)**.
* **Famous Folk Songs (RPSC High-Frequency Identifiers):**
  * **Kurjan (कूरजां):** Heartbreaking Viraha (separation / विरह गीत) song where a yearning wife sends love messages to her distant husband via the migratory Siberian crane (*Kurjan / कूरजां पक्षी*).
  * **Gorband (गोरबंद):** Rhythmic celebration of stitching the ornamental neck harness of a camel (ऊंट के गले का आभूषण).
  * **Suvatiya (सुवटिया):** Bhil woman's longing song sending a message through a parrot (*Suva / तोता*).
  * **Hichki (हिचकी):** Folk song of Mewat (Alwar) sung when remembering someone far away.
  * **Kamadh (कामण):** Sung to protect a bridegroom from black magic and the evil eye (जादू-टोने से बचाव).

---

## 2. Folk Dances of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के लोक नृत्य)

```
                                  FOLK DANCES OF RAJASTHAN (लोक नृत्य)
                                                     │
         ┌───────────────────┬───────────────────────┴───────────────────────┬───────────────────┐
         │                   │                                               │                   │
    STATE / HERITAGE     TRIBAL DANCES (जनजातीय नृत्य)                   PROFESSIONAL        REGIONAL DANCES
   (राज्य / शास्त्रीय)       │                                           (व्यावसायिक नृत्य)    (क्षेत्रीय नृत्य)
         │                   │                                               │                   │
 • Ghoomar (घूमर)         • Bhil: Gair, Gavri, Hathimana                  • Terah Taali       • Bam Dance (बमरसिया - Alwar/Brt)
 • Kalbeliya (कालबेलिया - • Garasia: Valar (वालर - No Inst.)              • Agni Dance        • Dhol Dance (ढोल - Jalore - Thaakna)
   UNESCO 2010)          • Sahariya: Shikari (शिकारी)                    • Chari Dance       • Gidar & Kachhi Ghodi (गीदड़ व
                         • Kanjar: Chakri, Dhakad (चकरी, धाकड़)           • Bhavai Dance        कच्छी घोड़ी - Shekhawati)
```

### Detailed Dance Profiles

* **Ghoomar (घूमर):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 State Dance of Rajasthan</span>
  * Revered as the **"Soul of Folk Dances"** (*Lok Nrityon ki Atma / लोक नृत्यों की आत्मा*) and the *Sirmour of Dances (नृत्यों का सिरमौर)*.
  * Graceful circular pirouettes of women wearing 80-kali skirts; set to an 8-beat rhythm called **Sawaai (सवाई)**.
* **Kalbeliya Dance (कालबेलिया नृत्य):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 UNESCO Heritage (2010)</span>
  * Sensuous snake-dance performed to **Pungi (पूंजी/बीन)** and **Khanjari (खंजरी)** by the nomadic snake-charmer community; globally popularized by **Padma Shri Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा)**.
  * 4 Sub-Dances: **Indoni (इण्डोणी)**, **Shankariya (शंकरिया - romantic couple dance)**, **Panihari (पणिहारी)**, **Bagariya (बागड़िया - performed while begging)**.
* **Terah Taali Dance (तेरह ताली नृत्य - Ramdevra):**
  * Performed exclusively by seated Kamad (कामुड़) women in devotion to Ramdevji.
  * **13 brass cymbals (Manjiras / मंजीरे)** tied on the body (9 on right leg, 2 on elbows, 2 held in hands); maestro: **Mangi Bai (मांगी बाई)**.
* **Valar Dance (वालर नृत्य - Garasia Tribe / गरासिया जनजाति):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 RPSC Favorite</span>
  * Slow, graceful circular dance performed **without any musical instruments whatsoever (बिना किसी वाद्य यंत्र के)** (dancers keep rhythm through hand-claps and foot-tapping).
* **Gavri / Rai Dance-Drama (गवरी / राई नृत्य - Bhil Tribe / भील जनजाति):**
  * 40-day sacred ritual dance-drama performed exclusively by Bhil men in Bhadrapada, reenacting the conflict between Lord Shiva and demon Bhasmasur (शिव-भस्मासुर कथा).
* **Agni Dance (अग्नि नृत्य - Katariasar, Bikaner):** Performed barefoot over live embers by Jasnathi Siddhs chanting *"Fateh Fateh" (फतेह-फतेह)*.
* **Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Kishangarh):** Gujjar women dance balancing 7 brass pots with flaming cottonseeds on their heads (master: **Phalku Bai / फलकू बाई**).
* **Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य):** Acrobatic dance balancing 8 to 10 earthen pots on the head while dancing on naked sword blades, broken glass, and brass plate edges (**Roop Singh Shekhawat / रूपसिंह शेखावत, Dayaram / दयाराम**).

---

## 3. Folk Drama of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के लोक नाट्य)

* **Turra-Kalangi Khayal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल - Mewar / Chittor):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Communal Harmony</span>
  * Created by Hindu saint **Tukangir (तुकनगीर)** (*Turra / Shiva*) and Muslim saint **Shah Ali (शाह अली)** (*Kalangi / Parvati*). Poetic duels (*Dangal / दंगल*) staged on grand *Akhadas (अखाड़े)*.
* **Kuchamani Khayal (कुचामणी ख्याल - Nagaur):** Pioneered by **Pt. Lachhiram (पं. लक्षीराम)**; operatic singing with open-air stages.
* **Rammat (रम्मत - Bikaner & Jaisalmer):** Staged on wooden community platforms (*Patta Sanskriti / पाटा संस्कृति*). In 1943, **Tej Kavi of Jaisalmer (तेज कवि जैसलमेरी)** authored the revolutionary play **Swatantra Bawni (स्वतंत्र बावनी)**, openly challenging British rule and presenting the book to Mahatma Gandhi.
* **Tamasha (तमाशा - Jaipur):** Open-air musical theater started under Sawai Pratap Singh by **Banshidhar Bhatt (बंशीधर भट्ट)**.
* **Chaar-Bait (चारबैंत - Tonk):** 300-year-old Pashtun musical poetry performed with a tambourine (*Dhaf / डफ*) in Tonk.

---

## ⚡ Chapter 7: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

| Dance / Drama / Song | Distinctive Core Feature | Key Region / Pioneer Exponent |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Ghoomar (घूमर)** | State Dance / 8-beat Sawaai rhythm | Rajasthan Statewide / Royal Court |
| **Kalbeliya (कालबेलिया)** | UNESCO 2010 / Pungi & Khanjari | Gulabo Sapera / Nomadic Tribes |
| **Terah Taali (तेरह ताली)** | 13 Manjiras tied on body (Kamad women) | Ramdevra / Mangi Bai |
| **Valar Dance (वालर)** | Circle dance without ANY instruments | Sirohi / Garasia Tribe |
| **Gavri Dance (गवरी)** | 40-day ritual drama (Shiva-Bhasmasur) | Mewar / Bhil Tribe |
| **Agni Dance (अग्नि नृत्य)** | Barefoot on coals (*"Fateh Fateh"*) | Katariasar (Bikaner) / Jasnathi |
| **Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य)** | Flaming brass pots on head | Kishangarh / Phalku Bai |
| **Turra-Kalangi (तुर्रा-कलंगी)** | Shiva-Parvati poetic duels (*Dangal*) | Chittorgarh / Tukangir & Shah Ali |
| **Swatantra Bawni (स्वतंत्र बावनी)** | Rammat play presented to Mahatma Gandhi | Jaisalmer / Tej Kavi (1943) |
| **Maand Raga (मांड गायकी)** | *Kesariya Balam* (Official State Song) | Bikaner / Allah Jilai Bai |




## 📝 Chapter 7: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Tribal Folk Dances & Performance Styles</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following folk dances of the Garasia tribe (गरासिया जनजाति) is performed in a slow, graceful circular formation <strong>without the accompaniment of any musical instrument</strong> (बिना किसी वाद्य यंत्र के)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Gair Dance (गैर नृत्य)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Mandal Dance (मांदल नृत्य)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Loor Dance (लूर नृत्य)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Valar Dance (वालर नृत्य)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Valar Dance (वालर नृत्य)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Valar is termed the "Ghoomar of the Garasias". Performed by men and women holding an umbrella or sword, it relies purely on rhythmic clapping and vocal cadence without any percussive musical instrument.<br>
        <strong>Examiner Trap:</strong> Gair is a Bhil dance using wooden sticks, whereas Valar is Garasia.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Jaipuri Tamasha (तमाशा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Kuchamani Khyal (कुचामणी ख्याल)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Shekhawati Khyal (शेखावाटी ख्याल)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Turra-Kalangi was founded ~400 years ago in Ghosunda/Chittorgarh by Hindu saint Tukangir (Turra - Shiva) and Muslim saint Shah Ali (Kalangi - Shakti/Parvati). The stage setup is called 'Dhangal' and the singing style is 'Ghammat'.
      </div>

  </details>
</div>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Mangi Bai Arya (मांगी बाई आर्य)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Gavari Bai (गवरी बाई)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Allah Jilai Bai (अल्लाह जिलाई बाई)</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Gulabo Sapera (Padma Shri recipient) brought Kalbelia dance to global prominence. The dance is performed to the musical accompaniment of <em>Pungi / Been</em> (पूंगी) and <em>Khanjari</em> (खंजरी). Sub-types include Indoni, Shankariya, Panihari, and Bagariya.

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Bhil Ritual Drama / Gavari</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Consider the following statements regarding the <strong>Gavari (गवरी / राई)</strong> dance-drama of Mewar:
    <br>1. It is a 40-day ritualistic folk drama performed exclusively by Bhil men during the month of Bhadrapada.
    <br>2. It is based on the mythological battle between Lord Shiva (Pashupati) and Demon Bhasmasura (Boodiya).
    <br>3. The central anchor-clown characters who link the story episodes are called 'Kutkudiya' and 'Pat-Bhomia'.
    <br>Which of the statements given above are correct?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 1 and 3 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 1, 2 and 3 (All correct)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 2 and 3 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 1 and 2 only</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) 1, 2 and 3 (All correct)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Gavari is India's oldest surviving tribal dance-drama (often termed 'Meru Natya' / मेरु नाट्य). During the 40 days of Gavari, performers maintain strict celibacy, abstain from alcohol/meat, sleep on the floor, and travel from village to village.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Tribal Folk Dances — Waler Dance of the Garasias — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which graceful circle dance of the <strong>Garasia tribe of Sirohi</strong>, celebrated as the <em>'Ghoomar of the Garasias'</em>, is performed in two concentric semicircles (men on outer, women on inner) by dancers holding swords/umbrellas <strong>WITHOUT the accompaniment of ANY musical instrument</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - सिरोही के गरासिया)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Gavari Dance (राई / गवरी नृत्य - Bhils)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Gujjars)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Chakri Dance (चकरी नृत्य - Kanjar women)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - सिरोही के गरासिया)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य)** is the iconic folk dance of the **Garasia tribe** (Mount Abu & Pindwara, Sirohi). Initiated by an elder carrying an umbrella (*Chhatri*) or sword, it progresses slowly to rhythmic hand clapping and foot-tapping **without any percussion or wind instruments**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Gavari?</strong> Gavari is a 40-day Bhil religious dance drama accompanied by the *Mandal* clay drum and *Thali*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Chari?</strong> Chari is a Gujjar dance accompanied by Dhol, Thali, and Bankiya.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Chakri?</strong> Chakri is a rapid 80-whirl spinning dance of Kanjar girls in Hadoti accompanied by Dholak and Manjira.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Dance WITHOUT ANY musical instrument = Waler (Garasias of Sirohi); 40-day Shiva-Bhasmasura drama = Gavari (Bhils of Mewar).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Theatre — Charbait Folk Opera of Tonk — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>

    The <strong>Charbait (चारबैत)</strong>, a distinctive 4-line Urdu/Pashto musical folk theatre performed in traditional military soldier costumes with dramatic rhythms of the <strong>Dhaf (डफ)</strong>, is the unique traditional theatre style of which former princely state of Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Bikaner (Rammat)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Tonk (टोंक का चारबैत - Started under Nawab Faizullah Khan by Khalifa Karim Khan)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Jaipur (Tamasha)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Alwar (Alibakshi Khyal)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Tonk (टोंक का चारबैत - Started under Nawab Faizullah Khan by Khalifa Karim Khan)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Charbait (चारबैत)** originated in Afghanistan and was introduced to the Nawabi princely state of **Tonk** during the reign of Nawab Faizullah Khan by Afghan soldier-artists **Abdul Karim Khan and Khalifa Karim Khan Nihang**. Performers recite four-line verses (*Char-bait*) in high-pitched voices while striking large handheld *Dhaf* tambourines.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Bikaner?</strong> Bikaner is the hub of *Rammat* folk drama on wooden platforms (*Pata Sanskriti*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jaipur?</strong> Jaipur is the home of *Tamasha* folk theatre (Banshidhar Bhatt).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Alwar?</strong> Alwar/Mundawar is the home of *Alibakshi Khyal*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Charbait (Pashto/Urdu folk opera with Dhaf) = Tonk; Rammat = Bikaner & Jaisalmer; Tamasha = Jaipur; Alibakshi = Alwar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Dramas — Turra-Kalangi Khyal (Chittorgarh) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In the 400-year-old popular <strong>Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल)</strong> of Ghosunda and Chittorgarh, what do <strong>'Turra'</strong> and <strong>'Kalangi'</strong> respectively represent?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Turra represents Lord Shiva (symbolized by Saffron Flag) and Kalangi represents Goddess Parvati (symbolized by Green Flag)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Turra represents Lord Rama and Kalangi represents Lord Krishna</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Turra represents the Sun and Kalangi represents the Moon</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Turra represents Emperor Akbar and Kalangi represents Maharana Pratap</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Turra represents Lord Shiva (symbolized by Saffron Flag) and Kalangi represents Goddess Parvati (symbolized by Green Flag)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Turra-Kalangi Khyal was founded by two saint-poets: Hindu saint **Tukangir** (who worshipped Lord Shiva and wore the royal crest *Turra* with saffron flag) and Muslim saint **Shah Ali** (who worshipped Goddess Shakti/Parvati and wore the crest *Kalangi* with green flag). In this folk opera, two opposing parties engage in spontaneous poetic musical duels (*Dangal*) atop an open wooden stage without written scripts.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Rama-Krishna folk operas are *Ram Lila* and *Ras Lila*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Sun-Moon symbolism is used in Hathayoga (*Ha-Tha*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Akbar-Pratap debates are modern historical enactments.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Turra = Shiva + Tukangir + Saffron flag; Kalangi = Parvati + Shah Ali + Green flag; Center = Ghosunda & Chittorgarh.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Dances — Bhavai Dance & Physical Stunts — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 2nd Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which thrilling professional folk dance of Rajasthan, founded by <strong>Bagaji of Kekri (Ajmer)</strong>, features dancers balancing <strong>7 to 11 earthen/brass pots</strong> on their heads while balancing barefoot on the <strong>edges of naked swords and broken glass shards</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Gujjar women)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य - Bagaji of Kekri)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Kacchi Ghodi Dance (कच्छी घोड़ी नृत्य - Shekhawati)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Gair Dance (गैर नृत्य - Bhils)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य - Bagaji of Kekri)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य)** (pioneered by Bagaji and Kankaji of Kekri) is Rajasthan's supreme dance of physical balance and daring acrobatics. Dancers balance towering columns of 8–11 brass pitchers (*Matkas*), pick handkerchiefs with their eyelids from the floor, and dance atop sword blades, brass *Thali* rims, and crushed glass shards. Famous artists: **Roop Singh Shekhawat, Dayaram, and Shrestha Soni**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Chari Dance?</strong> Chari dance is performed with brass pots containing burning cottonseeds on the head (*Falgu Bai*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Kacchi Ghodi?</strong> Kacchi Ghodi is a dummy horse mock-battle dance from Shekhawati.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Gair Dance?</strong> Gair is a community circle dance of Bhil men using wooden sticks (*Khanda*).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Dancing on sword edge / 8-11 pots = Bhavai (Bagaji of Kekri); Flaming pots = Chari (Falgu Bai); Dummy horse = Kacchi Ghodi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Dances — Tribal Waler Dance (Garasia of Sirohi) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which graceful folk dance of the <strong>Garasia tribe of Mount Abu / Sirohi</strong> is famously known as the <em>'Ghoomar of the Garasias'</em> and is performed strictly <strong>WITHOUT the accompaniment of any musical instrument (बिना किसी वाद्य यंत्र के)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Gair Dance (गैर नृत्य - Bhils with wooden sticks)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - Garasias of Sirohi)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Agni Dance (अग्नि नृत्य - Jasnathi Siddhs)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य - Gujjar women)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य - Garasias of Sirohi)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Waler Dance (वालर नृत्य)** is performed by Garasia men and women in two concentric semicircles. A male leader begins the rhythm with an open umbrella (*Chhata*) or sword. Uniquely in Rajasthan folk dance, **no drums, flutes, or musical instruments are used**—the rhythm is kept entirely by vocal clapping and coordinated footwork.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Gair Dance?</strong> Gair is performed with heavy wooden sticks (*Khanda*) accompanied by loud *Dhol*, *Bankiya*, and *Thali*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Agni Dance?</strong> Agni dance is performed to the loud beating of the *Nagada* drum at Katariasar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Chari Dance?</strong> Chari dance is performed with brass pots accompanied by the *Dhol*, *Bankiya*, and *Thali*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Dance WITHOUT any musical instrument = Waler Dance (Garasia tribe, Sirohi); Dummy horse dance = Kacchi Ghodi; 40-day harvest dance = Gavari.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Theatre — Rammat of Bikaner & Jaisalmer (Tez Kavi) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In 1943 during the Quit India Movement, which revolutionary folk poet authored the anti-British Rammat play <strong>'Swatantra Bawani' (स्वतंत्र बावनी)</strong> and boldly gifted a copy directly to <strong>Mahatma Gandhi</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Banshidhar Bhatt (बंशीधर भट्ट - Jaipur Tamasha)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Lachhiram (लच्छीराम - Kuchamani Khyal)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Tez Kavi of Jaisalmer (तेज कवि जैसलमेरी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Janakilal Bhand (जानकीलाल भांड - Behrupia artist)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Tez Kavi of Jaisalmer (तेज कवि जैसलमेरी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Folk playwright **Tez Kavi (तेज कवि)** of Jaisalmer founded the *Shri Krishna Company* and penned *'Swatantra Bawani'* in 1943, presenting it to Mahatma Gandhi. When British police came to arrest him, he famously challenged the commissioner outside the police station: <em>'कमिश्नर खोल दरवाजा, हमें भी जेल जाना है। हिंद परदेसियों का है, न तेरे बाप का खाना है॥'</em>.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Banshidhar Bhatt?</strong> Banshidhar Bhatt was the pioneer of the **Tamasha** musical theatre of Jaipur.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Lachhiram?</strong> Lachhiram was the pioneer who codified the **Kuchamani Khyal** (author of *Chandmal*, *Rao Ridmal*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Janakilal Bhand?</strong> Janakilal Bhand of Bhilwara ('Monkey Man') is the living master of the **Behrupia (स्वांग)** masquerade art.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Swatantra Bawani (1943) gifted to Gandhi = Tez Kavi (Rammat, Jaisalmer); Kuchamani Khyal = Lachhiram; Tamasha = Banshidhar Bhatt; Behrupia = Janakilal Bhand.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Dances — Kalbelia & UNESCO World Heritage — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which world-renowned female exponent from Rajasthan propelled the serpent-mimicking <strong>Kalbelia Dance (कालबेलिया नृत्य)</strong> onto the global stage, leading to its inscription in <strong>UNESCO's Representative List of Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in 2010</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Falgu Bai of Kishangarh (फलकू बाई - Chari Dance)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा - Kalbelia Dance)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Gavari Bai of Dungarpur (गवरी बाई - Bhakti Singer)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Shreshtha Soni of Udaipur (श्रेष्ठा सोनी - Bhavai Dance)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Gulabo Sapera (गुलाबो सपेरा - Kalbelia Dance)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Padma Shri **Gulabo Sapera** from Ajmer brought the nomadic Kalbelia snake charmer dance to international fame. The dance features black swirling skirts, agile acrobatic flexibility, and is accompanied by the *Pungi (Been)* and *Khanjari*. In 2010, UNESCO officially recognized it as Intangible Cultural Heritage.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Falgu Bai?</strong> Falgu Bai of Kishangarh is the master dancer of the **Chari Dance (चरी नृत्य)** of the Gujjar community, balancing brass pots with burning cottonseeds.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Gavari Bai?</strong> Gavari Bai of Dungarpur is a saintly 18th-century devotional singer revered as the *'Meera of Vagad' (वागड़ की मीरा)*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Shreshtha Soni?</strong> Shreshtha Soni (along with Dayaram and Tara Sharma) is a famous exponent of the **Bhavai Dance (भवाई नृत्य)** who set world records balancing brass pots on her head.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kalbelia & UNESCO (2010) = Gulabo Sapera; Chari Dance = Falgu Bai; Meera of Vagad = Gavari Bai; Bhavai = Shreshtha Soni / Dayaram.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Theatre — Turra Kalangi Tradition — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC Assistant Professor & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In the 400-year-old <strong>Turra-Kalangi Khyal (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल)</strong> folk opera tradition of Ghosunda and Chittorgarh, the <strong>'Turra' (तुर्रा)</strong> and <strong>'Kalangi' (कलंगी)</strong> emblems symbolize which two divine cosmic principles?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Turra = Lord Rama, Kalangi = Lord Krishna</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Turra = Lord Shiva (Hindu saint Tukangir), Kalangi = Goddess Shakti (Muslim saint Shah Ali)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Turra = Lord Vishnu, Kalangi = Goddess Lakshmi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Turra = Surya Dev, Kalangi = Chandra Dev</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Turra = Lord Shiva (Hindu saint Tukangir), Kalangi = Goddess Shakti (Muslim saint Shah Ali)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Turra-Kalangi was created in Chanderi when Hindu saint **Tukangir** (who worshipped Shiva - Turra crest) and Sufi saint **Shah Ali** (who worshipped Shakti - Kalangi crest) engaged in friendly poetic duels. The ruler of Chanderi awarded them royal crowns with Turra and Kalangi. In Rajasthan, Ghosunda, Nimbahera, and Chittorgarh are its primary centers, performed as an unscripted poetic competition (*Dangal*) using the Chang instrument.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A)?</strong> Vaishnava legends of Rama and Krishna are the primary themes of *Rammat* and *Rasleela*, not Turra-Kalangi.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Lakshmi-Narayan themes predominate in classical Temple Dhrupad music.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Solar and lunar motifs are dynastic symbols, not the theological basis of Turra-Kalangi.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Turra = Shiva (Tukangir); Kalangi = Shakti (Shah Ali); Main stage = Dangal; Key instrument = Chang.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


---

## 🎯 Chapter 7: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2016, 2021 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The sensational 'Kalbeliya Dance' (कालबेलिया नृत्य) was inscribed on UNESCO's Representative List of Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in which year?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 2008</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 2010</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 2012</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 2015</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) 2010</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Performed by women of the snake-charmer community dressed in black embroidered skirts (*Ghaghra*) replicating serpentine movements to the tune of Pungi and Khanjari. Gulabo Sapera brought it international acclaim.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Terah Taali Dance' (तेरह ताली नृत्य) is traditionally performed by women of the Kamada community in devotion to which folk deity?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Pabuji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Gogaji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Tejaji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Baba Ramdevji</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Baba Ramdevji</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Dancers tie 13 cymbals (*Manjiras*) across their body (9 on the right leg, 2 on elbows, 2 in hands) and strike them with incredible dexterity while sitting and balancing pots/swords. Famous dancer: Mangi Bai.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC Patwari 2021 / Constable 2020</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which dance is celebrated as the "State Dance of Rajasthan" (राज्य नृत्य) and the "Sirmaur of all folk dances" (नृत्यों का सिरमौर)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Gair</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Kachhi Ghodi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Ghoomar (घूमर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chari</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Ghoomar (घूमर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Performed on auspicious occasions like Gangaur and Teej with 8 rhythmic footsteps called *Sawai* (सवाई) and graceful swirling movements (*Ghoom*).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / RAS Pre 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The traditional folk theatre 'Turra-Kalangi Khyal' (तुर्रा-कलंगी ख्याल) features poetic dialogues between:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Shiva (Turra) and Shakti/Parvati (Kalangi)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Krishna and Radha</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Rama and Ravana</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> King and Minister</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Shiva (Turra) and Shakti/Parvati (Kalangi)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Originated ~400 years ago when the ruler of Chanderi honored Tukangir (Hindu saint representing Shiva / Saffron flag) with *Turra* and Shah Ali (Muslim saint representing Shakti / Green flag) with *Kalangi*. Popular in Ghosunda and Chittor.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC Assistant Professor 2021 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Tamasha' (तमाशा) folk theatre of Jaipur was patronized during the reign of which Kachwaha ruler?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Sawai Man Singh II</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Sawai Jai Singh II</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Sawai Pratap Singh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Sawai Ram Singh II</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Sawai Pratap Singh</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Introduced to Jaipur from Maharashtra by the Bhatt family (Banshidhar Bhatt and Gopiji Bhatt) during the reign of Sawai Pratap Singh (1778–1803 AD). It is performed on an open stage called *Akhada*.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Pundarik Vitthal</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Ali Baksh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Lachhiram</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Bagaji Jat of Kekri</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Bagaji Jat of Kekri</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Involves balancing 7 to 9 brass/earthen pitchers on the head while dancing on edge of swords, brass plates, and glass shards. Renowned dancers: Shreshtha Soni, Roop Singh Shekhawat, Tara Sharma.
      </div>
    </div>

</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Lachhiram created poetic folk operas such as *Chand Malagiri*, *Rao Ridmal*, and *Meera Mangal*. Performed with forceful operatic singing accompanied by Shehnai and Dholak.
