# Chapter 8: Folk Musical Instruments of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के लोक वाद्य यंत्र)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The 4-Way Classification (वाद्य यंत्रों का वर्गीकरण)</div>
  <em>"In ancient Indian musicology (Bharata Muni’s Natyashastra - भरत मुनि का नाट्यशास्त्र), musical instruments are classified into four scientific families based on acoustic vibration. In RPSC exams, 90% of questions are simple identification tests: 'Which of the following is a Tat / Sushir / Avanaddha / Ghan instrument?' Memorize the four categories and you will never lose a mark here."</em>
</div>

---

## The 4 Scientific Instrument Families (वाद्य यंत्रों के 4 प्रकार)

```
                           4 INSTRUMENT FAMILIES OF RAJASTHAN (लोक वाद्य)
                                                 │
         ┌───────────────────────┬───────────────┴───────────────┬───────────────────────┐
         │                       │                               │                       │
     TAT VADYA              SUSHIR VADYA                    AVANADDHA VADYA          GHAN VADYA
 (तत वाद्य - Stringed)   (सुषिर वाद्य - Wind/Air)        (अवनद्ध वाद्य - Membrane) (घन वाद्य - Metallic)
         │                       │                               │                       │
 • Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था)• Algoza (अलगोजा - State Inst.) • Mridang / Pakhawaj     • Khartal (खड़ताल - Siddique Khan)
 • Kamayacha (कामायचा)   • Shehnai (शहनाई)               • Mandal (मांदल - Molela) • Manjira (मंजीरा - 13 in Terah Taali)
 • Jantar (जन्तर)        • Pungi / Been (पूंजी/बीन)      • Chang & Dhaf (चंग व डफ) • Jhanjh (झांझ - Kachhi Ghodi)
 • Sarangi (सारंगी)      • Morchang (मोरचंग - Jew's harp) • Nagada & Naubat (नगाड़ा)• Chimta & Thali (चिमटा व थाली)
 • Bhapang (भपंग)        • Bankiya & Nagphani (बांकिया)  • Tasha (ताशा)
```

---

### Detailed Category Profiles

1. **Tat Vadya (तत वाद्य - Chordophones / Stringed):**
   * **Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था):** Half-cut coconut shell covered with goat hide, bamboo shaft, 9 strings. Played with horsehair bow (*Gaj / गज*) by Bhopas narrating **Pabuji ki Phad (पाबूजी की फड़)**.
   * **Kamayacha (कामायचा):** Single block of mango wood, 16–17 strings, wide parchment belly. Soul of **Manganiyar music** (**Padma Shri Sakar Khan / साकर खां मांगणियार**).
   * **Jantar (जन्तर):** Two round gourds, 5–6 strings. Hung around neck and played standing by Gurjar Bhopas narrating **Devnarayanji ki Phad (देवनारायण जी की फड़)**.
   * **Sarangi (सारंगी):** Most melodious bowed instrument with 27–29 gut strings; played by **Langa musicians (लांगा गायक)**.
   * **Bhapang (भपंग):** Single string plucked gourd instrument of Mewat (**Zahoor Khan Mewati / जहूर खां मेवाती**).
2. **Sushir Vadya (सुषिर वाद्य - Aerophones / Wind):**
   * **Algoza (अलगोजा):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 State Musical Instrument</span> Pair of matching beaked flutes played simultaneously in mouth with continuous circular breathing (राजस्थान का राज्य वाद्य यंत्र).
   * **Morchang (मोरचंग):** Smallest iron instrument (Jew's harp); held in teeth while blowing and plucking the iron tongue.
   * **Shehnai (शहनाई), Pungi (पूंजी/बीन), Bankiya (बांकिया), Satara (सतारा), Nagphani (नागफणी), Mashak (मशक - Bagpipe), Shankh (शंख)**.
3. **Avanaddha Vadya (अवनद्ध वाद्य - Membranophones / Percussion Drums):**
   * **Mridang / Pakhawaj (मृदंग / पखावज):** Barrel drum (**Padma Shri Pt. Purushottam Das / पं. पुरुषोत्तम दास**).
   * **Mandal (मांदल):** Earthen drum hand-molded in **Molela (मोलेला - Rajsamand)**; played in tribal dances.
   * **Chang & Dhaf (चंग व डफ):** Circular wooden frame covered with sheepskin played during Shekhawati Holi.
   * **Nagada & Naubat (नगाड़ा व नौबत):** Massive kettle drums at fort/temple gates (**Ramkishan Solanki / रामकिशन सोलंकी**).
4. **Ghan Vadya (घन वाद्य - Idiophones / Metallic Resonance):**
   * **Khartal (खड़ताल):** Pair of polished Sheesham clappers (**Siddique Khan Manganiyar / सिद्दीक खां मांगणियार** - *Khartal Wizard*).
   * **Manjira (मंजीरा):** 13 brass cymbals used in *Terah Taali*.
   * **Jhanjh (झांझ):** Large bronze cymbals clapped in *Kachhi Ghodi* dance.

---

## ⚡ Chapter 8: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

| Category | Instruments Included | Key Identifying Feature |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Tat (तत - String)** | **Ravanhattha, Kamayacha, Jantar, Sarangi, Bhapang** | *Ravanhattha* = Pabuji Phad \| *Jantar* = Devnarayanji Phad |
| **Sushir (सुषिर - Wind)**| **Algoza, Shehnai, Pungi, Morchang, Bankiya, Mashak**| *Algoza* = **State Instrument** (twin flutes) \| *Morchang* = Jew's harp |
| **Avanaddha (अवनद्ध - Drum)**| **Mridang, Mandal, Chang, Nagada, Naubat, Tasha** | *Mandal* = Molela earthen drum \| *Chang* = Shekhawati Holi |
| **Ghan (घन - Metal)** | **Khartal, Manjira, Jhanjh, Chimta, Thali** | *Khartal* = Siddique Khan \| *Manjira* = 13 in Terah Taali |




## 📝 Chapter 8: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Classification of Rajasthani Musical Instruments</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following is the state musical instrument of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का राज्य वाद्य यंत्र) that belongs to the <strong>Sushir (Wind / फूँक वाद्य)</strong> category and consists of a pair of identical flutes played simultaneously?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Morchang (मोरचंग)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Algoza (अलगोजा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Shehnai (शहनाई)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Satara (सतारा)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Algoza (अलगोजा)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Algoza is the official state instrument of Rajasthan. It features two beak-flutes blown together (one provides tonic drone while the other plays the melody). Renowned players include Ramnath Chaudhary (who famously plays Algoza using his nostrils).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Tat & Avanaddh Categorization</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Consider the following musical instruments:
    <br>1. Kamayacha (कामायचा)
    <br>2. Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था)
    <br>3. Bhapang (भपंग)
    <br>4. Khartal (खड़ताल)
    <br>Which of the instruments given above belong to the <strong>Tat Vadya (Stringed / तार वाद्य)</strong> category?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 1 and 2 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 2, 3 and 4 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 1, 2 and 3 only</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 1, 2, 3 and 4</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) 1, 2 and 3 only</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Kamayacha (bowed string with 17 strings), Ravanhattha (half coconut shell with 9 strings), and Bhapang (plucked single-string drum-tumbi) are all Tat Vadya. Khartal is a <em>Ghan Vadya</em> (Idiophone / wooden clapper) whose legendary virtuoso was Siddique Khan Manganiyar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Instrument Virtuosos</span>
  </div>

    <br><strong>List-I (Instrument)</strong>
    <br>(A) Kamayacha (कामायचा)
    <br>(B) Bhapang (भपंग)
    <br>(C) Khartal (खड़ताल)
    <br>(D) Pakhawaj / Mridang (पखावज)
    <br><strong>List-II (Maestro)</strong>
    <br>(i) Zahoor Khan Mewati & Umar Farooq Mewati
    <br>(ii) Sakar Khan Manganiyar (पद्मश्री)
    <br>(iii) Pandit Purushottam Das of Nathdwara
    <br>(iv) Siddique Khan Manganiyar
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(iii), (C)-(iv), (D)-(i)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> (A)-(iv), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iii)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong>
        <br>• Kamayacha = Sakar Khan (Padma Shri 2012).
        <br>• Bhapang = Zahoor Khan Mewati ("Jadugar of Bhapang").
        <br>• Khartal = Siddique Khan Manganiyar ("Jadugar of Khartal").
        <br>• Pakhawaj = Pandit Purushottam Das of Nathdwara (Padma Shri).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Dhonsa (धौंसा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Daph (डफ)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Mandal (मांदल)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chang (चंग)</div>
  </div>

    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>

### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Instruments — Bhapang & Zahoor Khan Mewati — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>Bhapang (भपंग)</strong>, an ancient Tat (stringed) percussion instrument shaped like a hollow cylindrical tumba/gourd with a single gut string plucked while varying tension under the armpit, was popularized globally by which legendary master of the Mewat region (Alwar)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Padma Shri Sakar Khan (Kamayacha maestro)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Ramnath Chaudhary (Algoza player from nose)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Zahoor Khan Mewati & Umar Farooq Mewati (जहूर खां मेवाती एवं उमर फारूक मेवाती - Bhapang maestros)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Padma Shri Siddique Khan (Khartal maestro)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Zahoor Khan Mewati & Umar Farooq Mewati (जहूर खां मेवाती एवं उमर फारूक मेवाती - Bhapang maestros)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Zahoor Khan Mewati</strong> is immortalized as the <em>'Magician of the Bhapang'</em> (भपंग का जादूगर), along with his illustrious successor **Umar Farooq Mewati**. The Bhapang is played extensively by Jogi singers in Alwar/Mewat while narrating the epics of Raja Gopichand and Raja Bharthari.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Sakar Khan?</strong> Sakar Khan was the Padma Shri maestro of the **Kamayacha** in Jaisalmer.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Ramnath Chaudhary?</strong> Ramnath Chaudhary of Jaipur is famous for playing the **Algoza through his nostrils**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Siddique Khan?</strong> Siddique Khan was the *'Magician of Khartal'*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Bhapang maestro = Zahoor Khan Mewati (Alwar); Kamayacha maestro = Sakar Khan (Jaisalmer); Khartal maestro = Siddique Khan; Nose Algoza = Ramnath Chaudhary.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Instruments — Kamayacha & Padma Shri Sakar Khan — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & College Lecturer</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>Kamayacha (कमायचा)</strong>, a majestic 27-stringed bowed instrument carved out of a single solid piece of mango wood with a goatskin-covered resonator and regarded as the <em>'King of Bowed Instruments'</em>, is the hallmark instrument of which traditional Muslim folk musician community of the Thar Desert?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Langa Community (लांगा समुदाय - Sarangi masters)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Kalbelia Community (कालबेलिया - Pungi masters)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Manganiyar Community (मांगणियार समुदाय - Master: Padma Shri Sakar Khan)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Bhopas of Pabuji (भोपा - Ravanhattha masters)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Manganiyar Community (मांगणियार समुदाय - Master: Padma Shri Sakar Khan)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Kamayacha** is played exclusively by the **Manganiyar community** of Jaisalmer and Barmer. Bowed with horsehair (*Gaj*), its 27 steel and gut strings produce deep resonant harmonies accompanying desert songs. Legend **Padma Shri Sakar Khan** (awarded 2012) was the greatest maestro of Kamayacha in world history.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Langas?</strong> The Muslim Langa community of Jodhpur/Barmer specializes in the **Sindhi Sarangi** and **Gujrati Sarangi**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Kalbelias?</strong> Kalbelias play the **Pungi (Been)** and **Khanjari**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Bhopas?</strong> Nayak Bhopas play the **Ravanhattha**.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kamayacha = Manganiyars (Sakar Khan); Sarangi = Langas; Ravanhattha = Pabuji Nayak Bhopas; Jantar = Devnarayanji Gurjar Bhopas.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Instruments — Percussion Avanaddha (Mandal & Chang) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The <strong>Mandal (मांदल)</strong>, an ancient folk percussion instrument made out of <strong>baked terracotta clay</strong> from the artisan village of Molela and played by Bhils during the 40-day Gavari dance drama, belongs to which category of Indian musical instruments?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Tat Vadya (Chordophone / String Instrument)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Ghana Vadya (Idiophone / Solid metal-wood Instrument)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Sushir Vadya (Aerophone / Wind Instrument)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Avanaddha Vadya (Membranophone / Leather-covered percussion instrument - अवनद्ध वाद्य)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Avanaddha Vadya (Membranophone / Leather-covered percussion instrument - अवनद्ध वाद्य)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Mandal (मांदल)</strong> is an **Avanaddha Vadya (अवनद्ध / ताल वाद्य)**. Its barrel-shaped body is sculpted out of baked red clay by Molela potters and both drumheads are covered with goat parchment skin. It is played with hands during the Bhil *Gavari* and Garasia *Mandal* dances.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Tat?</strong> *Tat* instruments use vibrating strings (Ravanhattha, Kamayacha, Jantar, Bhapang).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Ghana?</strong> *Ghana* instruments are solid metal/wood struck together (Khartal, Manjira, Thali, Chimta).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Sushir?</strong> *Sushir* instruments are blown with breath (Algoza, Shehnai, Pungi, Bankiya, Morchang).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Clay drum = Mandal (Avanaddha); State Instrument = Algoza (Sushir); 4 wooden clappers = Khartal (Ghana); 9 strings coconut = Ravanhattha (Tat).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Musical Instruments — Official State Instrument (Algoza) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which instrument is officially designated as the <strong>'State Musical Instrument of Rajasthan' (राजस्थान का राज्य वाद्य यंत्र)</strong>, consisting of a pair of joined beak flutes blown simultaneously through circular breathing?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Shehnai (शहनाई)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Bankiya (बांकिया)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Pungi / Been (पूंजी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Algoza (अलगोजा - Double beak flute)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Algoza (अलगोजा - Double beak flute)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Algoza (अलगोजा)** is the **Official State Musical Instrument of Rajasthan**. It comprises two hollow bamboo or Sheesham wood flutes (each with 4 holes) played simultaneously in the mouth. Master musician **Ramnath Chaudhary** of Padampura (Jaipur) gained worldwide fame by playing the Algoza using his nostrils / nose!<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Shehnai?</strong> Shehnai is a conical classical aerophone played during auspicious weddings.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Bankiya?</strong> Bankiya is a curved brass trumpet played by the Sargara community.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Pungi?</strong> Pungi (Been) is crafted from a dried bottle-gourd used by Kalbelia snake charmers.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> State Musical Instrument = Algoza (Double flute); Nose player = Ramnath Chaudhary; 27-stringed bowed lute = Kamayacha (Sakar Khan).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Musical Instruments — Bhapang of Mewat — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which single-stringed plucked gourd instrument belonging to the <strong>Tat Vadya (Chordophone)</strong> category is famously associated with <strong>Zahoor Khan Mewati (जहूर खां मेवाती)</strong> as the 'King of the Instrument'?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Kamayacha (कमायचा - Played by Manganiyars)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Bhapang (भपंग - Mewati folk instrument)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Ravanhattha (रावणहत्था - Pabuji Phad)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Jantar (जन्तर - Devnarayanji Phad)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Bhapang (भपंग - Mewati folk instrument)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Bhapang (भपंग)** is a plucked stringed instrument crafted from a hollow dry bottle-gourd (*Tumbe*) covered with goat skin and played with a bamboo plectrum under the armpit. **Zahoor Khan Mewati** and **Umar Farooq Mewati** of Alwar are its legendary world masters.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Kamayacha?</strong> The Kamayacha is a 27-stringed bowed instrument made of mango wood, played by Manganiyar folk masters like Padma Shri Sakar Khan of Barmer/Jaisalmer.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Ravanhattha?</strong> The Ravanhattha is a 9-stringed bow-and-coconut instrument played by Nayak Bhopas to recite the Phad of Pabuji.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Jantar?</strong> The Jantar is an ancient veena-like instrument with two gourds and 5 or 6 strings, played resting on the chest by Gurjar Bhopas during the Phad of Devnarayanji.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Bhapang = Mewat (Zahoor Khan); Kamayacha = Marwar (Sakar Khan); Jantar = Devnarayanji Phad (Gurjars); Ravanhattha = Pabuji Phad.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Folk Musical Instruments — Percussion & Idiophones — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC Senior Teacher & SI</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following Rajasthani musical instruments is classified as an <strong>Avanaddha Vadya (Membranophone / अवनद्ध वाद्य — Percussion with Leather Skin)</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Khartal (खड़ताल - Wood clapper idiophone)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Morchang (मोरचंग - Iron Jew's harp wind instrument)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Mandal / Madal (मांदल - Terracotta clay drum)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Manjira (मंजीरा - Brass cymbal idiophone)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Mandal / Madal (मांदल - Terracotta clay drum)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Mandal (मांदल)** is a traditional barrel drum made of a baked clay body (crafted by the potters of Molela) and covered with animal skin on both ends, played by Bhil and Garasia tribes (Gavari and Gair dances).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Khartal?</strong> Khartal is a solid idiophone (**Ghana Vadya / घन वाद्य**) made of four sheesham wood clappers.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Morchang?</strong> Morchang is an iron wind instrument (**Sushir Vadya / सुषिर वाद्य**) held between the teeth and blown with the mouth (Jew's harp).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Manjira?</strong> Manjira is a pair of brass cymbals classified under **Ghana Vadya (घन वाद्य)**, used in Terah Taali and devotional Bhajans.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Mandal/Chang/Dhol/Dhak = Avanaddha (Skin); Manjira/Khartal/Thali/Chimta = Ghana (Metal/Wood); Morchang/Algoza = Sushir (Wind); Ravanhattha/Bhapang = Tat (String).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

## 🎯 Chapter 8: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022 / RAS Pre 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following is/are Sushir (Aerophone / Wind) musical instrument(s) of Rajasthani folk music? (A) Algoza, (B) Bhapang, (C) Satara, (D) Nad
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Only (A)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A) and (B)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> (A), (C) and (D)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A), (B) and (C)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) (A), (C) and (D)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Algoza, Satara, and Nad are Sushir (wind) instruments blown with air. Bhapang is a Tat (stringed / chordophone) instrument played under the armpit!
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

# Chapter 9: Rajasthani Language and Literature (राजस्थानी भाषा और साहित्य)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Rich Idiom of the Dunes (मरुवाणी का वैभव)</div>
  <em>"Rajasthani is not a broken dialect of Hindi; it is an ancient, independent Indo-Aryan language with a literary heritage older than modern Hindi and Bengali! From the heroic Dingal (डिंगल) war poems chanted before Rajput cavalry charges to the soul-stirring couplets of Kripa Ram Khidiya's (कृपाराम खिड़िया) Rajiya, Rajasthani literature combined fiery courage with deep practical wisdom."</em>
</div>

---

## 1. Linguistic Origin & Dialect Classification (भाषा की उत्पत्ति एवं वर्गीकरण)

* **Evolutionary Lineage:**
  $$\text{Vedic Sanskrit (वैदिक संस्कृत)} \longrightarrow \text{Prakrit (शौरसेनी प्राकृत)} \longrightarrow \text{Maru-Gurjari Apabhramsa (मरु-गुर्जरी अपभ्रंश)} \longrightarrow \text{Rajasthani / Marubhasha (राजस्थानी / मरुभाषा)}$$
* **First Textual Evidence:** **Udyottan Suri (उद्योतन सूरि - 778 AD / VS 835)** mentioned **'Marubhasha' (मरुभाषा)** among 18 native languages (*Desi Bhashas / देशी भाषाएं*) in his classic work **Kuvalayamala (कुवलयमाला)**.
* **Coined by George Abraham Grierson (जॉर्ज अब्राहम ग्रियर्सन - 1912):** First linguist to use the formal collective term **'Rajasthani' (राजस्थानी)** in his monumental work *Linguistic Survey of India (भारतीय भाषा सर्वेक्षण)*.

---

## 2. Major Regional Dialects (प्रमुख बोलियां) <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Match Table</span>

* **Western Dialects (Dingal Literary Tradition / डिंगल साहित्य शैली):**
  * **Marwari (मारवाड़ी):** Standard literary language of Western Rajasthan (Jodhpur, Nagaur, Jaisalmer, Pali, Barmer). Sub-dialects: *Thali (थली), Godwadi (गोडवाड़ी)*.
  * **Mewari (मेवाड़ी):** Spoken in Udaipur, Chittorgarh, Rajsamand, Bhilwara.
  * **Wagdi / Bagri (वागड़ी / बागड़ी):** Spoken in Dungarpur and Banswara (strong Gujarati influence; Grierson called it **"Bhili dialect" / भीली बोली**).
* **Eastern Dialects (Pingal Literary Tradition / पिंगल साहित्य शैली):**
  * **Dhundhari (ढूंढाड़ी):** Spoken in Jaipur, Dausa, Tonk; characterized by the auxiliary verb **'Chhe' (छै / छो)**. Sub-dialects: *Torawati (तोरावाटी), Rajawati (राजावाटी)*.
  * **Hadoti (हाड़ौती):** Spoken in Kota, Bundi, Jhalawar, Baran. Also uses *'Chhe'*; Suryamal Misan composed in Hadoti.
  * **Mewati (मेवाती):** Spoken in Alwar and Bharatpur (Braj influence); Saint Laldas (लालदास जी) & Charandas (चरणदास जी) preached in Mewati.
  * **Ahirwati / Rathi (अहीरवाटी / राठी):** Spoken by Yadavs in Behror, Kotkasim, Mundawar (Alwar).

---

## 3. Four Eras & Specific Literary Genres (साहित्य के काल एवं विधाएं)

1. **Ancient / Viragatha Period (आरंभिक / वीरगाथा काल - 1050–1450 AD):** Heroic ballads (*Rannmal Chhand / रणमल छंद* by Shridhar Vyas / श्रीधर व्यास).
2. **Pre-Medieval / Bhakti Period (पूर्व-मध्य / भक्ति काल - 1450–1650 AD):** *Veli Kisan Rukmani Ri (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)* by **Prithviraj Rathore (पृथ्वीराज राठौड़ / पीथल)** (hailed as the **"5th Veda & 19th Purana" / पाँचवाँ वेद व 19वाँ पुराण** by Durasa Arha / दुरसा आढ़ा); *Mirabai Padavali (मीराबाई की पदावली)*; *Hariraso (हरिरस)* by Isardas (ईसरदास जी).
3. **Post-Medieval / Niti Period (उत्तर-मध्य / नीति व श्रृंगार काल - 1650–1850 AD):** *Rajiya ra Sortha (राजिया रा सोरठा)* by **Kripa Ram Khidiya (कृपाराम खिड़िया)** (ethical couplets addressed to his loyal servant Rajiya).
4. **Modern Period (आधुनिक काल - 1850–Present):** **Kaviraj Bankidas (कविराजा बांकीदास)** (*Aayo Angrez Mulk Re Upar / आयो अंग्रेज मुल्क रे ऊपर*) and **Suryamal Misan (सूर्यमल्ल मीसण)** (*Vansh Bhaskar / वंश भास्कर, Veer Satsai / वीर सतसई*).

<div class="term-box">
  <div class="term-title">📖 Terminology Decoder: Literary Forms & Genres (साहित्यिक विधाएं)</div>
  <ul>
    <li><strong>Khyat (ख्यात):</strong> Official historical chronicles of dynasties (*Nainsi ri Khyat / नैणसी री ख्यात, Dayaldas ri Khyat / दयालदास री ख्यात*).</li>
    <li><strong>Vachnika (वचनिका):</strong> Rhyming rhythmic prose-poetry (*Achaldas Khinchi ri Vachnika / अचलदास खींची री वचनिका* by Shivdas Gaadan / शिवदास गाडण).</li>
    <li><strong>Dawavait (दवावैत):</strong> Prose style incorporating Urdu and Persian loan words.</li>
    <li><strong>Vaat (वात):</strong> Traditional short story / folktale (*Pabuji ri Vaat / पाबूजी री वात*).</li>
    <li><strong>Vigat (विगत):</strong> Statistical gazetteer (*Marwar Ra Pargana Ri Vigat / मारवाड़ रा परगना री विगत* by Munhta Nainsi — **"Gazetteer of Rajasthan / राजस्थान का गजेटियर"**).</li>
    <li><strong>Raso (रासो):</strong> Heroic epics (*Prithviraj Raso / पृथ्वीराज रासो* by Chand Bardai, *Bisaldeo Raso / बीसलदेव रासो* by Narpati Nalha).</li>
  </ul>
</div>

---

## 4. Renowned Modern Authors (आधुनिक साहित्यकार)

* **Vijaydan Detha ('Bijji') (विजयदान देथा 'बिज्जी' - 1926–2013):** Born in Borunda (बोरून्दा - Jodhpur); co-founded **Rupayan Sansthan (रूपायन संस्थान)**; authored 14-volume Rajasthani folk tale epic **Baataan Ri Phulwari (बातां री फुलवारी)**; short story *Duvidha (दुविधा)* adapted into films *Duvidha* & *Paheli*; Padma Shri (2007) and Rajasthan Ratna (2012).
* **Kanhaiyalal Sethia (कन्हैयालाल सेठिया - 1919–2008):** Born in Sujangarh (सुजानगढ़ - Churu); authored patriotic anthems **"Dharti Dhoran Ri" (धरती धोरां री)** and **"Pathal aur Peethal" (पाथल और पीथल)**; Sahitya Akademi Award for **Leeltans (लीलटांस - 1976)**; Moortidevi Award for *Nirgranth (निर्ग्रन्थ)*; Padma Shri (2004).
* **Lakshmi Kumari Chundawat (लक्ष्मीकुमारी चूण्डावत):** Authored *Mumal (मूमल), Manjhal Raat (मांझल रात), Gir Uncha Uncha Garhan (गीर ऊंचा ऊंचा गढ़ां)*.
* **Major Journals (प्रमुख पत्रिकाएं):** **Jagti Jot (जागती जोत)** (*Rajasthani Bhasha Academy, Bikaner / राजस्थानी भाषा, साहित्य एवं संस्कृति अकादमी, बीकानेर*) and **Madhumati (मधुमती)** (*Rajasthan Sahitya Akademi, Udaipur / राजस्थान साहित्य अकादमी, उदयपुर*).

---

## 📝 Chapter 9: High-Yield Practice Questions (अभ्यास प्रश्न) — Expected RPSC Pattern

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Classical Rajasthani Literature & Epics</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The celebrated poetic treatise <strong>"Veli Krishan Rukmani Ri" (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)</strong> was authored in the Dingal style by which royal poet, described by contemporary bard Durasa Adha as the "5th Veda and 19th Purana" (5वां वेद और 19वां पुराण)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (पीथल)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Kaviraja Shyamaldas (कविराजा श्यामलदास)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Chandbardai (चंदबरदाई)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Suryamalla Misran (सूर्यमल मीसण)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (पीथल)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Prithviraj Rathore (younger brother of Raja Rai Singh of Bikaner) composed <em>Veli Krishan Rukmani Ri</em> while stationed at Gagron Fort under Akbar. Italian linguist Dr. L.P. Tessitori called him the "Hero of Dingal" (डिंगल का हेरोस). In Kanhaiyalal Sethia's poem, he is immortalized as 'Peethal' writing to Maharana Pratap ('Paathal').
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 State Poet & Modern Literature</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which of the following magnum opus works of Rajasthan's State Poet (राज्यकवि) <strong>Suryamalla Misran</strong> provides the detailed historical genealogy of the Hada Chauhans of Bundi and was completed after his demise by his adopted son Muraridhan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Vansh Bhaskar (वंश भास्कर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Chhandomayukh (छंदोमयूख)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Balwant Vilas (बलवंत विलास)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Vansh Bhaskar (वंश भास्कर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> <em>Vansh Bhaskar</em> is an epic Pingal-Dingal verse chronicle commissioned by Maharao Ramsingh of Bundi. Suryamalla Misran also composed <em>Veer Satsai</em> during the 1857 revolt to awaken Rajput valour against British colonial rule ("इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिये हुलराय...").
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">

    Match List-I (Modern Rajasthani Writer) with List-II (Iconic Literary Creation):
    <br><strong>List-I (Author)</strong>
    <br>(A) Kanhaiyalal Sethia (कन्हैयालाल सेठिया)
    <br>(B) Vijaydan Detha 'Bijji' (विजयदान देथा)
    <br>(C) Meghraj Mukul (मेघराज मुकुल)
    <br>(D) Chandra Singh Birwali (चंद्रसिंह बिरकाली)
    <br><strong>List-II (Creation)</strong>
    <br>(i) Bataan ri Phulwari (बातां री फुलवारी, 14 खण्ड)
    <br>(ii) Sainani (सेनाणी — "चूंडावत मांगी सैनाणी, सिर काट दे दियो क्षत्राणी")
    <br>(iii) Patal aur Peethal & Leeltans (पातल और पीथल, लीलटांस)
    <br>(iv) Badli & Loo (बादली, लू)
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> (A)-(i), (B)-(ii), (C)-(iii), (D)-(iv)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A)-(ii), (B)-(i), (C)-(iv), (D)-(iii)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> (A)-(iii), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iv)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> (A)-(iii), (B)-(iv), (C)-(i), (D)-(ii)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) (A)-(iii), (B)-(i), (C)-(ii), (D)-(iv)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong>
        <br>• Kanhaiyalal Sethia: Born in Sujangarh (Churu); won Sahitya Akademi for <em>Leeltans</em> (1976), immortalized <em>Dharti Dhoran Ri</em> and <em>Patal aur Peethal</em>.
        <br>• Vijaydan Detha 'Bijji': Born in Borunda (Jodhpur); compiled 14 volumes of folklore in <em>Bataan ri Phulwari</em>; his story <em>Duvidha</em> became iconic films.
        <br>• Meghraj Mukul: Wrote <em>Sainani</em> describing Hadi Rani Sahal Kanwar's head sacrifice for Rao Ratan Singh Chundawat.
        <br>• Chandra Singh Birwali: Wrote <em>Badli</em> (first modern Rajasthani nature poem) and <em>Loo</em>.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Practice Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Linguistic Geography & Dialects</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In 1912, which British linguist first coined the term <strong>"Rajasthani"</strong> for the languages spoken in Rajputana in his monumental work <em>"Linguistic Survey of India" (Vol IX, Part II)</em>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Col. James Tod</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Dr. Suniti Kumar Chatterji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Dr. L.P. Tessitori</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Sir George Abraham Grierson</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Sir George Abraham Grierson</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Sir George Abraham Grierson first formally used the term "Rajasthani" in 1912 in <em>Linguistic Survey of India</em>, classifying Rajasthani dialects into 5 major branches: Western Rajasthani (Marwari), North-Eastern (Mewati/Ahirwati), Middle-Eastern (Dhundhari/Hadoti), South-Eastern (Malvi), and Southern Rajasthani (Nimadi).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Linguistic Pioneers — Dr. L.P. Tessitori & Dingal — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The Italian philologist and Indologist <strong>Dr. Luigi Pio Tessitori (लुइगी पिओ तेस्सीतोरी)</strong>, who conducted extensive research on Rajasthani Bardic literature in Bikaner and termed Prithviraj Rathore the <em>'Dingal ro Heros'</em>, has his memorial cenotaph (Chhatri) located in which city?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Jodhpur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Jaipur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Udaipur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Bikaner (बीकानेर - Demised 1919 AD)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Bikaner (बीकानेर - Demised 1919 AD)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Dr. L.P. Tessitori (from Udine, Italy) came to Rajasthan under the Asiatic Society of Bengal. Commissioned by Maharaja Ganga Singh of Bikaner, he catalogued ancient Dingal manuscripts and identified the pre-Harappan antiquities at Kalibangan. He passed away at the young age of 31 in **Bikaner (1919)**, where his commemorative marble cenotaph stands.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Jodhpur?</strong> Jodhpur was the center of Munshi Devi Prasad and Pandit Ramkaran Asopa.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Jaipur?</strong> Jaipur was the seat of Purohit Harinarayan Sharma.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Udaipur?</strong> Udaipur was the research hub of Col. James Tod and Kaviraja Shyamaldas.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> L.P. Tessitori memorial = Bikaner; Called Prithviraj Rathore 'Dingal ro Heros'; First identified Kalibangan pre-Harappan antiquity.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Shivdas Gadan (शिवदास गाडण)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Vithu Suja (वीठू सूजा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Durasa Adha (दुरासा आढ़ा)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Padmanabha** authored *Kanhadade Prabandha* in 1455 AD under the patronage of Rawal Akhairaj Songara of Jalore. Written in Old Western Rajasthani, it vividly chronicles King Kanhadade's heroic defense, Prince Viramdev's sacrifice, and the 1311 Saka of Jalore Fort.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Shivdas Gadan?</strong> Shivdas Gadan authored *Achaldas Khinchi ri Vachanika* (1423 AD Gagron Saka).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Vithu Suja?</strong> Vithu Suja authored *Rao Jaitsi ro Chhand* (1534 AD).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Durasa Adha?</strong> Durasa Adha authored *Kirtar Bavani* and *Virud Chhihattari*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kanhadade Prabandha = Padmanabha (1455 AD); Achaldas Khinchi ri Vachanika = Shivdas Gadan; Rao Jaitsi ro Chhand = Vithu Suja.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Patal aur Peethal (Kanhaiyalal Sethia) — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In the celebrated patriotic poem <strong>'Patal aur Peethal' (पाथल और पीथल)</strong> composed by Padma Shri <strong>Kanhaiyalal Sethia of Sujangarh</strong>, who do <strong>'Patal'</strong> and <strong>'Peethal'</strong> respectively represent?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Patal represents Maharana Pratap and Peethal represents Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (डिंगल का हेरोस)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Patal represents Rana Sanga and Peethal represents Babur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Patal represents Rao Jodha and Peethal represents Rao Maldeo</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Patal represents Prithviraj Chauhan and Peethal represents Chandbardai</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Patal represents Maharana Pratap and Peethal represents Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner (डिंगल का हेरोस)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> In Kanhaiyalal Sethia's immortal poem, **'Patal' (पाथल)** is **Maharana Pratap** and **'Peethal' (पीथल)** is courtier-poet **Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner**. When a rumored letter of submission from Pratap reaches Akbar's court, Peethal sends inspiring Dingal couplets to Pratap to verify if the lion of Mewar will truly bow down, prompting Pratap's defiant vow to fight to the end.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> Rana Sanga and Babur fought the Battle of Khanwa in 1527.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Rao Jodha founded Jodhpur; Rao Maldeo fought Giri-Sumel.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Prithviraj Chauhan and Chandbardai are the subjects of *Prithviraj Raso*.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Patal = Maharana Pratap; Peethal = Prithviraj Rathore; Author = Kanhaiyalal Sethia (who also wrote 'Dharti Dhoran Ri' and 'Leeltans').
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Vansh Bhaskar & Suryamal Misran — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The monumental historical epic <strong>'Vansh Bhaskar' (वंश भास्कर)</strong>, chronicling the royal history of the Hada Chauhans of Bundi and written in Pingal style by state poet <strong>Suryamal Misran (सूर्यमल्ल मीसण)</strong>, was completed after his demise by whom?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Kaviraja Shyamaldas</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> His foster son Muraridan (दत्तक पुत्र मुरारीदान)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Chandbardai's son Jalhan</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Vijaydan Detha</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) His foster son Muraridan (दत्तक पुत्र मुरारीदान)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **'Vansh Bhaskar'** was commissioned by Maharao Raja Ram Singh of Bundi to state poet **Suryamal Misran** (1815–1868 AD). When Suryamal passed away leaving the vast epic incomplete, it was completed and published by his adopted/foster son **Muraridan (मुरारीदान)**. Suryamal also authored the fiery 1857 patriotic work **'Veer Satsai'** (*'इला न देणी आपणी, हालरियै हुलराय'*).<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Shyamaldas?</strong> Kaviraja Shyamaldas of Bhilwara authored *Vir Vinod* under Maharana Sajjan Singh.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Jalhan?</strong> Jalhan was the son of Chandbardai who completed *Prithviraj Raso*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Vijaydan Detha?</strong> Vijaydan Detha ('Bijji') authored *Bataan ri Phulwari* in 14 volumes.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Vansh Bhaskar completed by = Muraridan; Prithviraj Raso completed by = Jalhan; Vir Vinod author = Shyamaldas.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Literature — Veli Krishan Rukmani ri & 5th Veda — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & RAS Pre</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The monumental Dingal romantic-devotional masterpiece <strong>'Veli Krishan Rukmani ri' (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)</strong>, composed by <strong>Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner</strong> at Gagron Fort, was hailed by contemporary poet Durasa Adha as which of the following?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> The 5th Veda and the 19th Purana (पांचवां वेद और 19वां पुराण)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> The Bible of Rajasthan</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> The Mahabharata of the Desert</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> The First Novel of Hindi Literature</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) The 5th Veda and the 19th Purana (पांचवां वेद और 19वां पुराण)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Charan poet **Durasa Adha (दुरसा आढ़ा)** praised *'Veli Krishan Rukmani ri'* as the **'5th Veda and 19th Purana'** for its peerless poetic brilliance. Italian philologist Dr. L.P. Tessitori called Prithviraj Rathore the **'Dingal ro Heros' (डिंगल का हेरोस)**, and Col. James Tod remarked that its verses possessed the vigor of 10,000 horses.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B)?</strong> *Bible of Rajasthan* is not a formal literary title.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> *Vansh Bhaskar* of Suryamal Misran is often compared to historical epics.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> The first novel in Rajasthani is *'Kanak Sundar'* (1903) by Shivchandra Bhartiya.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> 5th Veda & 19th Purana = Veli Krishan Rukmani ri (Durasa Adha's title); Dingal ro Heros = Prithviraj Rathore (Tessitori's title).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Rajasthani Literature — Mahakavi Suryamal Misran — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & College Lecturer</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Mahakavi <strong>Suryamal Misran of Bundi (1815–1868 AD)</strong> composed which fiery patriotic Dingal verse collection during the <strong>1857 Revolt</strong> to awaken Rajput rulers against British colonial subjugation, opening with <em>'इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिया हुलराय...'</em>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Vansh Bhaskar (वंश भास्कर - Bundi chronicle)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई - 1857 patriotic verses)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Chetavni ra Chungtiya (चेतावनि रा चूंघट्या - 13 Sorathas)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Patal aur Peethal (पाथल और पीथल)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई - 1857 patriotic verses)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Suryamal Misran (the state poet of Maharao Ramsingh of Bundi) authored the legendary **Veer Satsai (वीर सतसई)** during the 1857 uprising. His opening couplet: <em>'इला न देणी आपणी, हालरिया हुलराय। पूत सिखावै पालणै, मरण बड़ाई माय॥'</em> (Do not surrender your motherland even for a moment; mothers teach their infants supreme sacrifice in the cradle itself) ignited heroic anti-colonial resistance across Rajasthan.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Vansh Bhaskar?</strong> *Vansh Bhaskar* is an epic verse chronicle detailing the genealogy and history of the Hada Chauhans of Bundi, completed by Suryamal's adopted son Muraridan.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Chetavni ra Chungtiya?</strong> *Chetavni ra Chungtiya* comprises 13 Dingal couplets composed in 1903 by revolutionary poet **Kesari Singh Barhath** to prevent Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar from attending Lord Curzon's Delhi Durbar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Patal aur Peethal?</strong> *Patal aur Peethal* is a modern classic Rajasthani poem composed by Padma Shri **Kanhaiyalal Sethia** describing the dialogue between Maharana Pratap (Patal) and Prithviraj Rathore (Peethal).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Veer Satsai (1857) = Suryamal Misran; Chetavni ra Chungtiya (1903) = Kesari Singh Barhath; Patal aur Peethal = Kanhaiyalal Sethia; Bataan ri Phulwari = Vijaydan Detha.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Rajasthani Dialects & Geography — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC EO/RO & 2nd Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Match the major regional dialects of Rajasthan with their respective geographic speaking regions:<br><br><strong>Which of the following pairs is INCORRECTLY matched?</strong>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Dhundhari Dialect (ढूंढाड़ी बोली) — Jaipur, Dausa, Tonk (Sant Dadu's literature)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Hadoti Dialect (हाड़ौती बोली) — Kota, Bundi, Baran, Jhalawar (Suryamal Misran)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Mewati Dialect (मेवाती बोली) — Alwar, Bharatpur (Sant Laldas & Charan Das)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Vagadi Dialect (वागड़ी बोली) — Sikar, Jhunjhunu, Churu (Shekhawati tract)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Vagadi Dialect (वागड़ी बोली) — Sikar, Jhunjhunu, Churu (Shekhawati tract)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> <strong>Vagadi (वागड़ी)</strong> is spoken in the southern **Vagad region (Dungarpur and Banswara)**; Sir George Grierson called it 'Bhili' due to strong Gujarati and tribal influence. The dialect spoken in Sikar, Jhunjhunu, and Churu is **Shekhawati (शेखावाटी)**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Dhundhari?</strong> Dhundhari is spoken across Jaipur, Dausa, and Tonk; Sant Dadu Dayal composed his spiritual discourses (*Vani*) in Dhundhari.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Hadoti?</strong> Hadoti is the standard dialect of the southeastern Hadoti plateau (Kota, Bundi, Baran, Jhalawar), used in Mahakavi Suryamal Misran's poetry.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Mewati?</strong> Mewati is spoken in the northeastern border region of Alwar and Bharatpur; Sant Lal Das, Sant Charan Das, Sahjo Bai, and Daya Bai authored their works in Mewati.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Vagadi = Dungarpur & Banswara (Sant Mavji's Chopada); Shekhawati = Sikar & Jhunjhunu; Ahirwati / Rathi = Kotputli, Behror & Alwar.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


---

## 🎯 Chapter 9: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2016, 2021 / College Lecturer 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Who was the first linguist to undertake a systematic linguistic classification of Rajasthani dialects in his 1912 work *Linguistic Survey of India*?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Dr. L.P. Tessitori</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Dr. Suniti Kumar Chatterji</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Sir George Abraham Grierson</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Col. James Tod</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Sir George Abraham Grierson</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Grierson classified Rajasthani into 5 sub-groups: Western Rajasthani (Marwari), North-Eastern (Mewati/Ahirwati), Middle-Eastern (Dhundhari/Harauti), Southern (Nimadi), and South-Western (Malvi).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q2</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 2nd Grade 2022 / SI 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    'Dingal' (डिंगल) is primarily the literary language of which region of Rajasthan?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Southern Rajasthan (Malvi base)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Western Rajasthan (Marwari base)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Northern Rajasthan (Punjabi base)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Eastern Rajasthan (Braj base)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Western Rajasthan (Marwari base)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Dingal is the heroic poetic style of Charan and Bhat bards in Western Rajasthan. Pingal is the Eastern style influenced by Braj Bhasha.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q3</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2018 / 1st Grade 2020</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which classic Dingal work composed by Prithviraj Rathore of Bikaner was hailed by Durasa Adha as the "Fifth Veda and Nineteenth Purana" (पांचवां वेद व 19वां पुराण)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> *Dhola Maru Ra Duha*</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> *Rao Jaitsee Ro Chhand*</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> *Kanhadade Prabandh*</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> *Veli Krisan Rukmani Ri* (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) *Veli Krisan Rukmani Ri* (वेलि क्रिसन रुकमणी री)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Written during Akbar's court in 1580 AD. It contains 305 verses celebrating the divine wedding of Krishna and Rukmini. Italian scholar L.P. Tessitori called Prithviraj Rathore the <em>"Horace of Dingal"</em>.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q4</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC SI 2021 / RAS Pre 2021</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The monumental 14-volume Rajasthani folk lore compendium *Baataan Ri Phulwari* (बातां री फुलवारी) was authored by:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Vijaydan Detha ("Bijji")</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Sitaram Lalas</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Suryamal Misran</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Kanhaiyalal Sethia</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Vijaydan Detha ("Bijji")</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Borunda-based Padma Shri author Vijaydan Detha documented Rajasthan's rich oral lore. His stories inspired Bollywood films like *Paheli* and *Duvidha*.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q5</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC College Lecturer 2021 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    *Kanhadade Prabandh* (कान्हड़दे प्रबन्ध) — which documents the heroic resistance of Songara Chauhans against Alauddin Khilji in 1311 AD — was composed by:
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Jayanak</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Chand Bardai</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Padmanabha (पद्मनाभ)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Hemchandra</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Padmanabha (पद्मनाभ)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Written in 1455 AD in Old Western Rajasthani (Maru-Gurjar), providing vivid accounts of the Siege of Jalore and Siwana.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q6</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC Patwari 2021 / 2nd Grade 2019</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Who was the author of the monumental epic *Prithviraj Raso* (पृथ्वीराज रासो)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Jayanak</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Sarangdhar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Narapati Nalha</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Chand Bardai (चंदबरदाई)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Chand Bardai (चंदबरदाई)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Chand Bardai, court poet and close friend of Prithviraj Chauhan III, composed *Prithviraj Raso* in Pingal. (Note: *Prithviraj Vijaya* was authored in Sanskrit by Jayanak!).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

# Chapter 10: Prominent Tourist Destinations & Heritage Circuits (प्रमुख पर्यटन स्थल एवं परिपथ)

* **Tourism Milestones:** Department established in **1956**; declared an **Industry in 1989** (Mohammad Yunus Committee / मोहम्मद युनुस समिति); divided into **10 Tourist Circuits (10 पर्यटन परिपथ)**.

## Key District Landmarks & Circuits

### 1. Ajmer & Pushkar (अजमेर एवं पुष्कर)
* **Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra (अढ़ाई दिन का झोंपड़ा):** Built by **Vigrahraj IV (विग्रहराज चतुर्थ)** as a Sanskrit college (*Harikeli* drama inscribed); converted to a mosque in 1199 AD by Qutbuddin Aibak (कुतुबुद्दीन ऐबक).
* **Dargah of Khwaja Saheb (ख्वाजा साहब की दरगाह):** *Nizam Gate* (Hyderabad), *Shahjahani Gate*, *Buland Gate* (Mahmud Khilji); **Badi Deg (बड़ी देग)** (4,800 kg - Akbar) & **Chhoti Deg (छोटी देग)** (2,240 kg - Jahangir).
* **Anasagar Lake (आनासागर झील):** Built by Arnoraj (अर्णोराज); **Baradaris (सफेद संगमरमर की बारादरी)** by Shah Jahan; **Daulat Bagh / Subhash Udhyan (दौलत बाग)** by Jahangir (where Nur Jahan's mother Asmat Begum / असमत बेगम invented rose perfume / गुलाब का इत्र).
* **Brahma Temple (ब्रह्मा मन्दिर - Pushkar):** Four-faced marble statue; Sun God idol wearing boots (घुटनों तक जूते पहने सूर्य प्रतिमा).

---

### 2. Alwar, Banswara & Bharatpur (अलवर, बांसवाड़ा एवं भरतपुर)
* **Alwar:** Sariska Tiger Reserve (सरिस्का - 1978–79); Bhangarh Fort (भानगढ़ दुर्ग - haunted city by Madho Singh); Moosi Maharani 80-pillar chhatri (मूसी महारानी की छतरी); Siliserh Lake Palace (सिलीसेढ़ - Vinay Singh 1845).
* **Banswara (City of 100 Islands / सौ द्वीपों का शहर):** Mahi Dam (माही बजाज सागर बांध - 16 gates, 3.1 km); Tripura Sundari / Tartai Mata (त्रिपुरा सुन्दरी / तरताई माता - 18-armed black stone goddess).
* **Mangarh Dham (मानगढ़ धाम - Banswara):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Jallianwala Bagh of Rajasthan (राजस्थान का जलियांवाला बाग)</span>
  * On **17 November 1913 (Margashirsha Purnima / मार्गशीर्ष पूर्णिमा)**, British troops fired upon peaceful Bhil tribals assembled under **Guru Govind Giri (गुरु गोविन्द गिरि)**, massacring over **1,500 Bhils**.
* **Bharatpur:** Keoladeo Ghana National Park (केवलादेव घना राष्ट्रीय उद्यान - UNESCO 1985; winter home of **Siberian Cranes / साइबेरियन सारस**); Bandh Baretha Dam (बांध बारैठा).

---

### 3. Jaipur (UNESCO World Heritage City 2019 / विश्व धरोहर नगर)
* **Hawa Mahal (हवा महल - 1799 AD):** Built by **Sawai Pratap Singh (सवाई प्रताप सिंह)** (Architect: **Lal Chand Ustad / लालचंद उस्ताद**); 5 stories (*Sharad, Ratan, Vichitra, Prakash, Hawa Mandir / शरद, रतन, विचित्र, प्रकाश, हवा मन्दिर*); 953 jharokhas (खिड़कियां).
* **Jantar Mantar (जंतर मंतर):** Astronomical observatory built by **Sawai Jai Singh II (सवाई जयसिंह द्वितीय)** (1734 AD); UNESCO Site (2010); world’s largest stone sundial (**Samrat Yantra / सम्राट यंत्र**).
* **Jaigarh Fort (जयगढ़ दुर्ग):** Houses **Jaivana Cannon (जयबाण तोप)** (Asia's largest wheeled cannon).
* **Nahargarh Fort (नाहरगढ़ / सुदर्शनगढ़):** Built by Sawai Jai Singh; Sawai Madho Singh II built **9 identical luxury palaces for his 9 queens (एक जैसे 9 महल)**.
* **Albert Hall Museum (अल्बर्ट हॉल म्यूजियम - 1876):** Indo-Saracenic design by Sir Swinton Jacob.
* **Isarlat / Sargasuli (ईसरलाट / सरगासूली - 1749):** 7-story tower by Maharaja Ishwari Singh after Battle of Rajmahal.

---

### 4. Other Prominent Heritage Landmarks
* **Jhalawar (झालावाड़):** **Bhawani Natyashala (भवानी नाट्यशाला - 1921 AD)** — Rare authentic Parsi-style opera theater with underground hydraulic stage; Sun Temple of Jhalrapatan (*City of Bells / घंटियों का शहर*).
* **Jaisalmer (जैसलमेर):** Patwon ki Haveli (पटवों की हवेली), Salim Singh ki Haveli (सलीम सिंह की हवेली), Nathmal ki Haveli (नथमल की हवेली); **Tanot Mata Temple (तनोट माता मन्दिर)** (Goddess of Soldiers / थार की वैष्णो देवी where 1965 bombs failed to explode).
* **Sri Ganganagar (श्रीगंगानगर):** **Buddha Johad Gurudwara (गुरुद्वारा बुड्ढा जोहड़)** — Where Sikh warriors **Sukha Singh & Mehtab Singh (सूखा सिंह व मेहताब सिंह)** brought the severed head of Massa Ranghar in 1740 to avenge Harmandir Sahib's desecration.

---

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The world-famous <strong>Hawa Mahal of Jaipur</strong> was constructed in 1799 CE by Sawai Pratap Singh. How many storeys does it have, and who was its chief architect?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> 7 Storeys; Vidyadhar Bhattacharya</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> 5 Storeys; Lal Chand Ustad</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> 5 Storeys; Mandan</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> 9 Storeys; Jaita and Napa</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) 5 Storeys; Lal Chand Ustad</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Hawa Mahal is a 5-storey pyramidical crown structure designed by architect Lal Chand Ustad with 953 jharokhas (casements). Its 5 storeys from base to top are: 1. Sharad Mandir, 2. Ratan Mandir, 3. Vichitra Mandir, 4. Prakash Mandir, and 5. Hawa Mandir.

    </div>
  </details>

Jantar Mantar was inscribed earlier in 2010, and Keoladeo Ghana in 1985 (Natural category).

### 🌟 Comprehensive Topic-Wise Master Concept Questions (प्रत्येक विषय के गहन अभ्यास प्रश्न)


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Tourism Policy — Industry Status & Yunus Committee 1989 — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    On <strong>4 March 1989</strong>, Rajasthan became the <strong>very first state in India</strong> to grant the formal status of <strong>'Industry' (उद्योग का दर्जा)</strong> to the Tourism sector based on the recommendations of which expert committee?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Kothari Committee</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> G.V.K. Rao Committee</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Shivaraman Committee</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Based on the visionary recommendations of the **Mohammad Yunus Committee (1989)**, the Government of Rajasthan accorded **Industry status to Tourism on 4 March 1989**, entitling heritage hotels and tourism enterprises to industrial power tariffs, tax concessions, and subsidized loans.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Kothari Committee?</strong> Kothari Commission was for National Education Reform (1964–66).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) G.V.K. Rao?</strong> G.V.K. Rao Committee was for Panchayati Raj (1985).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Shivaraman?</strong> Shivaraman Committee recommended the establishment of NABARD (1981).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Tourism as Industry (4 March 1989) = Mohammad Yunus Committee; RTDC started = 1 April 1979; Palace on Wheels = 26 Jan 1982.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 UNESCO World Heritage — Chronology in Rajasthan — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Match the prestigious <strong>UNESCO World Heritage Inscriptions of Rajasthan</strong> with their exact years of designation:<br><br><strong>Which of the following pairings is INCORRECT?</strong>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Keoladeo Ghana National Park (Bharatpur) — Inscribed in 1985 (World Natural Heritage)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Jantar Mantar Astronomical Observatory (Jaipur) — Inscribed in 2010</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Jaipur Walled City (परकोटा शहर) — Inscribed in 1999</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> 6 Hill Forts of Rajasthan (Chittor, Kumbhalgarh, Gagron, Jaisalmer, Ranthambore, Amer) — Inscribed in 2013</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Jaipur Walled City (परकोटा शहर) — Inscribed in 1999</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Jaipur Walled City (परकोटा क्षेत्र)** was officially inscribed as a UNESCO World Heritage City on **6 July 2019** at the 43rd World Heritage Committee session in Baku (Azerbaijan), NOT 1999.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Keoladeo?</strong> Keoladeo Ghana National Park was inscribed in **1985** as a UNESCO World Natural Heritage site.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Jantar Mantar?</strong> Sawai Jai Singh's stone observatory Jantar Mantar was inscribed in **2010** (along with Kalbelia dance as Intangible Heritage).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) 6 Hill Forts?</strong> The 6 Hill Forts were inscribed in **2013** at Phnom Penh, Cambodia.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> UNESCO Years: Keoladeo = 1985; Jantar Mantar & Kalbelia = 2010; 6 Hill Forts = 2013; Jaipur Walled City = 2019.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Kothari Commission</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
