      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Mohammad Yunus Committee (मोहम्मद यूनुस समिति - 1989)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> On **4 March 1989**, following the recommendations of the **Mohammad Yunus Committee**, Rajasthan became the pioneering state in the country to declare tourism an **Industry (उद्योग)**, opening access to industrial finance, land concessions, and power subsidies for hotels and heritage resorts.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) B.R. Mehta Committee?</strong> The Balwant Rai Mehta Committee (1957) recommended the 3-tier Panchayati Raj system inaugurated in Nagaur on 2 Oct 1959.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Kothari Commission?</strong> The Kothari Commission (1964) was appointed for national educational reforms.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission?</strong> The Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission investigated the murder of freedom fighter **Sagarmal Gopa** inside Jaisalmer jail in 1946.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Tourism as Industry (1989) = Mohammad Yunus Committee; RTDC Inception = 1978/1979; Sagarmal Gopa Inquiry = Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 UNESCO World Heritage — 6 Hill Forts of Rajasthan — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In June 2013 at the 37th World Heritage Committee session in Phnom Penh (Cambodia), <strong>six magnificent hill forts of Rajasthan</strong> were inscribed collectively into UNESCO's World Heritage List.<br><br><strong>Which of the following forts is NOT among those six UNESCO World Heritage Forts?</strong>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Gagron Fort (गागरोन दुर्ग, झालावाड़)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग, जोधपुर)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Kumbhalgarh Fort (कुम्भलगढ़ दुर्ग, राजसमंद)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Sonargarh / Jaisalmer Fort (सोनारगढ़ दुर्ग, जैसलमेर)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Mehrangarh Fort (मेहरानगढ़ दुर्ग, जोधपुर)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Mehrangarh Fort (Jodhpur) and Junagarh Fort (Bikaner) are magnificent forts but are **NOT included in the UNESCO 6 Hill Forts of Rajasthan**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Gagron?</strong> Gagron Fort (Jhalawar) is included as the premier Water/Hill fort (*Jal Durg*).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Kumbhalgarh?</strong> Kumbhalgarh Fort (Rajsamand) is included for its massive 36-km wall and *Katargarh*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Jaisalmer?</strong> Jaisalmer Fort (Sonargarh) is included as the living golden sandstone desert fort on Trikuta hill.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Master Mnemonic for 6 UNESCO Forts (2013): <strong>'चीकू गाजर आम' (Chiku Gajar Aam)</strong> = <strong>चि</strong> (Chittor), <strong>कु</strong> (Kumbhalgarh), <strong>गा</strong> (Gagron), <strong>ज</strong> (Jaisalmer), <strong>र</strong> (Ranthambore), <strong>आम</strong> (Amer). Mehrangarh & Junagarh are NOT on this list!
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Heritage Luxury Trains — Palace on Wheels — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC EO/RO & Assistant Professor</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The iconic royal luxury heritage train <strong>'Palace on Wheels' (पैलेस ऑन व्हील्स)</strong> was launched as a joint venture between the Indian Railways and RTDC on which historic date?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> 15 August 1947</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> 26 January 1982</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> 1 April 1979</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> 1 November 1956</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) 26 January 1982</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> The **Palace on Wheels** was officially launched on Republic Day, **26 January 1982**, using the restored vintage royal saloons of former Rajputana maharajas.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) 15 August 1947?</strong> 15 August 1947 is Indian Independence Day.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) 1 April 1979?</strong> 1 April 1979 marks the commencement of official commercial operations of the **RTDC (Rajasthan Tourism Development Corporation)**.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) 1 November 1956?</strong> 1 November 1956 is **Rajasthan Foundation / Reorganization Day** marking the completion of the 7th and final stage of Rajasthan's integration with the merger of Ajmer-Merwara, Abu-Delwara, and Sunel Tappa.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Palace on Wheels = 26 Jan 1982; RTDC operations = 1 April 1979; Tourism as Industry = 1989 (Yunus Committee); Integration complete = 1 Nov 1956.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

# Chapter 11: Prominent Personalities of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के प्रमुख व्यक्तित्व)

<div class="story-box">
  <div class="story-title">🎙️ Faculty Classroom Opening: The Real Wealth of Rajasthan (राजस्थान के अनमोल रत्न)</div>
  <em>"When you look back at Rajasthan's history, its true wealth was never gold or diamonds—it was the character of its people. Foster mothers who sacrificed their own flesh and blood to protect royal heirs, warriors who fought 30-year desert guerrilla campaigns without asking for a crown, saints who walked thousands of miles for social equality, and soldiers who won India's highest wartime gallantry awards."</em>
</div>

---

## 1. Historical Legends & Unsung Heroes (ऐतिहासिक अमर व्यक्तित्व)

* **Panna Dai (पन्ना धाय):** Gurjar foster mother of Mewar; sacrificed her own infant son **Chandan (चन्दन)** in 1535 AD to shield toddler **Prince Uday Singh (उदयसिंह)** from the murderous sword of usurper Banbir (बनवीर).
* **Gaura Dai (गोरा धाय - 1646–1704 AD):** Revered as the **"Panna Dai of Marwar" (मारवाड़ की पन्ना धाय)**. Smuggled infant heir **Prince Ajit Singh (अजीत सिंह)** out of Aurangzeb's heavily guarded Delhi palace in 1679. Immortalized in Jodhpur's national song **Dhusan (धूंसा गीत)**; 6-pillared chhatri in Jodhpur.
* **Veer Durgadas Rathore (वीर दुर्गादास राठौड़ - 1638–1718 AD):** <span class="pyq-tag">🔥 Core Legend</span>
  * Son of Askaran (आसकरण); born in Salwa (सालवा). Known as the **"Ulysses of Marwar" (मारवाड़ का यूलीसेस / राठौड़ों का यूलीसेस)**.
  * Fought an unyielding 30-year guerrilla resistance against Aurangzeb to crown Ajit Singh. Educated rebel Prince Akbar’s children (**Buland Akhtar & Safiyatunissa / बुलन्द अख्तर व सफियतुलनिसा**) with strict Islamic traditions. Died in exile on the banks of Shipra River in **Ujjain (उज्जैन)** (cenotaph stands there).
* **Gavari Bai (गवरी बाई):** Revered as the **"Meera of Wagad" (वागड़ की मीरा)**; Krishna devotee of Dungarpur; Maharawal Shiv Singh built Balmukund Temple (बालमुकुन्द मन्दिर) for her in 1829.
* **Kaviraj Shyamaldas (कविराजा श्यामलदास - 1836–1893 AD):** Court historian of Mewar (Sajjan Singh); authored **Veer Vinod (वीर विनोद)**; honored with titles **Kaviraja (कविराजा)** and **Kaiser-e-Hind (कैसर-ए-हिन्द)**.
* **Gaurishankar Hirachand Ojha (डॉ. गौरीशंकर हीराचंद ओझा - 1863–1947 AD):** Born in Rohida (रोहिड़ा - Sirohi); pioneer epigraphist; authored **Bharatiya Prachin Lipimala (भारतीय प्राचीन लिपिमाला)**; titled *Mahamahopadhyaya (महामहोपाध्याय)*.
* **Bavji Chatursingh (बावजी चतुरसिंह जी - 1879–1929 AD):** Royal saint-philosopher of Mewar; translated Bhagavad Gita into Mewari (**Gangajali commentary / गंगाजली टीका**).
* **Birbal Singh (बीरबल सिंह):** Bikaner Praja Parishad martyr; died holding the Tricolor on **1 July 1946** at Raisinghnagar (रायसिंहनगर) shouting *"Jhanda Ooncha Rahe Hamara" (झंडा ऊंचा रहे हमारा)*.

---

## 2. Param Vir Chakra Heroes, Social Reformers & Modern Icons (परमवीर चक्र विजेता एवं आधुनिक विभूतियां)

* **Param Vir Chakra Heroes (परमवीर चक्र विजेता):**
  * **CHM Piru Singh (कंपनी हवलदार मेजर पीरू सिंह शेखावत):** Born in Rampura Beri (रामपुरा बेरी - Jhunjhunu); 6th Rajput Rifles; destroyed multiple enemy bunkers at Tithwal on 18 July 1948. **First Param Vir Chakra recipient from Rajasthan (Posthumous / मरणोपरान्त)**.
  * **Major Shaitan Singh (मेजर शैतान सिंह भाटी):** Born in Banasar (बाणासर - Phalodi, Jodhpur); **"Banasur Ka Shaheed" (बाणासुर का शहीद)**; 13th Kumaon (Charlie Company); defended Rezang La / Chushul (रेजांग ला, लद्दाख) in 1962 Sino-Indian War; awarded **Param Vir Chakra (Posthumous)**.
* **Swami Keshwanand (स्वामी केशवानन्द - 1883–1972):** Born in Magloona (मंगलूणा - Sikar); "Pioneer of Desert Education"; established **Gramotthan Vidyapeeth Sangaria (ग्रामोत्थान विद्यापीठ, संगरिया)** and 300+ rural schools and mobile libraries.
* **Pt. Jhabarmal Sharma (पं. झाबरमल्ल शर्मा - 1880–1983):** Jasrapur (जसरापुर - Khetri); revered as the **"Patriarch of Journalism" (पत्रकारिता के भीष्म पितामह)**; wrote histories of Sikar, Khetri, and Swami Vivekananda.
* **Acharya Tulsi (आचार्य तुलसी - 1914–1997):** Born in Ladnun (लाडनूं - Nagaur); 9th Terapanth Acharya; launched the **Anuvrata Movement in 1949 (अणुव्रत आन्दोलन)** (*"इंसान पहले इंसान, फिर हिन्दू या मुसलमान"*).
* **Komal Kothari (कोमल कोठारी - 1929–2004):** Born in Kapasan (कपासन - Chittorgarh); co-founded **Rupayan Sansthan (रूपायन संस्थान - Borunda)** in 1960 to discover and promote Langa/Manganiyar folk musicians globally; Padma Bhushan.
* **Kripal Singh Shekhawat (कृपाल सिंह शेखावत - 1922–2008):** Born in Mau (मऊ - Sikar); "Shilp Guru"; revived **Jaipur Blue Pottery (ब्लू पॉटरी)** with 25 color shades; Padma Shri (1974).
* **Pt. Vishwa Mohan Bhatt (पं. विश्वमोहन भट्ट):** Born in Jaipur; invented the **Mohan Veena (मोहन वीणा)**; won the **Grammy Award (1994)** for *A Meeting by the River*.
* **Karpurchand Kulish (कर्पूरचन्द कुलिश - 1926–2006):** Born in Soda (सोड़ा - Tonk); launched **Rajasthan Patrika (राजस्थान पत्रिका - 1956)**; authored *Mein Dekhta Chala Gaya* and Dhundhari column *Polampol (पोलम्पोल)*.
* **Dr. P. K. Sethi (डॉ. पी. के. सेठी) & Mastercraftsman Ramchandra (शिल्पी रामचन्द्र):** SMS Hospital Jaipur; co-invented the **Jaipur Foot (जयपुर फुट - 1969)** artificial limb; awarded **Ramon Magsaysay Award (रेमन मैग्सेसे पुरस्कार)** and **Padma Shri**.

---

## ⚡ Chapter 11: 1-Minute Quick Cramming Table

| Personality | Master Contribution / Field | Key Honor / Identity |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Panna Dai (पन्ना धाय)** | Sacrificed Chandan to save Udai Singh | Mewar Heroine |
| **Gaura Dai (गोरा धाय)** | Rescued Prince Ajit Singh from Delhi | "Panna Dai of Marwar" / Dhusan Song |
| **Durgadas Rathore (दुर्गादास राठौड़)**| 30-year resistance against Aurangzeb | "Ulysses of Marwar" / Cenotaph in Ujjain |
| **Gavari Bai (गवरी बाई)** | Krishna devotee of Dungarpur | "Meera of Wagad" |
| **Kaviraj Shyamaldas (कविराजा श्यामलदास)**| Authored *Veer Vinod* history of Mewar | Kaiser-e-Hind / Kaviraja |
| **CHM Piru Singh (पीरू सिंह)**| 1948 Tithwal War hero | 1st Param Vir Chakra of Rajasthan |
| **Major Shaitan Singh (शैतान सिंह)**| 1962 Rezang La / Chushul hero | Param Vir Chakra / Banasur Shaheed |
| **Swami Keshwanand (स्वामी केशवानन्द)**| 300+ desert schools / Sangaria | Pioneer of Desert Education |
| **Pt. Jhabarmal Sharma (झाबरमल्ल शर्मा)**| Journalism & Khetri / Vivekananda history | Patriarch of Hindi Journalism |
| **Acharya Tulsi (आचार्य तुलसी)** | Founded Anuvrata Movement (1949) | 9th Terapanth Acharya |
| **Kripal Singh Shekhawat (कृपाल सिंह)**| Revived Blue Pottery (25 shades) | Shilp Guru / Padma Shri |
| **Pt. Vishwa Mohan Bhatt (विश्वमोहन भट्ट)**| Invented Mohan Veena | Grammy Award Winner (1994) |
| **Dr. P. K. Sethi (डॉ. पी. के. सेठी)** | Co-invented Jaipur Foot (1969) | Ramon Magsaysay Award |

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)</div>

His Vardhman Vidyalaya trained revolutionaries like Pratap Singh Barhath and Zorawar Singh Barhath. He lived his last days in Ajmer under the name 'Karim Khan' teaching at Dargah.

She surrendered her family's private Laxminarayan temple to Harijans in 1928, spearheaded Bhoodan movement with Vinoba Bhave, and authored her autobiography <em>Meri Jeevan Yatra</em>.

  </div>

    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Thakur Zorawar Singh Barhath (जोरावर सिंह बारहठ)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Kaviraja Shyamaldas (श्यामलदास)</div>

        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Thakur Kesari Singh Barhath (Shahpura, Bhilwara) sent 13 Dingal couplets (Sorathas) to Saradhana station, reminding Maharana Fateh Singh of his illustrious Guhil ancestors' pride (Bappa Rawal, Rana Sanga, Pratap). The Maharana went to Delhi but refused to attend the Durbar. Kesari Singh also established the <em>Veer Bharat Sabha</em> in 1910.

  </div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Seth Damodar Das Rathi</div>

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa</div>

    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Arjun Lal Sethi** of Jaipur founded the secret revolutionary training center **Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907)**. Accused in the Nimej Mahant Murder Case, he was imprisoned for 6 years in Vellore Jail (Madras). He authored revolutionary plays *Madra Parajaya, Parshva Yajna, and Mahendra Kumar*. In his final years, he taught Persian and lived under the secular name **'Karim Khan'** in Ajmer Dargah, where he was buried as per his last wish upon his demise in 1941.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Kesari Singh Barhath?</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath founded the *Veer Bharat Sabha (1910)* in Kota.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Damodar Das Rathi?</strong> Seth Damodar Das Rathi financed the armed revolution from Beawar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Gopal Singh Kharwa?</strong> Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa planned the 1915 armed uprising in Todgarh.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Vardhman Vidyalaya + 'If Arjunlal takes a job...' + Lived as Karim Khan = Arjun Lal Sethi; Veer Bharat Sabha = Kesari Singh Barhath.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Balmukund Bissa** joined the Marwar Prajamandal and launched civil disobedience against the Maharaja of Jodhpur. Arrested in 1942, he went on a relentless **hunger strike in Jodhpur Central Jail** to protest unhygienic food and police brutality, attaining martyrdom on **19 June 1942**. For this supreme sacrifice through fasting, he is revered as the **'Jatin Das of Rajasthan'**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>

          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Rameshwar Dayal Sharma was an activist in Alwar.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Hunger strike martyr in Jodhpur Jail (1942) = Balmukund Bissa ('Jatin Das of Rajasthan'); Burnt alive in Jaisalmer (1946) = Sagarmal Gopa.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">

    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> She hid the teacher inside a secret mountain cave</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> She went on a 21-day hunger strike outside the royal palace</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> When the Dungarpur state police raided the Prajamandal school at Rastapal and tied teacher **Sengabhai** behind a truck with ropes, **13-year-old Bhil girl Kali Bai** fearlessly dashed into the convoy wielding her grass-cutting sickle (*Daatli*) and severed the rope. The police opened fire, and Kali Bai was martyred on **21 June 1947**. A memorial statue stands in her honor at **Gaib Sagar Lake in Dungarpur**.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>

          <li><strong>Why not (C)?</strong> Underground hiding was practiced by Prajamandal activists.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D)?</strong> Fasting was used by Balmukund Bissa.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Rastapal Incident (19 June 1947) = Kali Bai + Cut rope with sickle to save teacher Sengabhai; Memorial = Gaib Sagar Lake (Dungarpur).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> **Thakur Zorawar Singh Barhath** (brother of Kesari Singh and uncle of Pratap Singh) disguised himself as a woman in a Haveli balcony and hurled the bomb at Viceroy Hardinge's elephant in Delhi (1912). He successfully evaded British intelligence for 27 straight years by adopting the guise of ascetic sadhu **'Amardas Bairagi' (अमरदास बैरागी)** in the ravines of Kota, MP, and Malwa until his peaceful demise in 1939.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Kesari Singh?</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath was imprisoned for 20 years in Hazaribagh Jail for the murder of Mahant Pyare Lal.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Gopal Singh Kharwa?</strong> Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa was interned at Todgarh Fort for planning the 1915 armed uprising.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Pratap Singh?</strong> Pratap Singh Barhath was arrested in the Benaras Conspiracy Case and tortured to death in Bareilly Jail (1918).</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Amardas Bairagi = Zorawar Singh Barhath (Bomb at Hardinge 1912); Bareilly Jail martyr = Pratap Singh Barhath; Chetavni ra Chungtiya = Kesari Singh Barhath.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Women Freedom Movement — Katrathal Conference 1934 — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 1st Grade & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Under whose dynamic leadership was the historic <strong>Katrathal Women's Conference (कटराथल महिला सम्मेलन - 25 April 1934, Sikar)</strong> organized, where over <strong>10,000 peasant women</strong> gathered in defiance of feudal bans against the atrocities of the Thakur of Sihanot?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Anjana Devi Chaudhary (अंजना देवी चौधरी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Kishori Devi (किशोरी देवी - Wife of Sardar Hararlal Singh)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Janakidevi Bajaj (जानकीदेवी बजाज)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Ratna Shastri (रत्ना शास्त्री)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Kishori Devi (किशोरी देवी - Wife of Sardar Hararlal Singh)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> On **25 April 1934**, **Kishori Devi** (wife of Shekhawati leader Sardar Hararlal Singh of Hanumanpura) presided over the mammoth gathering of **10,000 Jat peasant women at Katrathal (Sikar)** to protest the misbehavior and humiliation of women by the feudal Thakur of Sihanot (Maan Singh). Eminent orator **Uttama Devi** (wife of Thakur Deshraj) delivered the keynote speech.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Anjana Devi?</strong> Anjana Devi Chaudhary was the first woman in Rajasthan arrested during the Bijolia and Begun peasant movements.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Janakidevi Bajaj?</strong> Janakidevi Bajaj presided over the Jaipur Prajamandal 1944 session and was awarded Padma Vibhushan (1956).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Ratna Shastri?</strong> Ratna Shastri co-founded *Banasthali Vidyapith* (Shanta Kuteer) alongside Pandit Hiralal Shastri.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> 10,000 Women at Katrathal (1934) = Led by Kishori Devi (Keynote: Uttama Devi); First arrested woman = Anjana Devi Chaudhary; Banasthali Vidyapith = Ratna Shastri.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="C">

  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which pioneer revolutionary established the <strong>Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907) in Jaipur</strong> as a secret training ground for freedom fighters and famously declared: <em>'यदि अर्जुनलाल नौकरी करेगा तो अंग्रेजों को भारत से बाहर कौन निकालेगा?'</em>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Seth Damodar Das Rathi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(C) Arjun Lal Sethi (अर्जुन लाल सेठी)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Padmashri **Arjun Lal Sethi** (born 1880 in Jaipur) was the vanguard of Rajasthan's armed national awakening. He rejected the lucrative post of Prime Minister / Diwan of Jaipur offered by Maharaja Madho Singh II with the iconic statement: <em>'यदि अर्जुनलाल नौकरी करेगा तो अंग्रेजों को भारत से बाहर कौन निकालेगा?'</em>. He authored *'Shudra Mukti'*, *'Stri Mukti'*, and *'Mahendra Kumar'*.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Kesari Singh Barhath?</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath founded the *Veer Bharat Sabha* (1910) and penned *Chetavni ra Chungtiya*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Seth Damodar Das Rathi?</strong> Seth Damodar Das Rathi was the financier / Bhamashah of the revolutionaries in Beawar.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa?</strong> Rao Gopal Singh Kharwa led armed revolt in Ajmer-Merwara alongside Vijay Singh Pathik.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Vardhman Vidyalaya (1907) & 'If Sethi does job...' = Arjun Lal Sethi; Veer Bharat Sabha = Kesari Singh Barhath; Bhamashah of Revolution = Damodar Das Rathi.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="D">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Women Martyrs — Veerangana Kali Bai of Rastapal — Deep Fact Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC 2nd Grade & SI</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    On <strong>19 June 1947</strong> in the princely state of Dungarpur, 13-year-old Bhil girl <strong>Kali Bai (काली बाई)</strong> sacrificed her life during the <strong>Rastapal Incident (रास्तापाल काण्ड)</strong> while rescuing which revered teacher from being dragged behind a police truck?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Nanabhai Khant (नानाभाई खांट)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Manikyalal Verma (माणिक्यलाल वर्मा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Bhogilal Pandya (भोगीलाल पण्ड्या)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Sengabhai (सेंगाभाई - school teacher)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Sengabhai (सेंगाभाई - school teacher)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> During the Rastapal incident (June 1947), when Dungarpur state police tied schoolteacher **Sengabhai** to their vehicle and dragged him on the road for refusing to close the Sewa Sangh school, brave 13-year-old **Kali Bai** rushed forward and cut the rope with her sickle (*Danti*). The police opened fire, and she was martyred on 21 June 1947. A memorial statue stands at Gaib Sagar Lake in Dungarpur.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Nanabhai Khant?</strong> *Nanabhai Khant* was the village school patron who was beaten to death inside the schoolroom by police before Sengabhai was dragged.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Manikyalal Verma?</strong> Manikyalal Verma was the leader of the Mewar Prajamandal and author of the song *Panchhida*.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Bhogilal Pandya?</strong> Bhogilal Pandya ('Gandhi of Vagad') was the founder-president of the Dungarpur Prajamandal.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Kali Bai rescued teacher = Sengabhai (Rastapal, 1947); Teacher killed in schoolroom = Nanabhai Khant; Weapon used by Kali Bai = Sickle (दांती).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>



<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="A">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Freedom Fighters — Kesari Singh Barhath & Chetavni ra Chungtiya — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC RAS Pre & 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    In 1903 AD, revolutionary Charan poet <strong>Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)</strong> sent <strong>13 Dingal couplets (सोरठे)</strong> titled <strong>'Chetavni ra Chungtiya' (चेतावनि रा चूंघट्या)</strong> to which Rajput sovereign, successfully preventing him from attending Lord Curzon's imperial Delhi Durbar?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar (महाराणा फतेह सिंह)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Maharaja Ganga Singh of Bikaner (महाराजा गंगा सिंह)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Maharaja Man Singh II of Jaipur (महाराजा मानसिंह द्वितीय)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Maharana Sajjan Singh of Mewar (महाराणा सज्जन सिंह)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar (महाराणा फतेह सिंह)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath sent 13 inspiring Dingal Sorathas titled *Chetavni ra Chungtiya* to **Maharana Fateh Singh of Mewar** before the 1903 Delhi Durbar. The couplets reminded the Maharana of Bappa Rawal, Rana Sanga, and Maharana Pratap's unblemished dignity (*'पग-पग भम्या पहाड़, धरा छोड़ राख्यो धरम...'*) causing Fateh Singh to reach Delhi but refuse to enter Curzon's imperial court.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (B) Ganga Singh of Bikaner?</strong> Maharaja Ganga Singh attended imperial conferences, signed the Treaty of Versailles (1919), and brought the Gang Canal to Bikaner (1927).</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Man Singh II?</strong> Maharaja Sawai Man Singh II was the last ruling Maharaja of Jaipur and became the first Rajpramukh of Rajasthan upon Greater Rajasthan's formation in 1949.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Sajjan Singh?</strong> Maharana Sajjan Singh ruled Mewar from 1874 to 1884 and hosted Swami Dayanand Saraswati during the writing of *Satyarth Prakash* in Udaipur.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Chetavni ra Chungtiya recipient = Maharana Fateh Singh (1903); Author = Kesari Singh Barhath; Host of Swami Dayanand = Maharana Sajjan Singh.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>


<div class="pyq-card" data-correct="B">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge practice-badge">🎯 Freedom Fighters — Martyrdom of Sagarmal Gopa — Deep Concept Check</span>
    <span class="pyq-year">RPSC College Lecturer & EO/RO</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which brave freedom fighter and author of <strong>'Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj' (जैसलमेर में गुंडाराज)</strong> and <strong>'Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma'</strong> was subjected to inhuman torture and burnt alive inside Jaisalmer Central Jail on <strong>3 April 1946</strong>?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="A"><strong>(A)</strong> Pratap Singh Barhath (प्रताप सिंह बारहठ - Bareilly Jail Martyr)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="B"><strong>(B)</strong> Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा - Jaisalmer Jail Martyr)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="C"><strong>(C)</strong> Zorawar Singh Barhath (जोरावर सिंह बारहठ - Amardas Bairagi)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt" data-opt="D"><strong>(D)</strong> Bhogilal Pandya (भोगीलाल पण्ड्या - "Gandhi of Vagad")</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Detailed 4-Option Analysis</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा - Jaisalmer Jail Martyr)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>🎯 Why this is the Correct Answer:</strong> Sagarmal Gopa of Jaisalmer fearlessly exposed the tyrannical autocracy of Maharawal Jawahar Singh through his revolutionary books *'Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj'*, *'Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma'*, and *'Azadi ke Deewane'*. On 3 April 1946, royal jailer Guman Singh poured kerosene over him and burnt him alive inside Jaisalmer jail. The murder was investigated by the Gopal Swaroop Pathak Commission.<br><br>
        <strong>🔍 Comparative Distractor Breakdown (अन्य विकल्पों का विस्तृत विश्लेषण — Why Not the Others?):</strong>
        <ul>
          <li><strong>Why not (A) Pratap Singh Barhath?</strong> Kunwar Pratap Singh Barhath (son of Kesari Singh) was arrested in the Benaras Conspiracy Case and tortured to death in **Bareilly Central Jail** (1918) by Charles Cleaveland, refusing to betray his comrades: <em>'I cannot make thousands of mothers weep to stop my single mother weeping.'</em></li>
          <li><strong>Why not (C) Zorawar Singh Barhath?</strong> Zorawar Singh Barhath threw the bomb at Viceroy Lord Hardinge in Delhi Chandni Chowk (1912) and eluded capture for 27 years, living under the ascetic identity of **Amardas Bairagi** in the ravines of Kota-Gwalior.</li>
          <li><strong>Why not (D) Bhogilal Pandya?</strong> Bhogilal Pandya is celebrated as the **'Gandhi of Vagad' (वागड़ के गांधी)** who established the Dungarpur Prajamandal (1944) and Sewa Sangh to uplift tribal communities.</li>
        </ul>
        <br><strong>💡 RPSC Memory Hook / Trap Alert:</strong> Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj & burnt in jail = Sagarmal Gopa (1946); Bareilly Jail martyrdom = Pratap Singh Barhath (1918); Hardinge Bomb & Amardas Bairagi = Zorawar Singh Barhath; Gandhi of Vagad = Bhogilal Pandya.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

## 🎯 Chapter 11: Topic-wise RPSC Previous Years' Questions (PYQs)

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q1</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC RAS Pre 2018, 2021 / EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Who was the revolutionary leader originally named 'Bhup Singh' who led the historic 44-year-long non-violent Bijolia Peasant Movement (1916 onwards)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Vijay Singh Pathik (विजय सिंह पथिक)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Sadhu Sitaram Das</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Manikyalal Verma</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Vijay Singh Pathik (विजय सिंह पथिक)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Born in Gouthavali (Bulandshahr, UP). Joined the Ras Bihari Bose revolutionary network. He established the *Bijolia Kisan Panchayat* (1917) and *Rajasthan Seva Sangh* (Wardha, 1919).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">

    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC 1st Grade 2020 / SI 2021 / EO/RO 2022</span>

  <div class="pyq-question">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Zorawar Singh Barhath</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Arjun Lal Sethi</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Pratap Singh Barhath</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Kesari Singh Barhath (केसरी सिंह बारहठ)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Kesari Singh Barhath stirred the Maharana's patriotic pride through 13 Dingal couplets sent to Sarai Rohilla station, reminding him that the Mewar dynasty had never bowed before foreign emperors.

  </details>

<div class="pyq-card">

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Raghunath Singh</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Balmukund Bissa</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Jwala Prasad</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Sagarmal Gopa (सागरमल गोपा)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Sagarmal Gopa exposed autocratic oppression in *Jaisalmer me Gunda Raj* and *Raghunath Singh ka Mukadma*. He was burnt alive in jail on 4 April 1946.

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Anandraj Surana</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Bhanwarlal Saraf</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Mathuradas Mathur</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Jai Narayan Vyas (जयनारायण व्यास)</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Jai Narayan Vyas (जयनारायण व्यास)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Pioneer of Marwar Prajamandal (1934), editor of *Tarun Rajasthan*, *Peeph* (English), and *Aageevan* (first political newspaper in Rajasthani language from Beawar).
      </div>
    </div>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia (डॉ. राम मनोहर लोहिया)</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Jai Narayan Vyas</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Hiralal Shastri</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia (डॉ. राम मनोहर लोहिया)</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Socialist leader Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia presided over the Rajasthan Aandolan Samiti in 1949, speeding up the integration of Jaipur, Jodhpur, Bikaner, and Jaisalmer into Greater Rajasthan (30 March 1949).
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q6</span>

  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Which British Political Officer put pressure on the ruler of Bundi, Rao Raja Bishan Singh, and successfully helped ban the Sati system in Bundi in 1822 (first state in Rajasthan)?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> C.E. Burton</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> James Tod</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> John Ludlow</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Robinson</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(D) Robinson</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Bundi was the first princely state in Rajasthan to legally outlaw Sati in 1822 under British pressure (Robinson/Tod). Later in 1844, Major John Ludlow influenced Jaipur State to ban Sati and female infanticide.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q7</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022 / 1st Grade</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    The 'Kangada Incident' (कांगड़ा काण्ड - 1946), where armed feudal Jagirdars brutally attacked famine-stricken protesting farmers, took place during which peasant movement?
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> Shekhawati</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> Bikaner</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Marwar</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Begun</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(B) Bikaner</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> In autumn 1946, during severe crop failure in Kangada village (Ratangarh, Bikaner), Jagirdars extracted tyrannical land revenue and tortured peasants, leading to nationwide condemnation.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>

<div class="pyq-card">
  <div class="pyq-header">
    <span class="pyq-qno">Q8</span>
    <span class="pyq-exam-badge">RPSC EO/RO 2022</span>
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-question">
    Consider the following statements about Prajamandal movements: (A) Banswara Prajamandal was established in 1943 due to the efforts of Bhupendranath Trivedi; (B) Dholpur Prajamandal was established under the Chairmanship of Krishna Dutt Paliwal.
  </div>
  <div class="pyq-options">
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(A)</strong> (A) and (B) both are true</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(B)</strong> (A) and (B) both are false</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(C)</strong> Only (B) is true</div>
    <div class="pyq-opt"><strong>(D)</strong> Only (A) is true</div>
  </div>
  <details class="pyq-details">
    <summary class="pyq-toggle-btn">👁️ Click to View Answer & Explanation</summary>
    <div class="pyq-solution">
      <div class="pyq-ans-badge">✅ Correct Answer: <strong>(A) (A) and (B) both are true</strong></div>
      <div class="pyq-explanation">
        <strong>Explanation:</strong> Both statements are 100% accurate facts from Rajasthan Hindi Granth Academy and RBSE textbooks.
      </div>
    </div>
  </details>
</div>
